## I / 一

===Original===
“Well, this is a nice thing! A nice thing this schoolmastering! Up at seven, and in a room with a black fire﻿ ﻿… I should have thought it might have occurred to one out of forty boys to poke it﻿ ﻿… and hard at work, before other men think it time to be awake! And while you are about it, don’t pile on as much coal as it would take the day’s profits of the school to pay for. And here is a thing I have to see every morning of my life! Here is a thing I have to be degraded by, every morning when I come down to an honest day’s work, a middle-aged man working to support his family! I am surprised to see people with such a want of self-respect. I admit that I am. I would rather see a boy come in roundly late, than slip in on the stroke, half dressed and half asleep, and pass as being in time. It is an ungentlemanly thing to do.”

Mr. Merry, a tall, thin man about fifty, leaned back in his chair, and fixed on his pupils his little, pale, screwed up eyes, to which he had the gift of imparting an alluring kindness. His feelings towards them, affection, disgust, pride and despair, seemed to oust each other over his face.

“Yes, I am sick and tired of this sort of thing. I will not mince matters with you. And get to your seats without upsetting everything on your way, will you please? Oh, who would be a schoolmaster? I should not be doing my duty to you all, if I did not warn you all against it. And I suppose it is a good thing to have the east wind from an east window blowing in upon forty people, thirty-nine of them growing boys, before their breakfast on a March morning? And﻿ ﻿… one, two, three, four, five, six, seven﻿ ﻿… it takes eleven boys to shut a window, does it? And I suppose I cannot make a few remarks, without having you all fidgeting and gaping, and behaving like a set of clodhoppers instead of gentlemen? Get to your work at once; and don’t look up again before the gong.”

Mr. Merry gave a gesture of despairing acquiescence, as the boys obeyed this summons without his endorsing it. He followed them down to their basement dining-room.

Mrs. Merry, a smooth-headed, mild-looking woman with a grieved expression, was standing at the head of a long table, pouring out tea. Her four little girls were seated near her. A thin dark lady of forty, matron and teacher of music and French, was cutting bread.

===Chinese===
「哼，这可真是一件妙事！教书这差事真是一件妙事！七点钟就起床，走进一间炉火灭得漆黑的屋子……我本以为四十个孩子里总该有一个想起去捅一捅炉子……别人还没睡醒，我们就得埋头苦干！还有，你们既然要捅，就别往里堆那么多煤，堆掉的煤够把学校一天的利润全搭进去。这就是我一辈子每天早晨都得看到的事！这就是每天早晨我下来做一天正经活计、一个中年人干活养家的时候，都得被它羞辱一回的事！看到这样不知自重的人，我很吃惊。我承认我吃惊。我宁可看到一个孩子大大方方地迟到，也不愿看他掐着钟点溜进来，衣衫不整、睡眼惺忪，还装作按时到了。这可不是绅士的行径。」

梅里先生（Mr. Merry）是个五十岁上下、瘦高个子的男人，他往椅背上一靠，把那双眯起的、苍白的小眼睛盯住他的学生们——他有一种本事，能让这双眼睛流露出诱人的和蔼。他对学生们的种种情感——慈爱、厌恶、骄傲与绝望——仿佛在他脸上轮番挤兑着彼此。

「是的，这种事我真是又厌又倦。我不想跟你们拐弯抹角。都到座位上去，一路别碰翻东西，劳驾了？唉，谁愿意当教书先生呢？我若不劝你们全都别干这一行，就是对你们大家不尽责。还有，我猜三月的一个早晨，还没吃早饭，东风从东窗灌进来，吹着四十个人——其中三十九个是正在长身体的孩子——想必是件好事吧？还有……一、二、三、四、五、六、七……关一扇窗子要用上十一个孩子，是吗？我猜我随口说几句，也免不了看你们一个个坐立不安、张嘴打哈欠，一副庄稼汉做派，全不像绅士的样子吧？立刻去做功课；锣响之前不许再抬头。」

孩子们遵命而行，并未得到他的首肯，梅里先生做了个绝望而又默许的手势。他跟着他们下了楼，进了地下层的饭厅。

梅里太太（Mrs. Merry）头发梳得平平整整，面容温和，带着一种哀伤的神情，正站在长桌上首斟茶。她的四个小女儿坐在她近旁。一位四十岁、肤色黝黑的瘦削女士——她兼做女管家，还教音乐和法语——正在切面包。

===Original===
“Well, Mother,” said Mr. Merry, in the tone of a tender husband and tried man.

“Well, dear,” said Mrs. Merry, without raising her eyes.

“Good morning, Miss Basden,” said Mr. Merry, with the almost exaggerated courtesy due to a lady he employed.

“Good morning, Mr. Merry,” said Miss Basden, in a tone in which equality, respect, and absorption in her duty were rather remarkably mingled.

“Now, look here,” said Mr. Merry, “I have never had such an ungentlemanly set of boys. Now, go out again, all of you, and come in like gentlemen meeting a lady for the first time in the day.”

===Chinese===
「哎，妈妈，」梅里先生说，用的是温柔丈夫兼饱经磨难者的口吻。

「哎，亲爱的，」梅里太太说，眼睛也没抬。

「早上好，巴斯登小姐（Miss Basden），」梅里先生说，带着对自己雇用的女士几乎近于夸张的礼数。

「早上好，梅里先生，」巴斯登小姐说，语气里平等、敬意与专注本职相当奇妙地混在一起。

「好了，都听着，」梅里先生说，「我从没带过这么不知绅士体统的一群孩子。现在，你们全都再出去，重新进来，要像绅士们当天头一回见到一位女士那样进来。」

===Original===
A retirement from the room was succeeded by a chorus of “Good morning, Mrs. Merry.”

“Good morning, boys,” said Mrs. Merry.

“Have you all met Miss Basden already today?” Mr. Merry inquired, looking round frigidly.

“Good morning, Miss Basden.”

“Good morning, boys,” said Miss Basden, in a casual tone, still cutting.

===Chinese===
一阵退场之后，紧跟着是齐声的「早上好，梅里太太」。

「早上好，孩子们，」梅里太太说。

「你们今天都已经见过巴斯登小姐了吧？」梅里先生冷冷地环视着问道。

「早上好，巴斯登小姐。」

「早上好，孩子们，」巴斯登小姐说，语调随意，手上仍切着面包。

===Original===
“Hillman!” said Mr. Merry. “How often am I to say that I will not have sitting down before grace is said? Pray do not show your nature to the rest of us.”

Hillman gained his feet.

“For these and all other mercies may we be given thankful hearts,” said Mr. Merry, his eyes taking a covert general survey.

“Johnson, I am disgusted! I am more. I cannot tell you before ladies what I think of you. I hoped I should never have a boy in my school who would not control himself for a moment to give his attention to sacred things. What was it, pray, that you had to say, of such importance that it could not wait for a second?”

Johnson, who had been observing that it was wise to ask for thankful hearts for such mercies, was silent.

===Chinese===
「希尔曼（Hillman）！」梅里先生说。「我还要说多少遍，谢饭祷告之前不许坐下？请别当着我们大家显露你的本性。」

希尔曼站起身来。

「为了这些以及一切别的恩惠，愿我们获得感恩的心，」梅里先生说，目光暗中做了一次总体巡视。

「约翰逊（Johnson），我真恶心！还不止恶心。当着女士们的面，我没法说我是怎么想你的。我原希望我的学校里永远不要出现一个连片刻也克制不了自己、不能专心于圣事的孩子。请问，你方才有什么话非说不可，要紧得连一秒钟也等不得？」

约翰逊方才一直在发表高见，说为这等恩惠祈求感恩之心实在明智，这时他闭了嘴。

===Original===
“Now,” said Mr. Merry, preparing again to bend his head, “we will say grace again, and I hope nothing so humiliating will occur a second time. I shall not speak of the matter, Johnson.”

Mrs. Merry and Miss Basden bowed their heads a little lower than before, though it was hardly to be thought that their experience had not been suitable.

“I cannot bear to see a sulky boy,” said Mr. Merry, throwing a disgusted glance at Johnson.

“Will you have tea or coffee, dear?” said Mrs. Merry.

“Oh, either, either; anything will do for me.”

===Chinese===
「好了，」梅里先生说，又准备低下头去，「我们重新做祷告，我希望这样丢人的事不会发生第二次。这事我就不多说了，约翰逊。」

梅里太太和巴斯登小姐把头垂得比方才更低了些，虽说很难认为她们以往的经验竟不足以应付此等场面。

「我最看不得绷着脸的孩子，」梅里先生说，朝约翰逊投去厌恶的一瞥。

「你喝茶还是咖啡，亲爱的？」梅里太太说。

「哦，随便，随便；我吃什么都可以。」

===Original===
“I always arrange for you to have your choice, dear.”

“Yes, Mother; yes. You take good care of me. You spend your life taking care of people, I am afraid.” Mr. Merry looked round on the boys with an air of rebuking ingratitude.

“Yes, I have done a good deal of taking care in my time. I think I have done very little else.”

“Well, Mrs. Merry, we must begin to take care of you,” said Miss Basden, putting the toast rack near to Mrs. Merry.

“How did you sleep, Miss Basden?” said Mr. Merry, in an extremely interested manner.

===Chinese===
「我总安排让你挑自己中意的，亲爱的。」

「是啊，妈妈；是啊。你把我照顾得很好。恐怕你是把一辈子都花在照顾人上了。」梅里先生环视孩子们，一副责备他们忘恩负义的神气。

「是啊，我这辈子照顾人照顾得可不少。我想我别的也没做什么。」

「那么，梅里太太，我们可得开始照顾您了，」巴斯登小姐说着，把吐司架挪到梅里太太近旁。

「您夜里睡得好吗，巴斯登小姐？」梅里先生说，语气关切至极。

===Original===
“Mrs. Merry,” Miss Basden said, in a rather high monotone, “the boys are saying that the marmalade is watery. I am telling them that no water is used in marmalade, that marmalade does not contain water; so I do not see how it can be.”

“I do not see how it can be, either; but of course I wish to be told if anything is not as nice as it can be. Let me taste the marmalade.”

Miss Basden offered a spoon from the pot.

“It seems to me that it is very nice. Perhaps I am not a judge of marmalade. I do not care to eat it on bread with butter myself. One or the other is enough for me. But it seems to me to be very nice.”

“Mother, don’t water the boys’ preserves,” said Mr. Merry, nodding his head up and down. “Don’t try to make things go further than they will go, you know. The game isn’t worth the candle.”

===Chinese===
「梅里太太，」巴斯登小姐用一种略高的单调声音说，「孩子们在说橘子酱掺了水。我在告诉他们，橘子酱里不用水，橘子酱不含水分；所以我看不出这怎么可能。」

「我也看不出这怎么可能；不过，要是有什么东西不够好，我当然愿意有人告诉我。让我尝尝橘子酱。」

巴斯登小姐从罐里递过一把小勺。

「我尝着这酱很好。或许我算不上品评橘子酱的行家。我自己不爱把它抹在黄油面包上吃。两样里有一样就够了。不过我尝着这酱确实很好。」

「妈妈，别往孩子们的果酱里兑水，」梅里先生说，脑袋一上一下地点着。「别老想把东西撑出它撑不出的分量，明白吗。这可是得不偿失。」

===Original===
“I do not understand you, dear. There is never any extra water in preserves. They would not keep if they had water in them. There would not be any object in it. It would be less economical, not more.”

“Oh, well, Mother, I don’t know anything about the kitchen business and that. But if the marmalade is not right, let us have it right another time. That is all I mean.”

“I do not think you know what you mean, dear.”

“No, Mother, no; very likely I don’t.”

“The housekeeping is not your province, Mr. Merry,” said Miss Basden. “You will have us coming and telling you how to teach Latin, if you are not careful.”

===Chinese===
「我不明白你的意思，亲爱的。果酱里从来不额外加水。要是进了水，酱就存不住了。那样做毫无意义。只会更费，不会更省。」

「哦，好吧，妈妈，厨房里的事我一窍不通。可要是橘子酱不对劲，下回就弄对了它。我要说的就这么点意思。」

「我看你自己也不知道自己是什么意思，亲爱的。」

「是啊，妈妈，是啊；我很可能真不知道。」

「家务可不是您的分内事，梅里先生，」巴斯登小姐说。「您要是不当心，我们就要来教您怎么教拉丁文了。」

===Original===
“Ah, Miss Basden, ah, you saucy lady! Not such a babel down at that part of the room! Not such a babel. Do you hear me? Be quiet, or go away, and leave the room to civilized people.” Mr. Merry looked fiercely towards the other end of the table, which certainly tended to be the noisier.

“Mr. Burgess is not going to allow us much of his company at breakfast this morning,” said Miss Basden.

“I wonder if Fanny called him,” said Mrs. Merry.

“Yes, she called him,” said Miss Basden.

“Ah! he’s fond of the sheets, is Mr. Burgess. He is fond of the sheets,” said Mr. Merry; while the boys found this talk of the under-master a cause for nudges and smiles.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，巴斯登小姐，哎呀，您这张嘴可不饶人！桌子那头别吵成巴别塔！别那么闹哄哄的。听见没有？安静下来，要么就走开，把屋子让给文明人。」梅里先生凶狠地朝长桌另一头望去，那一头确实总归是更吵闹的一头。

「伯吉斯先生（Mr. Burgess）今早早餐时大概不打算怎么陪我们了，」巴斯登小姐说。

「不知范妮（Fanny）叫过他没有，」梅里太太说。

「叫过了，她叫过他了，」巴斯登小姐说。

「啊！他恋着被窝呢，我们这位伯吉斯先生。他就是恋被窝，」梅里先生说；孩子们则觉得谈论这位副教师的话头正好用来互相捅胳膊、挤眉弄眼。

===Original===
“I never can understand how people can lie in bed in daylight,” said Miss Basden.

“Now it would do you good to do a little more of it, Miss Basden,” said Mr. Merry. “You do too much of the other thing, as I always tell you. It would do you good to do a little more of it.”

“Oh, that sort of advice does not have much effect on me, Mr. Merry,” said Miss Basden, again cutting bread.

“You give poor Miss Basden a great deal of work to do,” said Mr. Merry, looking down the rows of boys with vague disapproval.

“You must make haste, all of you. Mr. Herrick will be here to read prayers in a minute,” said Mrs. Merry.

===Chinese===
「我怎么也弄不懂，人怎么能在天亮以后还赖在床上，」巴斯登小姐说。

「哎，您要是多躺一会儿倒好呢，巴斯登小姐，」梅里先生说。「您另外那样做得太多了，这话我常跟您说。多躺一会儿对您有好处。」

「哦，这类劝告对我起不了多大作用，梅里先生，」巴斯登小姐说着，又切起面包来。

「你们叫可怜的巴斯登小姐干了多少活啊，」梅里先生说，带着含糊的不以为然，扫视着一排排孩子。

「你们都得抓紧些。赫里克先生（Mr. Herrick）马上就来读祈祷文了，」梅里太太说。

===Original===
“Mother, ought not Mr. Burgess’ bacon to be kept hot?” said Mr. Merry, his voice conveying criticism of Mr. Burgess, and the need of diplomacy with him.

“We do not usually expect people to come down when breakfast is over,” said Mrs. Merry. “The bacon was hot when it was brought in.”

“Fanny,” said Mr. Merry, in a tone of apology to the maid who was waiting, “just put Mr. Burgess’ bacon down in the fender, will you? Thank you, Fanny, very much.”

“I do not like more bread cut than will be eaten,” said Mrs. Merry.

“Mrs. Merry, several boys asked for some more only a minute ago,” said Miss Basden.

===Chinese===
「妈妈，伯吉斯先生的培根是不是该给他保着温？」梅里先生说，话音里既含着对伯吉斯先生的批评，也含着对这位下属须加周旋的意思。

「早饭吃完了才下楼的人，我们一般是不等的，」梅里太太说。「培根端进来的时候是热的。」

「范妮，」梅里先生用抱歉的口吻对侍立一旁的女仆说，「麻烦把伯吉斯先生的培根搁在炉围子上，好吗？谢谢你，范妮，多谢了。」

「我不喜欢切的面包超过吃得掉的量，」梅里太太说。

「梅里太太，好几个孩子一分钟前才又添了些，」巴斯登小姐说。

===Original===
“If you asked for it, eat it,” said Mr. Merry. “If you asked for it, eat it. Do not give the trouble of cutting it, and then cause more disturbance by refusing it. If you have eaten all the rest, you can easily manage a little more.”

As the chairs were pushed from the table, Mr. Herrick entered. He was the actual owner of the school, Mr. Merry being his partner. He was a short, impressive old man with a solid neck and head, heavy grey hair, and features with a touch of the Jew. He was a writer, and kept his time for his own, and read prayers to the school by way of acting as its head. He had placed his school in the old university town, to be near the college where he had spent his youth. Mr. Merry stood out in the room, half bowing towards him.

“Good morning, Mr. Herrick, good morning. It is a frosty day. That is why we are a little late. Frost makes good appetites; that is one good thing that it does. We were just going to get up from the table, when there was a demand for more bread and butter. By no means the first demand, eh, boys?”

The boys signified amongst themselves their sense of the doubleness of Mr. Merry’s nature. Mr. Herrick half bowed in his turn to Mr. Merry, quite bowed to Mrs. Merry, and again to Miss Basden; nodded to the boys, and sat down and opened the Bible. As he did so, there appeared Mr. Burgess, who walked into the room with an air of being unconscious that he would not find things at a natural stage for a newcomer.

“Ah, Mr. Burgess,” said Mr. Merry, as Mr. Burgess’ eyes began to take in that matters had gone so far; “we have been talking about you. The only thing we could do, as we could not talk to you. There is a place that is very comfortable on a frosty morning. Eh?”

===Chinese===
「要了就得吃掉，」梅里先生说。「要了就得吃掉。别劳人切了，回头又不要，惹出更多乱子。其余的都吃完了，再多吃一点也不费事。」

椅子从桌边推开时，赫里克先生走了进来。他才是学校真正的主人，梅里先生是他的合伙人。他是个矮小而气度慑人的老头，脖颈和头颅敦实，灰发浓重，眉目间带几分犹太人的相貌。他是个作家，时间归他自己支配，给全校读读祈祷文，算是履行校长之职。他把学校设在这座古老的大学城里，为的是离他度过青年时代的学院近一些。梅里先生在屋子当中迎着，朝他半欠着身。

「早上好，赫里克先生，早上好。今天有霜。所以我们略迟了些。霜天开胃口；这是它的一桩好处。我们正要离桌，偏偏有人又要添黄油面包。这可不是头一回有人要了，嗯，孩子们？」

孩子们彼此使着眼色，表示他们领教了梅里先生性格的两面。赫里克先生这回向梅里先生半欠了欠身，向梅里太太则实打实地鞠了一躬，又向巴斯登小姐一躬；朝孩子们点点头，坐下来翻开《圣经》。就在这时，伯吉斯先生现身了，他走进屋来，一副浑然不觉新来者本该赶上的自然进程早已过去的神情。

「啊，伯吉斯先生，」梅里先生说，此时伯吉斯先生的眼睛已开始看出事情进展到了哪一步；「我们方才正谈论您呢。没法跟您本人谈，只好这样了。有个地方在霜天的早晨可真舒服。嗯？」

===Original===
Mr. Merry’s way of addressing his head and his junior for the ears of the boys, had grown to a habit.

“Am I late?” said Mr. Burgess, in a casual, courteous tone.

“Good morning, Burgess,” said Herrick, his eyes on his book.

Mr. Merry sat down.

Mr. Burgess, as just perceiving that prayers were about to be held, took his seat.

===Chinese===
梅里先生当着孩子们的耳朵谈论自己的校长与年轻同僚的那套腔调，久已成了习惯。

「我迟到了吗？」伯吉斯先生说，口气随便而客气。

「早上好，伯吉斯，」赫里克说，眼睛盯着书。

梅里先生坐下了。

伯吉斯先生像是刚刚发觉祈祷就要开始，落了座。

===Original===
“Pray do not allow us to keep you waiting for breakfast, Burgess,” said Herrick. “Take your seat at the table, I beg of you.”

“Oh no, Mr. Herrick. I shall like to﻿ ﻿… take my part with you as usual, thank you. It matters very little to me what time I breakfast. Often I am out of doors for an hour or two before. I have very little sense of time in the morning.”

There was a titter from the boys.

“Yes, Mr. Herrick,” said Mr. Merry, with an air of apology, and a fierce sidelong glance; “he goes out sometimes before breakfast to get his exercise, you know. Young fellows always will be thinking of keeping fit, you know.”

“Mr. Burgess will have to take out the boys this morning, Mr. Merry,” said Miss Basden, leaning forward.

===Chinese===
「千万别让我们耽误了您吃早饭，伯吉斯，」赫里克说。「请务必入座用餐吧。」

「哦不，赫里克先生。我愿意……照常和你们一起尽我的那份，谢谢。几点吃早饭对我来说无关紧要。之前我常在外面待上一两个钟头。早晨我对时间是很没有感觉的。」

孩子们发出一阵窃笑。

「是啊，赫里克先生，」梅里先生说，一脸赔罪的神气，兼有一记凶狠的斜眼；「他有时早饭前出去锻炼身体，您知道。年轻人嘛，总惦记着保持体格，您知道。」

「伯吉斯先生今天上午总得带孩子们出去了，梅里先生，」巴斯登小姐欠身向前说。

===Original===
Mr. Burgess sat with an air exclusively expectant of Herrick’s reading. He greatly disliked Miss Basden, though there was a belief among the boys that he designed to wed her. Herrick began to read, and Miss Basden listened with colour deepened, and Mr. Burgess with an appearance of attentive interest. When the assembly rose from its knees, Herrick at once bore the Bible from the room. Mr. Burgess stepped to the window, and sought the signs of the sky.

“Come, now, Mr. Burgess,” said Mr. Merry, making amends to his junior by lifting his bacon himself from the fender. “If you are not hungry by this time, you should be. Pray do not hurry. The boys will not hurt by a little waiting.”

“Oh, thank you, Mr. Merry,” said Mr. Burgess, coming to the table, and taking up the sugar tongs. “I had no idea of the time. But boys do not hurt by a little waiting; that is true. Adams, just see if my paper is in the hall.”

Adams obeyed with a feeling of respect for Mr. Burgess, which Mr. Merry was almost disposed to share. Mr. Burgess received his paper, and put down his fork to turn to its pages. Mr. Merry inquired if the boys were waiting for their footman to bring them their boots.

Mr. Burgess, left to himself, set to his breakfast with an energy he had not shown that day, except at his toilet. Even if boys did hurt by waiting, these would not have suffered. He put his paper in his pocket, and at a distance from the house began to read. Returning, he saw Mr. Merry at a window. He looked back at the paper, and in a moment turned to a boy and said: “So you see, Parliament thought that Bill a wrong one, and it was thrown out.”

===Chinese===
伯吉斯先生坐着，一副专候赫里克开读的神情。他对巴斯登小姐厌恶得很，尽管孩子们中间流传着说他打算娶她。赫里克开始诵读，巴斯登小姐听着，脸色愈见深沉，伯吉斯先生则是一副专注而饶有兴味的样子。众人从跪姿中起身时，赫里克随即捧着《圣经》出了屋。伯吉斯先生踱到窗前，观望天色。

「来吧，伯吉斯先生，」梅里先生说，亲自把培根从炉围上端起来，以此向下属赔补方才的失礼。「到这时候您就算不饿，也该饿了。请千万别急。孩子们等一等没什么坏处。」

「哦，谢谢您，梅里先生，」伯吉斯先生说着走到桌边，拿起糖夹。「我没留意时间。不过孩子们等一等确实没什么坏处；这话不假。亚当斯（Adams），去看看我的报纸是不是在门厅里。」

亚当斯领命而去，心里对伯吉斯先生怀着一股敬意，这敬意梅里先生几乎也想沾上几分。伯吉斯先生接过报纸，放下叉子翻看起来。梅里先生于是问孩子们是不是在等他们的男仆替他们拿靴子来。

伯吉斯先生独自一人，便埋头吃起早饭来，那份起劲是当天除梳洗之外未曾显示过的。就算孩子们等一等真有坏处，这些孩子也不至于吃苦头。他把报纸揣进口袋，走到离住宅有一段路的地方才读起来。回来的路上，他看见梅里先生在一扇窗前。他又低头看了一眼报纸，片刻之后转向一个孩子说：「你瞧，议会认为那个法案是个坏法案，把它否掉了。」

===Original===
“Well, what news, Mr. Burgess?” said Mr. Merry, coming into the hall.

“Oh, nothing to speak of. Just a few little things to glance at,” said Mr. Burgess, defining the scope of his attention. “Would you care to have the paper, Mr. Merry?”

“Thank you, Mr. Burgess, thank you. I am a poor man. I don’t rise to the *Times*.”

Mr. Burgess went without a word to the classroom where the elder pupils were taught. Mr. Merry shuffled into the other, and sat down before the younger boys. He had not been prompted to the teacher’s life by his liberal education. There were other and more urgent reasons for his choice.

Mrs. Merry taught the scripture of the school, and there was a general sense of her fitness for the task, based upon her temperament rather than her scholarship. Mr. Merry often mentioned the fact of her teaching it to parents.

===Chinese===
「哎，有什么新闻，伯吉斯先生？」梅里先生说着走进门厅。

「哦，没什么值得说的。不过几件小事，浏览一眼罢了，」伯吉斯先生说，顺便给自己的关注范围下了个定义。「您想要这份报纸吗，梅里先生？」

「谢谢您，伯吉斯先生，谢谢。我是个穷人。《泰晤士报》（*Times*）我可高攀不起。」

伯吉斯先生一言不发地去了年长学生上课的教室。梅里先生拖着脚步进了另一间，在年幼的孩子们面前坐下。他走上教书这条路，并非出于他的人文教养所感召。促使他做此选择另有更迫切的理由。

梅里太太教学校的圣经课，大家普遍觉得她堪当此任，凭的是她的性情而非学问。梅里先生常对家长们提起由她执教这一事实。

===Original===
The boys, who had a tendency to giggle this morning, took their seats about her table.

“It is the scripture lesson,” she said, with peaceful lips. “I think we are forgetting that.”

The boys read aloud a chapter verse by verse, and Mrs. Merry added observations, in a gentle, rather peculiar voice used only on these occasions. At questions she turned to the commentary, and read it out; and it was felt that difficulties had been met as far as reverence permitted. An especial discrepancy caused an increase of mirth. Mrs. Merry looked straight at the questioner.

“I don’t think we will cavil about it, Johnson. We will just think of it. That will be the best, and the most difficult thing. The book says nothing, you see.”

It was felt that a cheap effort had been made; but the laughter held its own.

===Chinese===
这天早晨孩子们本就有些咯咯傻笑的倾向，此刻围着她的桌子坐了下来。

「这是圣经课，」她说，嘴唇安详。「我看我们把这点给忘了。」

孩子们一节一节地朗读一章经文，梅里太太再作讲解，用的是一种只在这样的场合才用的温软而颇为特别的嗓音。遇到问题，她便翻注释，念出来；大家都感到疑难之处已在敬虔所允许的范围内得到了解决。一处格外扎眼的抵牾引来了更多的笑。梅里太太直直地看着提问的人。

「我想咱们不必在这上头挑剔，约翰逊。咱们只要想一想它就好。那样最好，也最难。你看，书里并没有说什么。」

大家都觉得这不过是一番廉价的努力；笑声却自不肯休。

===Original===
Mrs. Merry looked very long at a boy.

“It is only the Bible, Bentley; only the most sacred book in existence that you are laughing at.”

“It is only the Bible, Bentley.”

“I shall have to give you up, boys.”

Mrs. Merry, with a look round that said many things, rose and walked from the room.

===Chinese===
梅里太太久久地盯住一个孩子。

「这不过是《圣经》罢了，本特利（Bentley）；你笑的不过是世上最神圣的一本书罢了。」

「这不过是《圣经》罢了，本特利。」

「我只好不管你们了，孩子们。」

梅里太太环顾一周，那一眼说了许多话，随即起身走出了教室。

===Original===
“Constant laughing and inattention make it impossible to teach them!”

Mr. Merry entered, hunching his shoulders and driving his hands violently into his pockets.

“Well, now, I am degraded. *I* am degraded. I feel it a personal humiliation to have to come and speak to people supposed to be gentlemen, about their making it impossible for a lady to teach them. For a lady to teach them scripture! For a lady to teach them holy things! There is an unspeakable thing to find happening in my school. I shall not speak of it. I cannot bring myself to do so. And I hope that no boy in this room will speak to me again today. Because I could not come down to his level, and there is the truth for him.”

It was Mrs. Merry’s birthday, and she knew that a present from the boys would be placed on the table at tea. It had been subscribed for by them in private consultation with Miss Basden, and chosen by that lady and Mrs. Merry in the town together.

By tea time Mrs. Merry had reached the state, which justified her yearly message, that she was prevented by a headache from appearing. The boys begged Miss Basden to approach her. Mr. Merry sat vaguely ill at ease, kept as it were in bands by the parcel at the head of the table.

===Chinese===
「笑个不停，心思涣散，简直没法教！」

梅里先生走了进来，耸着肩，两只手狠狠地插进口袋。

「好了，这下可好，我被羞辱了。被羞辱的是*我*。我居然要来对一群想必算得上绅士的人说话，说他们搅得一位女士没法教他们课，我觉得这是对我个人的羞辱。让一位女士教他们《圣经》！让一位女士教他们圣洁的事物！我的学校里竟发生了这样一件说不出口的事。我不愿谈它。我说不出口。我还希望这屋里今天别再有一个孩子跟我说话。因为我降不到他那个层次去，这就是给他的实话。」

这天是梅里太太的生日，她知道吃茶点时桌上会摆上孩子们送的礼物。礼物是孩子们私下与巴斯登小姐商量后凑钱买的，由那位女士和梅里太太一同在城里选中。

到了茶点时分，梅里太太已进入了那种状态——这为她一年一度的托词提供了凭据——说头痛得没法露面。孩子们求巴斯登小姐去请她。梅里先生坐立难安、心神不定，仿佛被桌首那只包裹捆住了似的。

===Original===
Mrs. Merry sent word that she would try and come for a moment; and entered, flushed and unexpectant, and approached her place.

“Why, what is this? What can this be? Why, I do not understand this, boys.”

Miss Basden stepped forward and quickly cut the string, in the manner of one tying gunpowder to a friend at the stake.

“Oh, boys,” said Mrs. Merry. “Oh, boys! Oh, what is this? Oh, you should not spend your money on me. I had no expectation of anything like this. And the very thing that I needed! Some good fairy must have﻿ ﻿…”

Her voice failed.

===Chinese===
梅里太太捎话说她尽量来待一会儿；随后她进来了，红着脸，一副并无预期的样子，走向自己的座位。

「哎呀，这是什么？这能是什么呀？哎呀，我都看不懂了，孩子们。」

巴斯登小姐抢上一步，飞快地割断绳子，那姿态像是在给绑上火刑柱的朋友系炸药。

「哦，孩子们，」梅里太太说。「哦，孩子们！哎呀，这是什么呀？哦，你们不该为我花钱。我完全没想到会有这样的事。而且正是我需要的东西！一定是有位善良的仙子……」

她的声音哽住了。

===Original===
“There, Mother, there, you’re not well,” said Mr. Merry. “You’re not well, as you told us, Mother. And so you get upset. We know how it is. It is just because you look after us all too well. We know how pleased you are. So you need not trouble to tell us any more. Come, have your tea, boys. Have your tea, and it is very kind of you all. What is that commotion in the hall above? Williams, just see what the commotion in the hall is. Thank you, Williams, very much.”

It was reported that a lady was in the hall, and that Mr. Herrick requested that Mr. Merry would join him.

“What a time to come to see a school! After keeping myself free from seven in the morning, I am not allowed to have a cup of tea in peace. People will be coming in the middle of the night next. If there’s a thing I can’t bear, it’s want of consideration.”

Mr. Merry hastened up the stairs, and at the top caused his face to undergo a change, preparatory to whatever final one might be expedient.

“Oh, this is Mr. Merry! What a shame to call him away from his boys! But I can only stay a moment, so I shall not keep him.”

===Chinese===
「好了，妈妈，好了，你身子不舒服，」梅里先生说。「你身子不舒服，你自己对我们说过的，妈妈。所以你才这样激动。我们明白是怎么回事。就是因为你对我们都照顾得太好了。你有多欢喜，我们都知道。所以不必再费心说了。来，喝你们的茶吧，孩子们。喝茶吧，你们大家真是太好了。楼上门厅里闹哄哄的是什么？威廉斯（Williams），去看看门厅里在闹什么。谢谢你，威廉斯，多谢了。」

传话来说门厅里有位女士，赫里克先生请梅里先生过去。

「挑这么个时候来看学校！从早上七点起我就空着身子候着，到头来连一杯茶都不让人安生地喝。往后人家半夜里也要上门了。要说有什么我受不了的，就是这种不体谅人。」

梅里先生急步上楼，到了楼梯口，先让脸上变了一番，预备着视情势再换上最后所需的那一副。

「哦，这位就是梅里先生吧！把他从孩子们身边叫走，真不应该！不过我只能待一小会儿，不会多耽搁他的。」

===Original===
“Now, what a thing to be saying of me!” said Mr. Merry, taking the stranger’s hand in both of his, with fond eyes. “What a thing to be saying of me! That I should think it a shame to be called away to you. Not but what I am fond of my boys. Yes, I am fond of my boys.”

“I saw such a dear little boy in the hall.”

“Ah, the little rascal! Little mischief that he is, always running about, when he ought to be at his books! Ah, well, they like a run from time to time. And he is not over strong, the little fellow. When he came to us, it went to my heart to see him. But my wife, she coddles him, you know. She coddles him, and he is a different boy.”

“Oh, how nice for an anxious mother to hear! Because we can’t expect Mr. Herrick to give his mind to all the little things a mother thinks of.”

“Oh, we won’t tell Mr. Herrick what we think of him,” said Mr. Merry, as his chief followed his custom of leaving him unhampered play for his gifts. “Perhaps he knows without our telling him. Sometimes I see him reading all about himself in the papers.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，怎么能这样说我呢！」梅里先生说，双手握住来客的手，眼里含着深情。「怎么能这样说我呢！说我被叫到您这儿来是受委屈。不过我倒真是喜欢我的孩子们。是的，我喜欢我的孩子们。」

「我在门厅里见到一个多乖的小男孩。」

「啊，那个小淘气！真是个小捣蛋，该念书的时候总到处乱跑！唉，孩子们嘛，隔三差五总想撒开腿跑跑。再说这小家伙身体也不算壮实。他刚来我们这儿的时候，我看着都心疼。不过我太太疼他，您知道。她把他养得娇，他简直换了个人。」

「哦，一位牵肠挂肚的母亲听了这话，该多宽心啊！赫里克先生的心思毕竟顾不上母亲们惦记的种种小事。」

「哦，我们对赫里克先生的看法，就不告诉他本人了，」梅里先生说，此时他的上司一如惯例，任他不受拘束地施展本领。「也许不用我们说他也就知道了。我有时还看见他在报纸上读有关他自己的种种呢。」

===Original===
“And about the general teaching of the school? Mr. Herrick does not do a great deal of the ordinary work, does he?” Mr. Merry reflected that Herrick had gone far in the minutes before his release. “But of course you have masters to help you?”

“Now, I tell you a thing,” said Mr. Merry, lifting his questioner’s hand up and down, a picture of Mr. Burgess before his eyes. “You have confidence in us. In me and my wife, and my good helpers; and above all, in your boy. And there is one of us you won’t be disappointed in. I can promise you that. And that is the last. And the first too, isn’t he? Bless him, yes. And now there is a thing I want to say. I am here to make the boys happy. Little fellows away from home need to be made happy, you know. A master who is known all over the world is an enormous thing for a boy. I wish I had had as much in my young days. But that is not quite all that is wanted. A mother knows that.”

On his way back through the hall Mr. Merry passed Mr. Herrick without a word. The quality of Mr. Merry’s that gained him his bread was never alluded to between them.

“Well, there is another one come and gone, Mother. Another ship in the night. One never knows if anything will come of it. And a third of what comes out of one more boy won’t overflow our coffers, will it?”

There was a titter from the boys.

===Chinese===
「那么学校总体的教学呢？日常的活计赫里克先生做得不多吧？」梅里先生心忖，在他得以脱身之前的那几分钟里，这一位可走得够远了。「当然，您另有几位先生帮衬着？」

「来，我跟您说一件事，」梅里先生说，把问话人的手握着一上一下地颠着，眼前浮现着伯吉斯先生的形象。「您信赖我们。信赖我和我太太，还有我的好帮手们；最要紧的是，信赖您的孩子。我们中间有一位，您绝不会对他失望。这一点我可以向您担保。他是最后一位。也是头一位，不是吗？愿老天保佑他，没错。现在我还有一句话要说。我在这儿，是为了让孩子们快活。离家在外的小家伙们，需要有人让他们快活，您知道。有一位举世闻名的老师，对一个孩子是天大的事。我年轻时要有这么些就好了。可这还不是全部需要的东西。做母亲的明白这一点。」

回来穿过门厅时，梅里先生与赫里克先生擦肩而过，一言不发。梅里先生那为他挣来面包的本事，两人之间是从不提起的。

「唉，又来了一位，又走了一位，妈妈。又是一条夜里交臂而过的船。能不能成什么事，谁也说不上。再说多一个孩子身上出产的三分之一，也漫不出咱们的钱箱，对不对？」

孩子们又是一阵窃笑。

===Original===
“You get on with your tea,” said Mr. Merry. “And don’t be so humiliatingly deceived as to think that another one of you would be of any advantage to anyone. Because such an idea would be humiliating to you. I can tell you that.”

“I do not think I could do with another boy,” said Mrs. Merry, in a gentle, distinct tone.

“No, I should not like you to have anything more on you, Mrs. Merry,” said Miss Basden.

“I don’t know that I could either,” said Mr. Burgess.

“Oh well, Mr. Burgess, it wouldn’t be too much on you,” said Mr. Merry. “We should see to that. It would be all fitting in together for you, you know.”

===Chinese===
「你们只管喝茶，」梅里先生说。「也别那么丢人地自欺，以为你们再多来一个对谁会有好处。因为这种念头本身就够丢你们的脸了。这话我可以告诉你们。」

「我想我应付不来再多一个孩子了，」梅里太太说，声音温和而清晰。

「是啊，我可不愿意您再添什么担子了，梅里太太，」巴斯登小姐说。

「我看我也应付不来，」伯吉斯先生说。

「哎，伯吉斯先生，那可累不着您，」梅里先生说。「这点我们会留意的。对您来说，一切都正好顺理成章地搭配上，您明白。」

===Original===
Mr. Burgess looked towards the window.

“I could always do with what had to be done with,” said Miss Basden. “But the boys are quite mistaken, if they think that is any indication that they are needed by anyone. It is they who do the needing, I think.”

“Yes, Miss Basden,” said Mr. Merry, nodding his head. “It is indeed. They who do the needing! I should think so. I should be glad to be told of something they don’t need. Because I don’t know of anything, and that is the truth. I have just been hearing what is good enough for them. And I don’t see anything about them that seems to call for it. I don’t, indeed.”

===Chinese===
伯吉斯先生朝窗户望去。

「凡是不得不做的事，我一向应付得了，」巴斯登小姐说。「不过孩子们若是以为这就表明有谁离不开他们，那他们就想岔了。离不开别人的，是他们，我看。」

「是啊，巴斯登小姐，」梅里先生点着头说。「一点不错。是他们离不开！可不是嘛。有谁要是能说出一样他们不需要的东西，我倒要谢谢他。因为我自己一样也想不出来，这是实话。方才我还听说了什么是够他们受用的。可我看不出他们身上有什么地方配得上这份受用。真看不出，确实。」

## II / 二

===Original===
“This is a good room to come back to,” said Herrick. “That hall and the woman, and poor Merry shuffling up to do his duty! It made me shiver.”

“The sight of duty does make one shiver,” said Miss Herrick. “The actual doing of it would kill one, I think.”

Emily Herrick was a tall, dark woman of fifty, half-sister to Herrick, with a face that somehow recalled an attractive idol’s, iron-grey hair wound in plaits about her head, and a quick, deep voice.

“Merry knows what the duty is,” said Herrick. “For my life, I don’t.”

“One couldn’t know what that duty was,” said Miss Herrick. “It could only be felt, and perhaps you have too good a brain to do things in that way.”

===Chinese===
「回到这间屋子里真好，」赫里克说。「那个门厅，那个女人，还有可怜的梅里拖着脚步上前尽职！看得我直打冷战。」

「看见别人尽职确实叫人打冷战，」赫里克小姐（Miss Herrick）说。「真要去做起来，我想能要人的命。」

艾米丽·赫里克（Emily Herrick）是个五十岁、深肤色的高个子女人，是赫里克的异母妹妹，那张脸不知怎的叫人想起一尊妩媚的神像，铁灰色的头发编成发辫盘在头上，嗓音急促而低沉。

「梅里知道那职责是什么，」赫里克说。「我呢，拼了命也不知道。」

「谁也不可能知道那职责是什么，」赫里克小姐说。「它只能去感觉，而您的脑子太好，或许干不来这种事。」

===Original===
“Let us leave it at that,” said Herrick.

“To think that you made the school!” said Emily. “For it was you who made it. But of course you would do the creative part.”

“Yes, yes. And I could go on with it,” said Herrick.

“Of course you could,” said Emily. “Wouldn’t it be dreadful if you had to? Or if you did? It is almost dreadful that you could.”

“Yes, yes, it is,” said Herrick. “I am an ordinary man.”

===Chinese===
「话就到此为止吧，」赫里克说。

「想想吧，这学校是您一手创办的！」艾米丽说。「创办它的确实是您。当然啦，您总是要揽创造的份儿。」

「是啊，是啊。而且我是办得下去的，」赫里克说。

「您当然办得下去，」艾米丽说。「可要是您非办不可呢？或者您真办下去了呢？岂不可怕？光凭您办得下去这一点，就够可怕的了。」

「是啊，是啊，够可怕的，」赫里克说。「我是个凡人。」

===Original===
“I didn’t mean that, darling,” said Emily. “They say that teachers are born, not made. I know that schoolmasters are. And it was for a schoolmaster that we wanted Mr. Merry.”

“It is for a schoolmaster we have him, anyhow,” said Herrick, sitting down at his desk.

“We must get out of the way of talking as if we were not honest, Nicholas,” said Emily. “Anyone dishonest beside Mr. Merry would be such a waste, and wastefulness in a school is so unwise. And Mr. Merry has such charm in all that he does.”

“Merry is as open as the day,” said Herrick.

“Of course,” said Emily. “That is what I meant. What charm could there be in dishonesty apart from that? Ought you to write any more today, dear? You have written more than usual, after being up all night.”

===Chinese===
「我不是那个意思，亲爱的，」艾米丽说。「人家说，教师是天生的，不是造就出来的。我知道校长是天生的。我们当初要梅里先生，要的就是个当校长的。」

「不管怎么说，我们要到手的正是个校长，」赫里克说着，在书桌前坐下。

「咱们得改掉这种说话的腔调，倒好像咱们不老实似的，尼古拉斯（Nicholas），」艾米丽说。「有梅里先生在旁边，谁还要不老实，那也太浪费了，而在一所学校里，浪费是顶不聪明的。再说梅里先生做什么都那么有魅力。」

「梅里坦荡得像白昼一样，」赫里克说。

「当然，」艾米丽说。「我就是这个意思。除了那份坦荡，不老实里还能有什么魅力？您今天还该再写吗，亲爱的？您熬了一整夜，写的已经比平常多了。」

===Original===
“I must go on separating my papers from old Crabbe’s,” said Herrick. “I found some things of his to go through, and I took some of my own to deal with. I knew it would be a long night. And he did not want much looking to, poor old Crabbe! Eighty-nine if he was a day, and a long sinking, as if he were not done out enough to die! He did not suffer, or hardly, and Masson and Bumpus and I watched by him at the end. Well, may we all do as well, die at ninety, easily, and with our friends round us. I had rather have friends than children. Men with the same outlook, not people looking for their main spell after we are gone! Well, Emily, I hope you will watch by me one day, as I watched by my old friend last night.”

“How beautifully you speak, darling!” said Emily, putting up her glass to look at her brother. “And what a lot of good you have done! But I don’t think you have made very kind plans for me. But perhaps you have left my deathbed to Mr. Merry. There is no reason why he shouldn’t take all your responsibilities. And to carry out your scheme for yourself for an outsider, is very rare of you. It could hardly be expected for one of your own family.”

“Ah, Emily,” said Herrick. “You are twenty years younger than me. That is a thing we don’t reckon with, but it is there. You don’t look back or forward yet. We can’t look forward really; but I think I can look back, and see my life as not lacking.”

Herrick lived in his disappointment that in letters he had done only critical work.

“I never look back on my life,” said Emily. “It seems to be lacking in too much. You are so brave. There are William and Richard coming in. Guests must not be torn between admiration and anxiety, like people in the house.”

===Chinese===
「我还得继续把我的文稿跟老克拉布（Crabbe）的分开来，」赫里克说。「我找出了一些他的东西要整理，也带了些我自己的来处理。我知道这一夜会很长。他也用不着怎么照料了，可怜的老克拉布！少说也有八十九岁了，久病消磨，仿佛不把他耗干他就不肯死似的！他没受什么罪，或者说几乎没受，最后是马森（Masson）、邦珀斯（Bumpus）和我守着他。唉，愿我们到时候都这样体面，九十岁上，安安然然地死去，朋友们围在身边。我宁可要朋友，不要儿女。是跟我见解相同的人，而不是等着我们一走就惦记着我们那份进项的人！哎，艾米丽，我希望有一天你也这样守着我，就像我昨夜守着我的老朋友一样。」

「您说得多动人啊，亲爱的！」艾米丽说，举起眼镜端详她的兄长。「您做了多少好事啊！可我觉得您为我作的安排可不怎么仁慈。不过您大概把我的临终托给梅里先生了。没有理由不让他把您的责任全都承揽过去。您把给自己定下的方案在一个外人身上演练了一遍，真是难得。对自己家里人，倒未必能指望这个。」

「啊，艾米丽，」赫里克说。「你比我年轻二十岁。这一点我们平时不计较，可它是在那儿的。你还不回头看，也不往前看。往前看其实我们谁也不能；可我想我已经能够回头看了，看到我这一生并无缺憾。」

赫里克一辈子耿耿于怀的是，在文学上他只做过批评的工作。

「我从不回头看我的生活，」艾米丽说。「缺的东西太多了，看它做什么。您真勇敢。威廉和理查德进来了。客人可不能像自家人这样，又敬佩又担忧，两头撕扯。」

===Original===
William Masson was a tall, large man in late middle age, with loose limbs and loose clothes, and a weather-beaten, high-boned face. He seemed an example of all the uneasinesses combined into ease.

His companion was a little, dark man about fifty-six, with eyes sunk deeply in a working face.

The two were Fellows and dons at Herrick’s college, and had meant romance for each other in youth. They had watched with Herrick at the deathbed of an old don, who had outlived his friends.

“Well, well,” said Herrick, “so he is gone, the old man! We shall all be in his place one day. I am fortunate in having you all younger than me. I have not to think of dying, surrounded by no one who is of my own kind and age. I can’t help being glad that it is I who will have to look to you at the end, and not you to me.”

“He has been explaining to me,” said Emily, “how I must die, unattended, while he has everybody kept alive for him. Nicholas has a wonderful gift for getting the best out of others. Think of Mr. Merry.”

===Chinese===
威廉·马森（William Masson）是位年近老境的高大男人，四肢松散，衣着宽大，一张饱经风霜、颧骨突出的脸。他仿佛是种种不安适汇聚成安适的一个范例。

他的同伴是个五十六岁上下、深肤色的小个子男人，一双眼睛深深陷在一张劳作的脸里。

这两人都是赫里克所在学院的研究员和导师，年轻时彼此都把对方当作一段传奇。他们同赫里克一起在一位老导师的临终榻前守过夜——那位老先生的友朋都已先他而去了。

「好了，好了，」赫里克说，「他到底是去了，那位老人家！有朝一日我们都会轮到他那个位置。幸好你们个个都比我年轻。我不必去想死的时候身边没有一个同类同辈的人。想到最后是我来照看你们，而不是你们来照看我，我不由得高兴。」

「他一直在给我讲解，」艾米丽说，「说我将来必定孤零零地死去，无人照料，而他却有本事让每个人都为他活着。尼古拉斯有一种奇妙的天才，能把别人身上最好的部分榨取出来。想想梅里先生吧。」

===Original===
“I never cease to revere you for that, Herrick,” said Bumpus.

“I don’t really revere Nicholas so much as Mr. Merry,” said Emily. “At least, I don’t know. It was wonderful of Nicholas. I thought it mattered about Mr. Merry’s not being educated, as we were having him for a schoolmaster.”

“Yes, yes,” said Masson, in his high, stammering voice. “I confess I rather thought that.”

“I did not think that,” said Bumpus. “But I didn’t know what did matter. The thing Merry has, isn’t a thing one could guess.”

“Well, I always trust myself to judge a person at sight,” said Herrick. “It is borne in upon me how a person is this or that. And I never find myself wrong. It is a gift that we are either with or without.”

===Chinese===
「为这一点我对您的敬意从未间断过，赫里克，」邦珀斯说。

「我对尼古拉斯的敬意其实还不及对梅里先生的，」艾米丽说。「至少，我也说不清。尼古拉斯是了不起。我原先以为，梅里先生没受过教育终归是个问题，既然我们是请他来当校长的。」

「是啊，是啊，」马森用他那尖细、结巴的嗓音说。「我承认我也这么想过。」

「我倒没这么想，」邦珀斯说。「可我也不知道要紧的究竟是什么。梅里身上那样东西，不是谁猜得出来的。」

「这么说吧，我一向相信自己一眼就能看准一个人，」赫里克说。「一个人是怎么样的，那是深印在我心里的。我还从没发现自己看错过。这种才能，人有就是有，没有就是没有。」

===Original===
“I believe gifts are like that,” said Bumpus.

“That is unkind, Dickie,” said Emily.

“You may have to see us all out, Herrick,” said Bumpus. “You are past the danger age.”

“It is true that people who live to be seventy often do live to a great age,” said Herrick. “They are in a sense out of danger.”

“I believe you seem to be planning my death,” said Emily, “and without any arrangements at all for my deathbed. I hope you really do trust Mr. Merry. Not only with the kind of trust that does for boys.”

===Chinese===
「我看各种才能都是这样，」邦珀斯说。

「这话可不厚道，迪基（Dickie），」艾米丽说。

「弄到头来，说不定是您送我们所有人，赫里克，」邦珀斯说。「您已经过了危险的年纪。」

「活到七十的人往往真能活到很大年纪，这话不假，」赫里克说。「从某种意义上说，他们已经脱离危险了。」

「我看你们简直在安排我的死期了，」艾米丽说，「而且对我的临终榻没有半点安排。我希望您对梅里先生是真心信任。可不只是对付男孩子的那种信任。」

===Original===
“I am sixty,” said Masson. “I claim also to be out of danger.”

“How exacting you all are about your amount of life!” said Bumpus. “But of course we are all of us too good to die.”

“Well, well, we shall go on, doing things it has been in us to do here, that we have not done,” said Herrick. “Ah, you do not think so, Emily. But this modern thought has you in its grip. You will grow out of it.”

“That will be nice and flexible of me, to grow out of things at fifty. They say that unusual people do develop very late.”

“I can’t understand how people fear death,” said Herrick. “It seems to me the most usual, natural thing, the last thing to be feared.”

===Chinese===
「我六十了，」马森说。「我也自认脱离危险了。」

「你们对自己那份寿命可真讲究！」邦珀斯说。「不过当然，我们个个都好得死不了。」

「好了，好了，我们还会接着活下去，去做那些一直在我们心里、却还没做的事，」赫里克说。「啊，你不这么想，艾米丽。可这种现代思想把你攥在手心里了。你早晚会长出它的。」

「到了五十岁还能从种种东西里长出来，我可真够灵便的。人家不常说，不寻常的人物都发育得极晚么。」

「我不明白人们怎么会怕死，」赫里克说。「在我看来，那是最寻常、最自然的事，是顶不必害怕的事。」

===Original===
“It is very usual. It might even be called universal,” said Bumpus.

“Don’t be shallow and witty, Dickie,” said Emily. “Nicholas was speaking of deeper truth.”

“I don’t find anything shocking in seeing death ahead for me,” said Masson.

“No, you see it as it is, and yourself as you are,” said Bumpus. “You are a long way above and below the rest of us. Below is the wrong word.”

“I should have thought he was above everything,” said Emily. “I never like to think of it for him. It must be so hard to be like that. We are very brave to be able to talk about death. As brave as people who feel able to make their wills.”

===Chinese===
「确实很寻常。简直可以叫作人人有份，」邦珀斯说。

「别贫嘴，迪基，」艾米丽说。「尼古拉斯讲的是更深的道理。」

「看到死亡在前头等我，我不觉得有什么骇人，」马森说。

「是啊，你照事物的本来面目看它，也照你自己的本来面目看自己，」邦珀斯说。「你比我们这些人高出许多，也低出许多。『低』字用得不对。」

「我倒以为他高出于一切之上呢，」艾米丽说。「我从来不喜欢替他想到那个。做那样的人一定很苦。我们能这样谈论死亡，真够勇敢的。跟那些自觉立得下遗嘱的人一样勇敢。」

===Original===
“I always wondered why Crabbe never wrote,” said Herrick. “He should have, shouldn’t he? Shouldn’t he now have written?”

“He never did. Not a line,” said Bumpus.

“I suppose a search after anything would be no good? I came across two or three scraps of different things. They are over there with my papers. But nothing of use.”

“No, no, no,” said Masson, firmly for him. “A search for anything would be no good. He had nothing. He kept nothing. He was as light as air. He had no friends but ourselves. No contemporaries outliving him. There seems to be no one to be notified of his death.”

“That is how Nicholas wants me to be,” said Emily.

===Chinese===
「我一直纳闷克拉布怎么从来不写东西，」赫里克说。「他本该写的，不是吗？他到现在不是早该写出来了吗？」

「他从来没写过。一行也没有，」邦珀斯说。

「我想再搜找什么也是白费？我撞见两三张零碎的不同东西。就在那边我的文稿里。可没有一样有用。」

「不，不，不，」马森说，就他而言算是斩钉截铁了。「搜找什么都是白费。他什么都没有。他什么也不留。他轻得像空气。除了我们，他没有朋友。同辈里没有活得比他久的。他的死讯似乎也没有谁需要通知。」

「尼古拉斯要的就是我变成这样，」艾米丽说。

===Original===
“Ah!” said Herrick.

“He has died as he lived,” went on Masson. “Keeping nothing, needing nothing, asking nothing. A search of his rooms would be like searching a field.”

“You ought to write, William,” said Emily. “You really have written, after saying things like that.”

“I have been thinking of writing again,” said Bumpus, putting his hand over his eyes. “But I expect you all forget that I ever wrote. I don’t know if you do remember how dramatic I was once as a youth, when﻿—natural and reasonable it seemed to me then﻿—I caused a manuscript to be put into the grave of a friend? I have written nothing since. I didn’t mean that his death prevented me from writing later. That sort of thing comes out, if it is there. Though it is astonishing how many of us are capable of a single thing. But I have been writing a little book lately. I don’t know why I should say little, except that it is short.”

“This is good to hear, Richard,” said Masson.

===Chinese===
「啊！」赫里克说。

「他死得就像他活着时一样，」马森接着说。「什么都不留，什么都不缺，什么都不求。搜他的屋子，就跟搜一片田野一样。」

「你真该写作，威廉（William），」艾米丽说。「说出这样的话来，你其实已经算写过了。」

「我近来一直在想重新写作，」邦珀斯说，一只手遮在眼睛上。「不过你们大概都忘了我也写过东西。不知你们还记不记得，我年轻时曾是多么戏剧性的人物——当时我自己觉得又自然又在理——我曾叫人把一部手稿放进一位朋友的坟墓？自那以后我再没写过什么。我并不是说他的死妨碍了我后来写作。那种东西要是还在，总会出来的。虽说我们中间能做成一件事的人何其多，倒也惊人。不过我近来一直在写一本小书。我不知道为什么要说『小』，只因为它篇幅短罢了。」

「听见这个太好了，理查德（Richard），」马森说。

===Original===
“Too good,” said Emily. “I am so jealous for Nicholas. And he hasn’t a story in his life, either. I mean, not a beautiful story.”

“I seem as if I must still write like a boy,” said Bumpus, more freely and eagerly. “I find myself having to prune and tighten and mature. I don’t know if it is breaking off in youth that makes me go on as if from where I left off. I don’t believe we are ever much farther than at twenty-five.”

“I don’t think I am,” said Herrick. “For I also am thinking of bringing out a little book soon. Yes, Emily, you may look at me through that glass. I put off telling you until I should have the countenance of friends. For pleading guilty to turning real author at seventy! So we coincide, Bumpus. But I wish our ages coincided.”

“This is surely coincidence enough,” said Bumpus.

“I don’t wish anything more,” said Emily. “And how wonderful we are to have you coincide! It is selfless and beautiful of William and me.”

===Chinese===
「好得过分了，」艾米丽说。「我都替尼古拉斯嫉妒了。再说他一辈子也挑不出一段故事。我是说，没有一段美的故事。」

「我好像还非得像个小伙子那样写不可，」邦珀斯说，语气更自由、更急切了。「我发现自己不得不修剪、收紧、变得成熟。我不知道是不是年轻时戛然中断，使我从当年搁笔的地方接着往下走。我不信我们这辈子比二十五岁时能走出多远。」

「我想我也没走出多远，」赫里克说。「因为我也打算不久拿出一本小书来。是啊，艾米丽，你尽管用那眼镜瞧我吧。我一直拖着不告诉你，就是等有朋友们给我撑腰。七十岁才承认自己成了真正的作家，这罪状我认了！这么一来我们撞上了，邦珀斯。可惜愿我们的年纪也撞上才好。」

「这巧合还不算够么，」邦珀斯说。

「我不指望更多了，」艾米丽说。「你们俩撞到一起，我们多了不起啊！这是威廉和我无私而美妙的地方。」

===Original===
“That is what I feel,” said Bumpus.

“And I also am pleased with my little book,” went on Herrick, taking his sister’s hand, “and also don’t know why I call it little, except that it is short. Short it is, and that’s the truth; though I don’t know why we should prefer a long book. If a book is a whole in itself, why is its length any matter?”

“But you do prefer a long one, darling,” said Emily. “I always like that side of you. And now I know it is the simplicity of greatness. But don’t make the book any longer. It is so careless of popular opinion to leave it short.”

“I wish﻿ ﻿… I wonder why we give voice to these wishes; but I wish old Crabbe had lived to see you with a book written, Herrick,” said Masson. “He always said that you were a man who would write, if you put yourself to it.”

“Just what Nicholas has been saying of him,” said Emily. “How beautiful they both are!”

===Chinese===
「我的感觉正是这样，」邦珀斯说。

「我也很中意我的小书，」赫里克接着说，握住妹妹的手，「也说不清为什么叫它小书，只因为它篇幅短罢了。短是真的短，这是实话；虽说我不明白我们为什么偏爱长书。一本书若是自成整体，长短又有什么要紧？」

「可你确实偏爱长的，亲爱的，」艾米丽说。「我一直喜欢你这一面。现在我明白了，这是伟大的质朴。不过可别把书再拉长了。就让它短短的，全不理会世人的看法，多好。」

「我但愿……我也奇怪我们为什么总要把这些愿望说出口；可我但愿老克拉布活着看到你把书写出来，赫里克，」马森说。「他常说，你这个人只要肯下功夫，是会写出东西来的。」

「尼古拉斯方才说的正是他。他们两个多美啊！」

===Original===
“I have been a long fool, idle through seventy years of a good life,” said Herrick. “But I don’t know that I can wish that about Crabbe. I feel as if I should not have written this book, apart from his death; as if it would not have shaped itself in my mind as I now feel it. Of course there is no connection. None at all. None. But it came to me, as I sat there, the whole thing, the whole book. There it was. I can’t explain it.”

“It doesn’t seem good management,” said Emily. “To keep your book to yourself for seventy years, and then have Mr. Crabbe die to make it come out. But you are not a good manager; and Mr. Merry can’t do more than most of our life for both of us, and my death. And Mr. Crabbe was ninety, and you had it all so nice for him.”

“How long will it take you actually to write it, Herrick?” said Bumpus.

“I have it this time,” said Herrick. “I have been letting it grow in my head. Because of course in a way it has been there for some time. It is as good as written down. It was the form of it that flashed on me. I do things much in my head, as you know.”

“Oh, Nicholas, I didn’t know. I do admire you,” said Emily.

===Chinese===
「我当了一辈子的傻子，虚度了七十年好时光，」赫里克说。「不过关于克拉布，我不知道自己愿不愿意那样想。我总觉得，若不是他的死，我写不出这本书；它也不会像我现在感到的那样，在我心里成形。当然，两者毫无关联。一点也没有。没有。可我坐在那儿的时候，整件事、整本书，就那样降临到我这里。它就在那儿了。我解释不了。」

「这可算不上经营有方，」艾米丽说。「把你的书在自己肚里揣了七十年，然后要让克拉布先生死一场，才把它催出来。可你本来就不善经营；梅里先生替我们俩打理大半个日子，还要打理我的死，也就做到头了。再说克拉布先生已经九十岁，你把一切都替他安排得那么妥帖。」

「把它真正写下来要用多久，赫里克？」邦珀斯说。

「这一回我心里已经有它了，」赫里克说。「我一直让它在脑子里生长。当然，在某种意义上，它已经在那儿有些时候了。它跟写成了也差不多。是它的形体在我心头一闪而成的。你们知道，我做事大半是在脑子里做的。」

「哦，尼古拉斯，我都不知道。我真是佩服你，」艾米丽说。

===Original===
“I think I have found myself at last,” said Herrick. “I think that, God willing, I shall have done my little bit for my generation, done what every man ought to do, before he dies.”

“You don’t really think it is what every man ought to do,” said Emily. “I do hope it isn’t.”

“Assuming God, you wouldn’t do much if he wasn’t willing,” said Masson.

Bumpus laughed, and looked almost proudly at Masson.

“Are we going to be broad and wicked?” said Emily. “I like that, because I am not very educated, and so still young in my mind. Really, it would be nice to have some religion, and not go on without ever any comfort. And I am not like Nicholas, who is really God’s equal, and not his child at all. I think it is better not to have God than to be like that with him.”

===Chinese===
「我想我终于找到了我自己，」赫里克说。「我想，若蒙上帝允准，我总算为同代人尽了绵薄，做成了每个人在死前都该做成的事。」

「你并不是真觉得那是每个人都该做的事，」艾米丽说。「我倒真希望不是。」

「假定有上帝，他不允准的话，你也做不成什么，」马森说。

邦珀斯笑了，几乎带着骄傲望着马森。

「我们要放达一下、亵渎一下了么？」艾米丽说。「我喜欢这样，因为我没什么学问，心性还年轻。说真的，有点宗教才好，免得一路走下去连半点安慰也没有。我又不像尼古拉斯，他其实是上帝的平辈，压根儿不是他的孩子。我看，与其那样跟上帝相处，还不如没有上帝。」

===Original===
“It is rather empty for him not to be had,” said Bumpus. “He always seems to me a pathetic figure, friendless and childless and set up alone in a miserable way.”

“Yes, he has a touch of William in him,” said Emily. “But you know he isn’t childless. We give even our boys more advantages than that. Mrs. Merry gives it to them.”

“You can have him childless in these days,” said Bumpus. “But if you have him, I like him really. I like him not childless, and grasping and fond of praise. I like the human and family interest.”

“Yes, he tends to be neutral nowadays,” said Masson. “Perhaps I do resemble him in that.”

“And he had such a personality,” said Emily. “Such a superior, vindictive and overindulgent one. He is one of the best drawn characters in fiction.”

===Chinese===
「没有了他，未免空得慌，」邦珀斯说。「在我看，他一向是个可怜的形象，无友无儿，孤零零凄惨惨地被供在那儿。」

「是啊，他身上有几分威廉的影子，」艾米丽说。「不过你知道，他可不是没有儿女。连我们的孩子们得到的待遇都比那强。是梅里太太给他们儿女之情的。」

「如今你可以叫他无儿无女了，」邦珀斯说。「可既然要有他，我就要个真的他。我喜欢他有儿有女，贪财爱听恭维。我喜欢这点人间烟火、家宅里巷的情味。」

「是啊，如今他往往不偏不倚，」马森说。「这一点上我或许真像他。」

「况且他当年多有个性，」艾米丽说。「那么高高在上、睚眦必报、又一味纵容的个性。他是小说里刻画得最出色的人物之一。」

===Original===
“I really cannot listen to this,” said Herrick.

“Isn’t it quietly conscientious of Herrick, to be behind the parents’ backs what he is to their faces?” said Bumpus. “What he would be to their faces if he saw them.”

“I am deeply grateful to Merry,” said Herrick. “Nobody knows what seeing them is.”

“Mr. Merry does,” said Emily. “I am not grateful to him. I am cringing under a load of obligation. And he is a tragic figure, and haunts me. Now to Mrs. Merry I am just healthily grateful. And to Miss Basden my gratitude is quite of a brisk, employing kind. I almost feel more kindly to Mr. Burgess, who has to have the opposite of gratitude, though I never quite know why. Now I must go and ask Mrs. Merry if we may ask you to stay to dinner. I always think Nicholas and I carry that off so well, having to ask permission to have guests.”

“How Emily runs on and on, doesn’t she?” said Herrick. “Day after day, year after year, the stream never runs dry.”

===Chinese===
「这样的话我实在听不下去，」赫里克说。

「赫里克背着家长是当面那一套，这可真是悄悄的尽责，」邦珀斯说。「也算他见了他们的面会拿出的那一套。」

「我对梅里深怀感激，」赫里克说。「没人知道见那些家长是怎么一回事。」

「梅里先生就知道，」艾米丽说。「我对他谈不上感激。我是压在一副人情重担底下直不起腰。而且他是个悲剧人物，缠得我不安。对梅里太太，我倒是健健康康地感激。对巴斯登小姐，我的感激完全是雇主家那种干脆利落的感激。对伯吉斯先生，我几乎更觉亲近些——他领受的注定是感激的反面，虽说我也始终不明白为什么。好了，我得去问梅里太太，看我们能不能留你们吃晚饭。想想尼古拉斯和我连请客人吃饭都先得求准许，还总能把这局应付得那么漂亮。」

「艾米丽这话说起来就没完，是不是？」赫里克说。「日复一日，年复一年，这道水从来不曾干过。」

===Original===
“It doesn’t seem that it could,” said Masson; “that Emily could ever be subject to age.”

“I always wonder if she had any youth,” said Bumpus, his eyes sweeping over Masson. “She seems somehow ageless, to have nothing to do with age.”

“You knew her when she was young,” said Herrick. “But you were young too, then. She was wonderfully like what she is now. No, I think in a sense she had no youth, just as in a way she will have no old age. She is of that type. A rare one.”

“I think the two omissions compensate for each other,” said Masson.

“I do not. I like the whole of experience,” said Bumpus.

===Chinese===
「好像也干不了，」马森说；「艾米丽好像根本不会受年龄的支配。」

「我一直怀疑她有没有过青春，」邦珀斯说，目光从马森身上扫过。「她不知怎的仿佛不老，跟年龄全不相干。」

「她年轻的时候你们是认识的，」赫里克说。「不过那时你们也年轻。她跟现在像得出奇。是啊，我想在某种意义上她没有过青春，正如在某种意义上她也不会有暮年。她是那一类人。罕见的一类。」

「我想这两样缺欠正好互相补偿，」马森说。

「我可不这么想。我要的是完整的阅历，」邦珀斯说。

===Original===
“I always wonder,” went on Herrick, in the tone of a man who kept back nothing of his heart, “if I should have been happier or unhappier without her. I am never bound by convention; and the question arises in the case of any intimate relationship, however good. We could all have moulded ourselves differently.”

“We can’t change the stuff in the mould,” said Masson.

“And Emily is a woman with a good deal of the man in her,” said Bumpus.

“Oh, I have had more to do with women than you two have,” said Herrick. “I am not as much off the common line. But most of my life’s relationships have been with men. But that is what I meant. I wonder what I should have been, if I had not had Emily. And, William, you are thinking, what would she have been. But I am never the slave of convention.”

“Don’t stop,” said Emily, returning. “I like talk that is unfit for me. It is having to get used to Nicholas, who is not the slave of convention, as he says. Fancy being able to say that truthfully!”

===Chinese===
「我总在想，」赫里克接着说，用的是一颗毫无保留的心所产生的口气，「若是没有她，我会更幸福些，还是更不幸福。我从不为俗套所缚；任何一种亲密的关系，无论多好，都会引出这个问题。我们谁都本可能把自己塑成另一个样子。」

「可模子里的料改不了，」马森说。

「再说艾米丽是个身上颇有些男子气的女人，」邦珀斯说。

「哦，我跟女人打的交道比你们两个多，」赫里克说。「我不像你们那么出格。可我此生的交往大半是与男人的。不过我的意思正是这个。我总想，若是没有艾米丽，我会成了什么样。还有，威廉，你此刻在想，那她会成了什么样。可我从不做俗套的奴隶。」

「别停啊，」艾米丽回来说。「我爱听不适合我听的话。谁叫我得习惯尼古拉斯呢，他自己说了，他是不做俗套的奴隶的。想想吧，这话居然能说得理直气壮！」

===Original===
“I believe in the interest of oneself, you know,” said Herrick, going on as if he had not heard his sister. “I take the deepest interest in myself, apart from egotism of personality, though I may have that. And I have no condemnation for egotists. I think they are often the higher type.”

“This is not the kind of unfitness I meant,” said Emily. “It does sound wicked, but not with a wickedness that I like.”

“I think on the whole they are not,” said Masson.

“You could not be an egotist, William, whether or no you wanted it,” said Bumpus.

“Surely everybody would want it,” said Emily. “I am sure it would be dreadful not to be one. Isn’t it, William?”

===Chinese===
「你们知道，我信的是对自身的兴趣，」赫里克说，接着往下讲，仿佛没听见妹妹的话。「我对自己怀有最深的兴趣，撇开个性上的自大不谈，虽说我也未必没有。我从不谴责自我主义者。我想他们往往是更高的一类。」

「我说的不适合不是这一种，」艾米丽说。「这听着确实邪乎，可不是我中意的那种邪乎。」

「我想总的来说他们并不是，」马森说。

「你当不成自我主义者，威廉，不管你想不想，」邦珀斯说。

「谁不想当啊，」艾米丽说。「我敢说当不上才可怕呢。是不是，威廉？」

===Original===
“Egotism is a gift, like anything else,” said Herrick.

“Then I grow prouder and prouder of you, darling,” said Emily. “An author and an egotist, and both of them such lovely things.”

“I suppose your book is a novel, Herrick? Mine is,” said Bumpus.

“Yes, yes. A short novel,” said Herrick. “I hold no brief for long books, as I say.”

“Real books coming out of our own heads!” said Bumpus. “And not just printed unkindness to other people’s.”

===Chinese===
「自我主义是一种禀赋，跟别的一样，」赫里克说。

「那我可越来越为你骄傲了，亲爱的，」艾米丽说。「又是作家，又是自我主义者，两样都这么可爱。」

「我猜你那本书是部小说吧，赫里克？我的是，」邦珀斯说。

「是啊，是啊。一部短小说，」赫里克说。「正如我方才说的，我并不为长书辩护。」

「从我们自己脑子里长出来的真正的书！」邦珀斯说。「不只是印出来的对别人书的刻薄话。」

===Original===
“My first original piece of writing!” said Herrick. “That I should have to say it at seventy! Not just what Richard has said. Ah, I have felt that. Not that I have not done good work in that way. And it all has to be done. But my first book. Probably my only book, though many would be surprised to hear me say so. I thrill like a youth at the thought of seeing it out.”

“Oh, so do I,” said Emily. “But not like a youth. Though I should love to do that once again. And it is better and safer at seventy. It will have to have the respect due to age, anyhow. But I am afraid Mr. Merry will think it is a story book. Perhaps we had better keep it from him.”

“He must know about it when it comes out,” said Masson.

“No, I don’t think Mr. Merry knows about books when they come out,” said Emily.

Masson and Bumpus returned to the college, entered Masson’s rooms, and sat for a time in silence.

===Chinese===
「我这头一件原创的文字！」赫里克说。「到了七十岁才说得出这句话！不只是跟在理查德后面说的。啊，这滋味我尝过了。倒不是说我那条路上没做过好事。那种事也都得有人做。可这是我的第一本书。多半也是唯一的一本，虽说许多人听见我这么说会吃惊。想到它问世，我激动得像个年轻人。」

「哦，我也是，」艾米丽说。「可不像个年轻人了。虽说我还真想再那样年轻一回。到七十岁上这样更稳妥也更安全。不管怎样，它总得领受一份对年岁的敬意。只是我怕梅里先生会把它当成一本故事书。也许我们最好瞒着他。」

「书出来的时候总得让他知道，」马森说。

「不，我看梅里先生根本不知道书什么时候出来，」艾米丽说。

马森和邦珀斯回到学院，进了马森的寓所，默默坐了一会儿。

===Original===
“Well, William,” said Bumpus, “I have protested that I have written a book. You must know that it is your part to seem to want to talk about it.”

“If you would like to talk of it with me,” said Masson. “I think that men who take only to science, cannot be on the point enough in these things to be bearable.”

“I suppose you will not read it,” said Bumpus. “Or if you do, will not tell me what you think of it.”

“Am﻿—am I of the sort to read it?” said Masson. “I hesitate, as I say, to inflict the alien touch.”

“It is this that thirty years has brought us to,” said Bumpus.

===Chinese===
「好了，威廉，」邦珀斯说，「我已经声明我写了一本书。你该如何道，你该演的角色是做出想谈谈它的样子。」

「要是你愿意跟我谈谈的话，」马森说。「我想只钻研科学的人，在这些事情上不够敏锐，怕是乏味得让人受不了。」

「我猜你不会去读它，」邦珀斯说。「或者读了，也不肯告诉我你觉得它怎么样。」

「我——我是读它的那类人么？」马森说。「我说过，我迟疑着，不肯施加外行的毒手。」

「三十年就让我们走到了这一步，」邦珀斯说。

===Original===
“I﻿—I think it is a good thing to come to, to keep to,” said Masson, his voice going high. “I think it is the worst thing about intimacy, that it may blunt every edge.”

“Yes, yes. Edges are the only thing,” said Bumpus. “It is odd about Herrick, isn’t it? His writing a book.”

“Odd, is it?” said Masson. “Oughtn’t he to have written before?”

“That does seem to me the alien touch,” said Bumpus.

“Well, perhaps it will hardly be a good book,” said Masson.

===Chinese===
「我——我想走到这一步是好事，守着它也是好事，」马森说，嗓音高了起来。「我想亲密最糟的地方，就是它会把每道锋刃都磨钝。」

「是啊，是啊。锋刃才是唯一要紧的，」邦珀斯说。「赫里克这事有点怪，是不是？他居然写起书来。」

「怪么？」马森说。「他不是早就该写了吗？」

「这话在我听来才是外行的毒手，」邦珀斯说。

「那么，它大概算不上一本好书，」马森说。

===Original===
Bumpus laughed.

“I should have said that Herrick knew too well for that. He has his hand on his name with great skill. ‘Oughtn’t he to have written?’ That is what he has done. I don’t follow. It is certainly odd how both he and I seem to have come to it suddenly. It is dwelling on the time when I sacrificed my other book, that has brought me to it. My mind has been on it lately. I don’t know why I should be sensitive about it. My feelings had nothing in them to be ashamed of. This book seems to start out of all that, somehow, to go on from it. Well, there is nothing between.”

“In that way,” said Masson. “You﻿—you feel deeply about the book, Richard?”

“Yes,” said Bumpus, leaning forward, looking small and tense and alive. “I have wanted to write all my life, felt it was this, it was mine to want. And it seemed not to come to me, after the early time when things happened as we know. There seems to be one book in a good many of us. And, of course, that one book of mine was mine no longer.”

“No, no,” said Masson, not raising his eyes.

===Chinese===
邦珀斯笑了。

「要我说，赫里克精明得很，坏不了事。他摆弄自己的名声极有手腕。『他不是早就该写了吗？』他做的正是这个。我跟不上了。他和我竟然都像是一下子走到这一步，确实蹊跷。正是对当年我舍掉另一本书那桩事的反复思量，把我带到了这一步。我近来心里一直搁着它。我不明白自己为什么对它那样敏感。我的感情里没有什么见不得人的东西。这本书不知怎的仿佛正是从那一切里出发，从那里接着走下去。唉，中间并没有隔着的。」

「这么说，」马森说。「你——你对这本书感受很深，理查德？」

「是的，」邦珀斯说着，身子前倾，显得瘦小、紧张而生气勃勃。「我这辈子一直想写作，觉得就是这件事，这份想望理应归我。可在早年那段我们知道的变故之后，它仿佛再不肯降临到我身上。我们中间似乎好些人都只有一本书。当然，我的那一本，已经不再是我的了。」

「是啊，是啊，」马森说，眼睛没有抬。

===Original===
“So this means much to me,” said Bumpus, looking at Masson.

“It﻿—it means then much to me,” said Masson. “Perhaps Herrick’s book is the one book in him that is in many of us.”

“No,” said Bumpus, laughing. “I have more than one in me, and Herrick has not one at all.”

“Then the book must explain itself,” said Masson.

===Chinese===
「所以这本书对我很要紧，」邦珀斯说，望着马森。

「那——那对我也很要紧，」马森说。「也许赫里克那本书，正是他身上那本我们许多人心里都有的书。」

「不，」邦珀斯笑着说。「我身上的书不止一本，赫里克身上一本也没有。」

「那么这本书只好自己解释自己了，」马森说。

===Original===
“I believe I am capable of any meanness,” said Bumpus suddenly, “short of actually cheating people of their own.”

“Is there much meanness short of some form of that,” said Masson.

“No, no, none that matters,” said Bumpus.

===Chinese===
「我相信什么卑鄙的事我都做得出来，」邦珀斯忽然说，「只是不去实际骗走别人自己的东西。」

「撇开这种那种的骗取，还剩多少卑鄙呢。」马森说。

「是啊，是啊，要紧的一种也没有，」邦珀斯说。

## III / 三

===Original===
The Reverend Peter Fletcher was a frail little elderly man, with a long, black beard, and a colourless face that carried a humorous kindliness. His wife, Theresa, was a large old woman, with fierce eyes looking out between a massive brow and chin. His sister, Miss Lydia Fletcher, was a clumsy-looking woman of sixty, with a broad, flat, benevolent face; and the Reverend Francis Fletcher, a nephew, was an oversized, youngish man, with solemn eyes.

“Say grace, Peter,” said Theresa, looking up from carving.

“I have just done so, my dear,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“Peter, you see the result of thirty years of your example,” said Bumpus, who was a cousin of Mr. Fletcher’s, and gave an impression of a faint Fletcher likeness embodied in a great difference.

“I think it is a beautiful result,” said Emily, who was a friend of Theresa’s. “Fancy just not noticing grace after thirty years!”

===Chinese===
彼得·弗莱彻牧师（the Reverend Peter Fletcher）是个瘦弱矮小的老人，蓄着一把长长的黑胡须，一张没有血色的脸上带着一种谐谑的慈祥。他的妻子特蕾莎（Theresa）是个高大的老妇人，一双凌厉的眼睛从宽重的眉骨与下颚之间望出来。他的妹妹莉迪亚·弗莱彻小姐（Miss Lydia Fletcher）是个六十岁、样子笨拙的女人，一张宽扁的仁善面孔；侄子弗朗西斯·弗莱彻牧师（the Reverend Francis Fletcher）则是个块头过大、还算年轻的人，眼神肃穆。

「做谢饭祷告，彼得，」特蕾莎说着，从切肉的手上抬起眼来。

「我刚才做过了，亲爱的，」弗莱彻先生说。

「彼得，这就是你以身作则三十年的结果，」邦珀斯说。他是弗莱彻先生的表弟，给人的印象是：一种淡淡的弗莱彻家的相像，落实在巨大的不同之上。

「我看这结果很美，」艾米丽说，她是特蕾莎的朋友。「想想吧，三十年了，祷告居然充耳不闻！」

===Original===
“Being so free from nervous hurry at the least,” said Bumpus.

“Oh, one does not offer oneself as an example to one’s elders and betters,” said Mr. Fletcher, giving his peculiar smile, in which he stretched his lips without parting them, so that his teeth were not displayed.

He was nine months younger than his wife.

“Ah, it is so difficult to have the control of oneself,” said Miss Lydia, in a mysterious undertone, with her hand over her mouth. “It seems that nothing is so small that we can do it without asking help.”

“Theresa does everything without help, doesn’t she?” said Bumpus.

===Chinese===
「至少是那样从容不迫，一点不慌，」邦珀斯说。

「哦，人总不至于把自己摆成长辈尊长的榜样，」弗莱彻先生说着，露出他那特有的微笑——嘴唇抿着拉开，并不张口，牙齿也就不露出来。

他比妻子小九个月。

「唉，管住自己真难哪，」莉迪亚小姐用一种神秘的低声说，手掩着嘴。「事情再小，似乎也非得求助不可。」

「特蕾莎做什么都不用人帮，不是吗？」邦珀斯说。

===Original===
“Of course nobody who does that, could do quite actually everything,” said Emily.

“No. Not without help, no. Without always asking help, perhaps,” murmured Miss Lydia. “For we can’t do things without help. No.”

“Is the farewell sermon ready, Peter?” said Theresa.

“A farewell sermon! I do hope you had help,” said Bumpus.

“A life of work, and extra work to round it off!” said Theresa.

===Chinese===
「当然，谁要是样样都自己来，也就做不到真正的样样，」艾米丽说。

「是啊。不用帮是不成的，是啊。也许只是不必事事求助，」莉迪亚小姐喃喃道。「因为我们做事离不开帮。是啊。」

「告别讲道准备好没有，彼得？」特蕾莎说。

「告别讲道！我真诚希望你得了助力，」邦珀斯说。

「操劳了一辈子，还要加一场操劳来收尾！」特蕾莎说。

===Original===
“Lyddie, is it ready?” said Mr. Fletcher.

“The sermon is not my province,” said Miss Lydia, firmly on the truth.

“But couldn’t it be with help?” said Emily. “Oughtn’t you to get particular help, being inside things as you are? Or is everything just coldly fair?”

Miss Lydia looked at the table.

“Aunt Lyddie is to do the work to the end, isn’t she, Uncle Peter?” said Francis.

===Chinese===
「莉迪，好了没有？」弗莱彻先生说。

「讲道不是我的分内事，」莉迪亚小姐说，牢牢站在事实一边。

「可加上帮手不就行了么？」艾米丽说。「您这样身在其中的人，难道不该得点特殊的帮助？还是说一切都冷冰冰地公平？」

莉迪亚小姐低头看桌子。

「莉迪姑妈是要把活儿干到底的，不是吗，彼得伯伯？」弗朗西斯说。

===Original===
“Yes, yes, surely,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“It is not a woman’s business to preach,” said Miss Lydia.

“Of course not,” said Emily. “I did not think of that. I didn’t mean I thought it was.”

“Is it done, dear?” said Theresa.

“I have mapped it out in outline,” said Mr. Fletcher, leaning back with his finger tips together. “I have still two or three matters to look up.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，是啊，当然，」弗莱彻先生说。

「讲道不是女人的本分，」莉迪亚小姐说。

「当然不是，」艾米丽说。「我没想到那上头去。我不是那个意思。」

「弄好了没有，亲爱的？」特蕾莎说。

「我已经搭好了大纲，」弗莱彻先生说，身子后靠，指尖对着指尖。「还有两三处要查一查。」

===Original===
“You are self-important, Peter,” said Bumpus.

“Well, with a farewell sermon, that is just being open and above deceit,” said Emily.

“I shouldn’t trouble about it,” said Theresa. “The people who will hear it have never troubled about you.”

“I should denounce them at the last,” said Bumpus. “No, I should not. I should be very subtle and aloof.”

“I do envy you for retiring,” said Emily. “Fancy, if Nicholas could give up the school!”

===Chinese===
「你架子不小，彼得，」邦珀斯说。

「唉，就一场告别讲道而言，这只是光明磊落、不弄虚虚，」艾米丽说。

「我才不为它操心呢，」特蕾莎说。「要听它的人可从没为你操过心。」

「换了我，临了要痛斥他们一场，」邦珀斯说。「不，我不会的。我会非常含蓄，非常超然。」

「我真羡慕你告老，」艾米丽说。「想想吧，要是尼古拉斯能把学校撂下！」

===Original===
“Ah, he is needed,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“Oh, he is not, Peter,” said Emily. “You should not have little ways of making yourself popular.”

“There is a great deal of ritualism in the town,” said Miss Lydia, rolling the *r*. “A great deal. It is so much we have to fight against. But we must be so thankful that we are allowed to do something.”

“The other side seems to be allowed to do more,” said Theresa. “I hope they are more thankful.”

“Lyddie,” said Bumpus, “have you been told about Theresa’s being made on purpose without charity, because of the double share of Peter, with whom she was to become one?”

===Chinese===
「啊，学校缺不了他，」弗莱彻先生说。

「哦，缺不了什么，彼得，」艾米丽说。「你可不该学这些讨人喜欢的小花招。」

「城里仪式派多得不得了，」莉迪亚小姐说，把那个 *r* 音滚了滚。「多得不得了。我们要对抗的就是这么多。不过人家还许我们做点事，我们就该感恩了。」

「人家那一派好像被许做的更多，」特蕾莎说。「但愿他们也更知恩。」

「莉迪，」邦珀斯说，「有人给你讲过吗——特蕾莎是特意造得没有慈心的，就因为彼得的份儿加了倍，而她要跟他合为一体？」

===Original===
“Ah, but these ritualists do harm. They do harm. They are not right. It is idolatrous,” said Miss Lydia, looking in front of her.

“The true reasons for the simpler service have possibly never even reached them,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“No, no, Peter; keep a hand on yourself,” said Bumpus. “That does not extend to us. We are more of a credit to you than that.”

“It is certainly time that Peter retired,” said Theresa.

“Where do your boys attend church?” said Miss Lydia to Emily, in a mysterious, piercing whisper.

===Chinese===
「唉，可这些仪式派实在有害。实在有害。他们不正派。那是偶像崇拜，」莉迪亚小姐说着，直望着前方。

「简化仪式的真正缘由，他们可能压根儿就没听说过，」弗莱彻先生说。

「不，不，彼得；管住你自己，」邦珀斯说。「那话可推广不到我们身上。我们给你增的光可比那多。」

「彼得是到告老的时候了，这是肯定的，」特蕾莎说。

「你们的孩子们上哪儿做礼拜？」莉迪亚小姐朝艾米丽用一种神秘而穿透的低声问。

===Original===
“My boys?” said Emily. “How nice of you! Because that is the impression we want to make. At the chapel of the college where Nicholas used to be. Richard’s college.”

“Merry arranged it as a reminder to people that Herrick was a Fellow there,” said Bumpus.

“You are very nice people to be employed by,” said Miss Lydia, her voice suggesting unworthiness in Mr. Merry.

“Yes, we try to be grateful,” said Emily. “It is so generous to be employed. Nicholas and I really have to shirk our part of it.”

“Mrs. Merry is a good religious woman?” said Miss Lydia, raising her eyes but not her head.

===Chinese===
「我的孩子们？」艾米丽说。「您多好！我们要造成的正是这个印象。在尼古拉斯从前所在学院的礼拜堂。理查德的学院。」

「这是梅里的安排，好提醒人们赫里克在那儿当过研究员，」邦珀斯说。

「受你们雇用可真好，」莉迪亚小姐说，话音里暗指梅里先生不配。

「是啊，我们尽量做到感恩，」艾米丽说。「受雇于人多么宽厚啊。尼古拉斯和我实在不得不把我们那份感激赖掉些。」

「梅里太太是位虔诚的好女人？」莉迪亚小姐说，抬起眼睛，头却没有抬。

===Original===
“Yes. That is what she is. You are wonderful at descriptions.”

“I am sure, Aunt Lyddie, that Miss Herrick’s and Mr. Herrick’s influence is everywhere in the school,” said Francis. “I have so often heard about it in the town.”

“I wonder if that is all right,” said Bumpus. “Are we quite sure what Merry wants about that?”

“Mr. Merry is very happy in having no place in his life for criticism at all,” said Miss Lydia.

“Very happy?” said Emily. “I think it would be unhappy and difficult. And we can’t take everything, and give what is less than nothing. And that is what criticism seems to be. Peter, you do make me so jealous, sitting there. I wish Nicholas had a beard, and a kinder expression.”

===Chinese===
「是的。她正是那样的人。您真会描摹人。」

「我敢说，莉迪姑妈，赫里克小姐和赫里克先生的影响遍及全校，」弗朗西斯说。「我在城里时常听人说起。」

「不知这样妥不妥，」邦珀斯说。「梅里对此是什么意思，我们弄得清楚吗？」

「梅里先生很自在，他生活里根本不留批评的位置，」莉迪亚小姐说。

「很自在？」艾米丽说。「我看那又苦又难。再说我们总不能什么都收下，回赠的东西却连没有都不如。而批评给人的正是这种东西。彼得，你坐在那儿真叫我嫉妒。我巴不得尼古拉斯也留把胡子，表情再和善些。」

===Original===
“Sixty-eight years behind him, and not a respectable grey hair to show for it!” said Miss Lydia.

Mr. Fletcher passed his hand down his beard.

“Wouldn’t vanity seem to you a feeling incongruous with your calling?” said Bumpus.

“It would lack, I fear, what may be regarded as its necessities of life,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“I wish I had said that. I mean, I wish I had thought of it,” said Emily. “I mean, I wish I had thought of it just as a cleverness.”

===Chinese===
「活了六十八年，竟拿不出一根体面的白头发作证！」莉迪亚小姐说。

弗莱彻先生一只手捋下胡须。

「依你看，虚荣心难道不是一种跟你的圣职不般配的情感么？」邦珀斯说。

「我怕它缺了那可以算作生活必需的东西，」弗莱彻先生说。

「这话我但愿是我说的。我是说，但愿是我想出来的，」艾米丽说。「我是说，但愿我单是作为一种机巧把它想出来。」

===Original===
“You see why he will not button his coat,” said Bumpus. “It would be taking thought for what he puts on, when he is provided as the lilies of the field.”

Francis looked grave.

Theresa rose and rang the bell.

“My dear,” said Mr. Fletcher, half rising himself, “why cannot you say when you want a thing done?”

“I do it and get back before anyone else is out of his seat,” said Theresa.

===Chinese===
「这下你明白他为什么不肯扣外衣扣子了，」邦珀斯说。「那是为穿什么操心，而他受的供养，就像野地里的百合花。」

弗朗西斯神色庄重起来。

特蕾莎起身摇铃。

「亲爱的，」弗莱彻先生自己也欠起身来，「你要人做事，怎么就不能说一声？」

「等别人还没离座，我做完已经回来了，」特蕾莎说。

===Original===
“It is unworthy of you to expect people to be prompt,” said Bumpus. “Such a cold, self-esteeming thing to be.”

“I am cold and self-esteeming,” said Theresa.

“It occurs to me,” said Francis, sinking back into his chair, “that it was just so with my mother. She used often to vex and grieve me, if it had been possible for me to be vexed or grieved by her, by doing for herself those little things that I should have liked to do for her. I used often to reason with her about it, but she always refused to hear reason.”

“I must just trot across to the post,” said Miss Lydia. “I have had to write to all my men, and tell them that my men’s class will not be held on Thursday. Dear souls, they will be so disappointed; but I could not help it, or I would indeed.”

“Let me go for you, Aunt Lyddie,” said Francis.

===Chinese===
「指望别人手脚麻利，这可有失你的身份，」邦珀斯说。「这么一个冷冰冰、自尊自大的东西。」

「我就是冷冰冰、自尊自大，」特蕾莎说。

「我忽然想到，」弗朗西斯说着，坐回椅子里，「我母亲从前正是这样。倘若她真能惹我恼、令我愁的话——她常自己去做那些我本想替她做的小事，惹得我又恼又愁。我常为这事跟她讲道理，可她从来不肯听道理。」

「我得赶紧跑一趟邮局，」莉迪亚小姐说。「我不得不给我们的男人们都写了信，告诉他们星期四的男人班不开了。亲爱的灵魂们，他们该多失望；可我没办法，有办法我还不么。」

「我替您去跑一趟吧，莉迪姑妈，」弗朗西斯说。

===Original===
“No, I am a person who does my own business. And this business is mine. I am so sad to disappoint my dear men things, who understand me so. I don’t often fail them. Not often. And I don’t often fail with them. I know I am different with women. I admit it. But men don’t often elude me. Not often.”

“I feel I do elude Lyddie,” said Emily. “I am always having proof that I am the average woman. And Nicholas has taught me to despise it.”

“She seems very pleased with not failing with men, and failing with women,” said Theresa. “It would be better not to fail with either.”

“No, if you think a minute, not so good,” said Emily. “Not so nice, anyhow.”

“I don’t think myself so good,” said Bumpus. “Much less good, of course.”

===Chinese===
「不，我这个人自己的事自己办。这件事是我的。让亲爱的人们失望，我难过极了，他们那么懂我。我不常让他们失望。不常。我也不常在他们那儿失手。我知道我对女人就不一样了。我承认。可男人们不常躲得开我。不常。」

「我就觉得我躲开了莉迪，」艾米丽说。「我总不断拿到证据，证明我是个平平常常的女人。尼古拉斯教会了我为此看不起自己。」

「跟男人不打折扣、跟女人尽打折扣，她好像还挺得意，」特蕾莎说。「两边都不打折扣才好。」

「不，你细想一分钟，就没那么好了，」艾米丽说。「至少不那么可人。」

「我也自认没那么好，」邦珀斯说。「当然差得远。」

===Original===
“It is something not to fail with one, my dear,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“I knew you would say that, Peter,” said Theresa. “You self-righteous, obvious old man. I did not say it was not.”

“Be patient with me, my dear. I shall be older and wiser presently.”

“You will be older and not wiser. At our age people get less wise.”

“Yes, that is true, my dear. I shall do my best for you, when the time comes for you to consult me generally.”

===Chinese===
「有一边不打折扣，也就不坏了，亲爱的，」弗莱彻先生说。

「我就知道你要这么说，彼得，」特蕾莎说。「你这自以为义、一眼见底的老头子。我可没说那不算数。」

「对我耐心些，亲爱的。过不了多久，我就会又老一些，又智慧一些。」

「你会老一些，却不会智慧一些。到了我们这年纪，人是越活越不智慧的。」

「是啊，这话不假，亲爱的。等你到了万事都要请教我的时候，我一定尽力为你效劳。」

===Original===
Mr. Fletcher’s eyes took on a look of grateful content, as the door shut on the women. He welcomed a man’s companionship, and his friendship with Bumpus gave to his days a hidden light.

“I have been longing to have a word with a woman,” said Emily, sitting down with Theresa. “Nicholas is so terribly a man. You must find that with Peter, too.”

“Sometimes,” said Theresa.

“Only sometimes?” said Emily. “Yes, that is true about Peter. Nicholas is always a man. Life is just labour and sorrow for us. Labour for Nicholas and sorrow for me. You wouldn’t think that Nicholas would labour. But lately he has. His way of writing this book is so like what an ordinary writer’s, a real writer’s way would be. He says it is ready in his head. Nicholas can’t be like that. He says it came suddenly to him, when he was sitting up with Mr. Crabbe. But he must have had it in his mind; and he has given no sign at all. And Nicholas ought to have given so many signs. It can’t mean anything. Do you think him aged lately?”

“No, no, darling,” said Theresa. “Particularly well and cheerful.”

===Chinese===
女眷们一出去，门一关上，弗莱彻先生的眼里便浮起一种感恩而知足的神色。他乐于有男性的陪伴，而他与邦珀斯的友谊给他岁月添了一道隐秘的光。

「我一直巴望跟一个女人说说话，」艾米丽说，挨着特蕾莎坐下。「尼古拉斯的男人气重得吓人。你跟彼得在一起，想必也有这个。」

「有时候，」特蕾莎说。

「只是有时候？」艾米丽说。「是啊，彼得确实是这样。尼古拉斯可是一贯的男人。生活对我们就是劳苦和愁烦。尼古拉斯劳苦，我愁烦。你不会想到尼古拉斯也会劳苦。可他近来劳苦了。他写这本书的样子，活脱是寻常作家——真正的作家——的样子。他说书在他脑子里已经成了。尼古拉斯不可能是那样的。他说它是守着克拉布先生的那晚忽然降临的。可它必定早在他心里；他却一点征兆也没有露过。尼古拉斯本该露过多少征兆啊。这不可能有什么名堂。你看他近来老了吗？」

「没有，没有，亲爱的，」特蕾莎说。「气色特别好，人也特别欢。」

===Original===
“That is being so pleased that he is going to be conspicuous and highly thought of,” said Emily. “He likes that much more even than the average man. In some ways Nicholas is built on a very large scale. I do hope it is all going to come off. I can hardly tell you how I hope it. He will be terribly shaken if it does not.”

“Well, why shouldn’t it?” said Theresa. “I wonder he hasn’t done it before.”

“So do I,” said Emily. “So does he. That is just it. At least, I don’t think I do, really. I shouldn’t have thought that Nicholas could write a book. Not a good book; not even just good enough.”

“Does your brother know what a good sister he has in you?” said Theresa.

“No,” said Emily. “He knows what a good brother I have in him. How I should have had to go on the streets, or even be a governess, without him.”

===Chinese===
「那是他想到自己就要出风头、受推崇，先乐上了，」艾米丽说。「这个他比寻常人还贪得多。尼古拉斯有些地方是按很大的规格造的。我真心盼着这事能成。我怎么盼的，几乎没法跟你说。要是不成，他会垮得厉害。」

「唉，为什么不成？」特蕾莎说。「我倒奇怪他以前没做过。」

「我也是，」艾米丽说。「他自己也奇怪。症结就在这儿。至少，说真的，我想我并不奇怪。我原本不会想到尼古拉斯写得出书。写不出好书；连勉强过得去的也写不出。」

「你哥哥可知道他有你这么个好妹妹？」特蕾莎说。

「不知道，」艾米丽说。「他知道的是他有他那么个好哥哥。没有他，我就得上街讨生活，去当家庭女教师都算好的。」

===Original===
“You had your own income, dear,” said Theresa.

“A hundred a year,” said Emily, “Nicholas is kind, and without a true dignity. He calls that on the streets.”

“Well, but there is the other side to it all.”

“Of course there is my side. But Nicholas doesn’t see that. He is very thorough. He gives all his attention to one side.”

“You are very devoted to him?” said Theresa.

===Chinese===
「你自己有进项啊，亲爱的，」特蕾莎说。

「一年一百镑，」艾米丽说，「尼古拉斯心是好心，只是没有真正的尊严。他把那叫作上街讨生活。」

「唉，可这事总有另一面。」

「当然有我这一面。可尼古拉斯看不见。他做事彻底得很。全部心思只给一面。」

「你对他一片深情？」特蕾莎说。

===Original===
“You know how I feel to him as well as I do myself. How utterly I see through him, and yet how necessary he is to me. And how pathetic. It will break my heart if this wretched book goes wrong.”

“Does he feel the same to you? As strongly, I mean?”

“No,” said Emily. “You know he does not feel strongly like that about anyone. He is rather glad he has me. But he has a feeling that without me he would not have kept a school, and would have been a real writer. I think it might break him up if anything were to happen to me. You know that means if I were to die.”

“He only gives about ten minutes a day to the school,” said Theresa.

“Well, he couldn’t give any more,” said Emily. “I did not know you were one of those people who talk about Nicholas’ ten minutes. And he doesn’t see the other side. I told you.”

===Chinese===
「你跟我一样清楚我对他是什么感情。我把他看得多么透，而他对我又是多么不可少。还有他多么可悯。这本倒霉的书要是有个闪失，我的心都要碎。」

「他对你也是这样？我是说，也这样强烈？」

「不，」艾米丽说。「你知道他对谁都没有那种强烈的感情。有我，他倒是相当满意的。可他隐隐觉得，若不是为了我，他就不至于守着一所学校，早成了真正的作家。我想我要是出了什么事，他也许会垮掉。你知道，这就是说我要是死了。」

「他一天只给学校十来分钟，」特蕾莎说。

「唉，再多他也拿不出来了，」艾米丽说。「我不知道你也是拿尼古拉斯那十分钟说事的人。他就是看不见另一面。我说过了。」

===Original===
“Have you pointed it out to him, dear?”

“You know that would not make him see it. Apart from the way he already sees it. And I believe it is good for him to feel himself a kind of hero. It holds him up from going down into old age.”

“What would he do if you married?”

“Live with me, I suppose. As a sort of upper husband. And look down on the other one.”

“My dear, if you are going to do it, it should be soon, shouldn’t it? And, as you say, your brother could live with you.”

===Chinese===
「你跟他点破过吗，亲爱的？」

「你知道点破了他也看不见。何况他已经有了他那种看法。再说，我觉得让他自觉是个英雄对他有好处。这才撑着他，不至于沉入老境。」

「你要是嫁了人，他怎么办？」

「跟我一起过呗，我猜。做个太上丈夫。再俯视另外那一个。」

「亲爱的，你要真打算这样，就该趁早，是不是？再说，照你自己说的，你哥哥也可以跟你一起过。」

===Original===
“You know I did not say it,” said Emily. “And as if Nicholas could really live with me, with somebody else taking some of my attention! You can’t be as unobservant as that. And if I can marry now, I can marry at any time. There is not much dependent on youth left at fifty. And William gets older at the same time. I don’t pretend I don’t understand you. I am not at all commonplace.”

“There is no one else, is there?” said Theresa.

“You should not actually talk to me as the average married woman thinks of a spinster.”

“Do you ever give him a chance?”

“Give William a chance? I see him every day.”

===Chinese===
「你知道我没说过这话，」艾米丽说。「再说尼古拉斯哪能真跟我一起过，让另外什么人分走我一部分心思！你不至于那么不长眼。我现在能嫁，几时都能嫁。到了五十岁，指望青春的余地可不多了。威廉也一样在变老。我不装作听不懂你的话。我一点也不平庸。」

「没有别的人吧？」特蕾莎说。

「你可不该真拿寻常已婚妇人的眼光看老姑娘那样跟我说事。」

「你可曾给过他机会？」

「给威廉机会？我天天见得到他。」

===Original===
“But alone, dear? So that he is a free man?”

“In the room that Nicholas and I share, with Dickie looking on. Where else could I see him? The boys’ basement dining-room wouldn’t be the right setting for William. But it wouldn’t make any difference. He couldn’t propose to me.”

“Why not?”

“Why, I should think he couldn’t. I haven’t thought about it. I should think it is one of the things he doesn’t do. We all have them.”

“But you could manage yourself, dear. People can,” said Theresa.

===Chinese===
「可单独见面呢，亲爱的？好让他做个自由身？」

「在尼古拉斯和我同用的那间屋里，迪基在旁边看着。不然我还能在哪儿见他？孩子们那间地下层饭厅可配不上威廉。不过在哪儿都一样。他不会向我求婚。」

「为什么不会？」

「嗐，我想他不会就是了。我没细想过。我看那是他不做的那些事之一。我们人人都有几件。」

「可你能安排得来，亲爱的。人是能的，」特蕾莎说。

===Original===
“Yes, of course they can. I’ve noticed that. And he would accept me, I am sure. I know he would spare me embarrassment. Dear William!”

“But he wants to marry you, doesn’t he?”

“As much as he can want to marry anyone. Anyone who is a woman. And that is not very much.”

“Oh dear! These dons and people!” said Theresa.

“Yes, it is something of that way. I knew you knew all the time. I might tell you it is that way with me, too. But I shall not tell you any more. Especially as you are ordinary and know it all. I understand now why people sometimes murder people they are known to be fond of. They ought to murder them.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，人当然是能的。这我注意到了。他也肯定会接受我。我知道他会不让我难堪。亲爱的威廉！」

「可他是想娶你的，不是吗？」

「以他对任何人能有的想望而言，是想娶的。对任何一个身为女人的人。而那也多不到哪儿去。」

「天哪！这些学究先生们！」特蕾莎说。

「是啊，多少是那么回事。我就知道你早明白了。我本可以告诉你，我自己也是那么回事。可我不再多讲了。尤其你又是寻常人，什么都懂。我现在明白了，为什么人们有时会杀了那些众所周知他们心爱的人。真该杀了他们。」

===Original===
“Suppose you outlive your brother, dear?”

“Suppose I outlive him! Why of course I shall outlive him. I am twenty years younger than he is; so I mean to live twenty years after him. Don’t you think a woman ought to live, if there isn’t a man making use of her?”

“I thought you might not find things worth while without him. You said he would break up without you. I shouldn’t like to live forever, myself.”

“Yes, so I expect he would,” said Emily. “But then I join him in living for himself. He doesn’t join me in anything like that. I should love to live forever. I don’t wonder that religious people, who can plan things, arrange it like that.”

“No; but you might not miss him the less for that,” said Theresa. “We miss what we give most to, the most.”

===Chinese===
「要是你比你哥哥活得久呢，亲爱的？」

「什么要是！我当然会活得比他久。我比他年轻二十岁；所以我打算比他多活二十年。要是没有男人在使唤她了，你不觉得女人更该活着吗？」

「我还以为没有他，你会觉得事事没了意思。你说没有你他会垮。要叫我自己，可不想永远活下去。」

「是啊，我想他会的，」艾米丽说。「可我也陪他一起为自己活。这样的事他可不陪我。我倒愿意永远活下去。宗教里的人能把诸事安排停当，安排成那样，我不奇怪。」

「不；可你未必就因此少想他些，」特蕾莎说。「我们最想念的，是我们付出最多的。」

===Original===
“I do not,” said Emily. “I should miss him the less for that. But I should miss him. That is why I want this book to come off, so as to give him a new start of life. And I should live in a nice little house, if he died. Mr. Merry would have the school, and make me an allowance. I might have two sitting-rooms, and I should get so muddled between them. Where could William propose to me then? Though of course the school might not pay without Nicholas. People do admire him so for giving no time to it. It was so clever of him to think of it. Anyone else might have thought it would pay better to give all his time to it.”

“Darby and Joan gossiping together in the dark!” said Miss Lydia. “Gossiping and gossiping away in the dark.”

“Well, the dark can soon be remedied,” said Theresa.

“Yes, yes, it is not incurable,” said Miss Lydia, feeling for matches as one wont and willing to do all to be done. “It is not incurable, that is one thing. Not like the dark in the room I have just been in, a room under some stairs, with no light, and no fire, and some little children. Oh, dear! Something must be done. It must be done. How thankful we ought to be!”

“With a little less, we should be in the dark and cold too,” said Theresa. “You could find people better for preaching thankfulness to.”

===Chinese===
