「我可不，」艾米丽说。「正因如此我才会少想他些。可我总会想他的。所以我要这本书成，好给他一个新的起头。他要是死了，我就住进一所漂亮的小房子。学校归梅里先生，他会给我一笔津贴。我兴许有两间起居室，我会在两间之间犯迷糊。那时候威廉上哪儿向我求婚呢？虽说学校没了尼古拉斯未必还赚钱。人们就为他一点时间不给学校那么佩服他。他能想到这一着真是聪明。换了别人，兴许还以为把全部时间搭进去更赚钱呢。」

「达比和琼在黑暗里一起说体己话呢！」莉迪亚小姐说。「黑暗里说呀说呀，说个没完。」

「唉，黑马上就能治，」特蕾莎说。

「是啊，是啊，这病不是不治之症，」莉迪亚小姐说着，摸索火柴，一副惯于并且甘愿事事亲办的样子。「不是不治之症，这是一桩。不像我方才待的那间屋里的黑，楼梯底下一间屋，没有灯，没有火，还有几个小孩子。哦，天哪！总得想个办法。非想不可。我们该多么感恩啊！」

「家底再薄一点，我们照样又黑又冷，」特蕾莎说。「要宣讲感恩，你找得着更合适的人。」

===Original===
“Oh, no. For some people never know how nice it is to help,” said Miss Lydia. “We ought to be so sorry for them. Because it is so nice. It is so nice.”

“Perhaps the people are pretending distress out of kindness to you,” said Theresa.

“No. People are not so difficult in their kindness,” said Emily. “And they wouldn’t guess it was nice.”

“Out of kindness to themselves, then,” said Theresa.

“No, no. Not in this case. No,” said Miss Lydia. “The man has struggled to get work until he is hopeless, just to get work, just that, poor soul! Hopeless and distrustful of everything. He hardly trusted me at first. But he does now, dear fellow. Dear fellow, how he does!”

===Chinese===
「哦，不。有些人永远不知道帮人是多么好的事，」莉迪亚小姐说。「我们该为他们难过。因为那太好了。太好了。」

「也许那些人是出于对你的好心，才装出愁苦来的，」特蕾莎说。

「不会。人们的好心没有那么曲折，」艾米丽说。「再说他们也猜不到那是好事。」

「那就是出于对他们自己的好心，」特蕾莎说。

「不，不。这一桩不是。不是，」莉迪亚小姐说。「那个人为了找活儿干，挣扎到彻底绝望，就为了找活儿干，就为这个，可怜的灵魂！绝望了，对什么都不信。起先他几乎不信我。可他如今信了，亲爱的人。亲爱的人，他信得多厉害！」

===Original===
Miss Lydia went to her desk, and stood with her knuckles on it, her eyes looking into space. The matter seemed not long without light for her; for she hastened towards the door.

“What a good soul she is!” said Mr. Fletcher, smiling, as he came in from seeing Bumpus off in the hall.

“A soul who ought to be good,” said Theresa. “No family, and an income of her own! She could not spend every penny she has on herself.”

“She could not, but there are people who could, my dear.”

“I could,” said Emily. “I do.”

===Chinese===
莉迪亚小姐走到书桌前，指节抵着桌面站着，眼睛望进虚空里。这事在她那里似乎没多久就见了光；只见她匆匆朝门口走去。

「她心肠多好啊！」弗莱彻先生笑着说，刚从门厅送走邦珀斯回来。

「心肠好是应该的，」特蕾莎说。「没有家累，自己又有进项！她想把钱全花在自己身上都花不完。」

「是花不完，可有人花得完，亲爱的。」

「我就花得完，」艾米丽说。「我也真这么花。」

===Original===
“Well, it is not for us to admire this dropping of driblets,” said Theresa. “We had half we had swallowed at one draught. That was a thing, if you want one.”

“I wish I had the use of vigorous metaphor,” said Emily.

“My dear, we did not intend to give it,” said Mr. Fletcher, who had lent his inheritance, and failed to recover it.

“No. And so we had all the giving and no gratitude. That would not suit Lydia.”

“You know,” said Emily, “I hate to say it, but I believe it would not suit her as badly as most of us. But very badly, I hope, of course.”

===Chinese===
「唉，这种一滴一滴的施舍轮不到我们来佩服，」特蕾莎说。「我们那份家产，一半一口气就吞没了。要找件事，这才算件事。」

「我真想有本事使这么生猛的比喻，」艾米丽说。

「亲爱的，我们当初可没打算送出这个，」弗莱彻先生说，他借出去了自己继承的遗产，再没能收回来。

「是啊。于是我们给是给足了，感激却没捞着。那可不合莉迪亚的意。」

「你知道，」艾米丽说，「这话我不愿出口，可我相信她受这个，还不至于像我们大多数人那么受不住。当然，我希望还是很受不住。」

===Original===
“It did not suit us, did it, Theresa?” said Mr. Fletcher. “I fear we murmured. I fear we did.”

“How conceited you are! Of course you did,” said Emily. “I wonder if Lydia would not have spoken about it. If so, what a good thing she did not lose it! We should have had to admire her.”

“It is a good idea to leave debts unpaid when you die,” said Theresa.

“Very good. I will tell Nicholas about it,” said Emily. “He very likely has not thought of it. He is really much more honest than people think. And he does not think he is going to die. And of course he is not.”

“My dear, he felt it more than we did,” said Mr. Fletcher to his wife. “And we have been a happy old pair without it. Though I don’t know why I should call myself old, except that I like to be coupled with you, my dear.”

===Chinese===
「我们就受不住，是不是，特蕾莎？」弗莱彻先生说。「我怕我们是抱怨过的。我怕真是这样。」

「你们多自命不凡！当然抱怨过，」艾米丽说。「不知莉迪亚会不会把这事故在嘴上。若真是，那笔钱她没丢可真是好事！我们就不得不佩服她了。」

「死的时候留下一屁股债不还，倒是个好主意，」特蕾莎说。

「好得很。我要把这话告诉尼古拉斯，」艾米丽说。「他多半还没想到过。他其实比人们想的诚实得多。他也不觉得自己会死。他当然不会。」

「亲爱的，这事他比我们感受得深，」弗莱彻先生对妻子说。「没了那笔钱，我们也做了一对快活的老伴。虽然我不知道凭什么把自己叫老，不过是喜欢跟你配成对罢了，亲爱的。」

===Original===
“I do wish I were not a spinster,” said Emily. “I retract all I have said.”

“Of course, Lydia flirts with her men,” said Theresa. “She may not know it, but she does.”

“I wish I could do difficult things without knowing it,” said Emily. “I always know, when I do them, so clearly and conceitedly.”

“Well, that kind of thing must be at the bottom of most things,” said Mr. Fletcher. “It may as well be put to a good purpose, whether or not people know it.”

“Peter, you really are rather like other people sometimes,” said Emily, “though I don’t like to belittle you before Theresa.”

===Chinese===
「我真希望自己不是老姑娘，」艾米丽说。「我说过的话一律收回。」

「当然啦，莉迪亚跟她的男人们调情，」特蕾莎说。「她自己也许不知道，可她是在调。」

「我巴不得也能不知不觉地做成难事，」艾米丽说。「我一做难事，自己总是清清楚楚、自鸣得意地知道。」

「唉，多数事情的根底里，怕都免不了这种东西，」弗莱彻先生说。「不管人们自己知不知道，用在正经用途上也就罢了。」

「彼得，你有时候还真跟旁人差不多，」艾米丽说，「虽说我不愿当着特蕾莎贬低你。」

===Original===
“Emily, you are sometimes severe,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“And that is like other people, isn’t it?” said Emily. “But why should you want not to be like other people, when you are a good man?”

“I do not want it,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“Then why am I severe?” said Emily. “But you are right not to let us speak wickedly about Lydia. It must be terrible to do good.”

===Chinese===
「艾米丽，你有时候太刻薄，」弗莱彻先生说。

「这倒跟旁人一样，不是吗？」艾米丽说。「可你既然是个好人，为什么还要跟旁人不一样呢？」

「我并不要这样，」弗莱彻先生说。

「那我干吗刻薄？」艾米丽说。「不过你不让我们说莉迪亚的坏话是对的。行善一定是件可怕的事。」

## IV / 四

===Original===
“Now, I don’t know how it is,” said Mr. Merry, “but you all look half-dressed, and in a state of slovenliness, somehow. I can’t understand how it is that my boys cannot manage to look like gentlemen. Now, whatever is it so scrappy and untidy about you all?”

“We are to dress again, sir, presently,” said a boy; “before the prize-giving!”

“Dress again, presently, before the prize-giving! Dress again, presently, before the prize-giving! Dress again presently! And so it is not worth while to come down looking like gentlemen, though there are five or six hours before the prize-giving begins! Have any of you washed this morning, pray? Have you washed, Johnson? Have any of you said your prayers? Or have you put them off, till you shall be in church in four days’ time? I have never heard of such a thing.”

“Miss Basden told us to﻿—to do those things, and then not very much. We are to change all our things again presently. Miss Basden said﻿—”

“Miss Basden said! Miss Basden said! You are a lot of boys to require a lady to come and say that kind of thing to you! Have you no gentlemanly sense of decency? Have you no self-respect? Have you no﻿ ﻿… ? Oh, I have no patience to talk to you. I cannot understand it. I cannot. When I was your age﻿ ﻿… But get to your books, and let me hear no more of this dressing again presently because of the prize-giving. I cannot put up with it.”

===Chinese===
「唉，我也说不上是怎么了，」梅里先生说，「可你们一个个都像穿了一半衣裳，一副邋遢相。我就不明白，我的孩子们怎么就拾掇不成个绅士样。唉，你们身上这股零碎劲儿、这股乱劲儿，到底是怎么回事？」

「我们等一会儿还要再换装，先生，」一个孩子说；「在发奖典礼之前！」

「等一会儿再换装，在发奖典礼之前！等一会儿再换装，在发奖典礼之前！等一会儿再换装！这么说，离发奖典礼还有五六个钟头，就不值得像绅士样地下楼来了！请问你们今早有人洗漱了吗？你洗了吗，约翰逊？你们有人做过祷告没有？还是把它推迟了，等四天之后进了教堂再说？这种事我闻所未闻。」

「巴斯登小姐嘱咐我们——嘱咐我们做那些事，随后别的不多做。我们等一会儿要从头到脚再换一遍。巴斯登小姐说——」

「巴斯登小姐说！巴斯登小姐说！你们这帮孩子，竟要劳一位女士来跟你们说这种话！你们有没有绅士的体统？有没有自尊？有没有……？哦，我没耐心跟你们说了。我不明白。不明白。我像你们这么大的时候……罢了，去做功课，什么等一会儿再换装、因为发奖典礼的话，别再让我听见。我不能容忍。」

===Original===
A maid appeared with a request from Mrs. Merry, that the boys would go down to breakfast directly they heard the gong, as there was no time to spare that morning.

“Yes! Oh; what, Fanny? Directly they hear the gong? Thank you, Fanny, very much. Boys, you go down to breakfast directly you hear the gong. So don’t stay behind to finish what you are doing; and to let me go out first, as I am forty years older than you, and your schoolmaster. I suppose Mother thought you would miss out hearing the gong this morning, as you have missed out most other things. And there is the gong! So rush down; and go stampeding like a herd of tatterdemalions who have never been to school, instead of gentlemen spending their lives in one.”

Mrs. Merry was sitting before an empty tray, looking as if she had given up hope, except in smoothing her hair and glancing at the clock.

“Now, Mother, now, it’s all right, Mother,” said Mr. Merry, in an almost imploring tone. “You are managing it all as well as it can possibly be managed. You know you are, Mother.”

“But it is not all right, dear. The cups cannot be done without. The boys have to have their breakfast as usual,” said Mrs. Merry, almost implying that it would be only reasonable to waive this material start to the day.

===Chinese===
一个女仆进来，带来梅里太太的话：孩子们一听见锣响就下楼吃早饭，那天早上工夫一点也耽搁不起。

「好！哦；什么，范妮？一听见锣响就下楼？谢谢你，范妮，多谢了。孩子们，你们一听见锣响就下楼吃早饭。所以别磨蹭着把手头的做完；还得让我先出去，因为我比你们大四十岁，又是你们的校长。我猜妈妈是怕你们今早连锣也听漏了，旁的事你们差不多都听漏了。锣响了！冲下去吧；撒开蹄子狂奔吧，活像一群没进过学堂的叫花子，全不像一辈子待在学堂里的绅士。」

梅里太太坐在空茶盘前，那神情仿佛一切都不抱指望了，只除了一边抚平头发、一边瞟着时钟。

「好了，妈妈，好了，没事的，妈妈，」梅里先生说，口吻近乎哀求。「这一切您张罗得再好不过了。您自己知道，妈妈。」

「可并不是没事，亲爱的。茶杯万万少不得。孩子们总得照常吃早饭，」梅里太太说，语气几乎在暗示，把这一天开头的这一顿免了才在情理之中。

===Original===
“Oh, yes; ring the bell, Johnson,” said Mr. Merry, avoiding looking at his wife. “Ring the bell, Johnson, will you, please? Thank you, Johnson, very much. Oh, Miss Basden, good morning, Miss Basden. We were getting to feel all of a muddle without you.”

“You will have to feel all of a muddle again, very soon, then, I am afraid, Mr. Merry. I have come in only to rush away again. Oh, the cups! I could not be there for once. No, Mrs. Merry, I insist upon your not getting up. Mr. Merry, will you please forbid Mrs. Merry to rise?”

“Boys, go out and get your own cups for your own breakfast! Get up, and wait upon yourselves. When I was a boy, there was none of this sitting about of boys to be waited on. Oh, Fanny! Here you are, Fanny. The cups, Fanny, please. They have been forgotten somehow, in the bustle about everything today, you know. It is all right, Fanny. Thank you, Fanny, very much. Boys, be still, and don’t first sit about to have everything done for you, and then when it is being done, begin to make a fuss. Can’t you see that Fanny is getting the cups for you now? Oh, you are a set of boys! Now, Mother, have a cup of tea, and let your mind rest, while you have your breakfast. Miss Basden, you do the same.”

“It is more pouring out three or four dozen cups of tea for other people, than having one cup myself, that I have to think about.”

“Yes, Mother, yes,” said Mr. Merry, looking guiltily at the array of cups. “You are always looking after three or four dozen people, it seems to me. But don’t give up heart, Mother, you know. All days are not like today, you know. Why, Mr. Burgess! How are you, Mr. Burgess? Why, you look as fresh as if you were to have a prize. Ah! Anything out of the common is a great thing for you, isn’t it, Mr. Burgess?”

===Chinese===
「哦，对了；按铃，约翰逊，」梅里先生说，避开妻子的目光。「按铃，约翰逊，劳驾了？谢谢你，约翰逊，多谢了。哦，巴斯登小姐，早上好，巴斯登小姐。没有您，我们都要乱成一锅粥了。」

「那恐怕您很快又要乱成一锅粥了，梅里先生。我进来只为马上再走。哦，茶杯！我难得有一回赶不上。不，梅里太太，我坚决不许您起身。梅里先生，请您禁止梅里太太起来，好不好？」

「孩子们，出去自己拿自己的茶杯，吃自己的早饭！起来，自己伺候自己。我小时候，可没有孩子这样坐着等人伺候的。哦，范妮！你来了，范妮。茶杯，范妮，劳驾。今天事情一乱，不知怎么就忘了，你知道的。没事的，范妮。谢谢你，范妮，多谢了。孩子们，安分些，别先是坐着样样等人伺候，等人伺候上了又闹腾起来。你们看不见范妮这就去给你们拿茶杯吗？哦，你们这帮孩子！好了，妈妈，喝杯茶，趁吃早饭的工夫让心歇一歇。巴斯登小姐，您也一样。」

「我挂心的倒不是自己那一杯，是给旁人斟那三四十杯茶。」

「是啊，妈妈，是啊，」梅里先生说，心虚地看着那一排茶杯。「我看您呀，永远在照料三四打人。可您别灰心，妈妈。日子不都是今天这样的。哎呀，伯吉斯先生！您好吗，伯吉斯先生？瞧您容光焕发，像要领奖似的。啊！但凡有点不寻常的事，对您都是件大事，是不是，伯吉斯先生？」

===Original===
“Well, it depends upon how much it has to do with me, Mr. Merry,” said Mr. Burgess, who had come in brighter for the break in routine, but was already at his level. “It sounds rather a confession, but I am afraid I had not thought of the boys’ prize-giving. It was very self-centred of me.”

“It has brought you down in time, Mr. Burgess, at any rate,” said Miss Basden, draining her cup on her feet.

“I had not thought of it, Miss Basden. But I am glad, if I have even unconsciously honoured the day.”

“Ah, Mr. Burgess, it would not bring you down earlier, would it?”

“It appears that it has, Mr. Merry. But it was an office performed for me quite without my own participation, as I say; though it seems these things are to be settled for me.”

===Chinese===
「唉，那要看它跟我的关系有多大，梅里先生，」伯吉斯先生说，他因日常例行被打断而神采奕奕地进来，不过已经恢复了他素常的水位。「说来像是自供，我恐怕根本没想到孩子们的发奖典礼。我太以自我为中心了。」

「不管怎么说，它总算把您及时带了回来，伯吉斯先生，」巴斯登小姐说着，站着饮尽了自己那杯。

「我是没想到它，巴斯登小姐。不过我高兴，哪怕是无意识地为这个日子增了光。」

「啊，伯吉斯先生，它可不会把您提早带回来，是吧？」

「看样子它已经办到了，梅里先生。可如我方才所说，那是别人替我履行的职责，全然没有我自己参与；虽说这些事看来都要别人替我定夺。」

===Original===
“Boys, now, will you all be in your rooms by half-past eleven?” said Miss Basden, in a sudden, aloof tone, from the door. “By half-past eleven; so that you can have done all you have to do, by twelve, when I shall come round and see that you are properly ready. Do you understand?”

“Can’t any of you speak?” said Mr. Merry. “Can’t you do something else than sit, when a lady makes kind proposals of this sort to you? What do any of you ever do for Miss Basden, that she should go round, when she has so much to do, and see that you are all ready? Now, thank Miss Basden; and say that, of course, you will be ready to the moment.”

“Fanny,” said Mrs. Merry, “will you remember that I shall want you all down in the basement this morning? So none of you are to be upstairs at all, after the rooms are done. Miss Herrick is answering the front door bell.”

“What, Mother?” said Mr. Merry, leaning to his wife. “Miss Herrick, Mother? Miss Herrick answering the front door bell! Why, is that necessary, Mother? Does Mr. Herrick know about that?”

“Quite necessary, dear; or I should not have arranged it.”

===Chinese===
「孩子们，你们十一点半都能回到自己房间吗？」巴斯登小姐从门口用一种突然的、冷淡的声调说。「十一点半；这样十二点前你们就能把该做的做完，我到时候挨个去看，看你们是否齐齐整整。听明白了吗？」

「你们没一个会开口的吗？」梅里先生说。「一位女士向你们提出这样一番好意，你们除了干坐着就不能干点别的？你们谁为巴斯登小姐做过什么，值得她那么多事缠身还要挨个去看你们齐整了没有？来，谢谢巴斯登小姐；还要说，你们当然会一分不差地准备妥当。」

「范妮，」梅里太太说，「记住，今早我要你们全都待在地下层。房间收拾完之后，谁也不许在楼上。赫里克小姐在应前门的门铃。」

「什么，妈妈？」梅里先生朝妻子俯过身去。「赫里克小姐，妈妈？赫里克小姐去应前门的门铃！唉，这有这个必要吗，妈妈？赫里克先生知道这事吗？」

「很有必要，亲爱的；不然我也不会这样安排。」

===Original===
“Yes, but Mother, Miss Herrick! Miss Herrick, you know! Why, I should not have thought that﻿ ﻿… I mean, is that all right, Mother?”

“Quite, dear. It is just a little arrangement between Miss Herrick and me.”

“Yes, Mother,” said Mr. Merry, with compliment in his tone towards his wife’s relation with the sister of the head. “But, you know, Mother! Well, wasn’t there any way at all out of it but that?”

“None at all, dear. Unless you can spare me one of the boys, to sit in the hall all the morning and answer the bell?”

“Oh, well, Mother! I don’t mean that they wouldn’t be a great deal more useful, sitting in the hall, and answering the bell, than doing anything they will be doing. For what will be the good of teaching them this morning, sitting patiently and trying to teach them, half dressed as they are, and their heads full of every kind of thing but what they ought to be full of? But you know, Mother”﻿—Mr. Merry spoke low﻿—“the parents, you know! If it got to them, all about being used, and missing a morning’s work, you know! So I don’t think, Mother﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「是，可是妈妈，赫里克小姐呀！赫里克小姐，您知道的！唉，我真是想不到……我是说，这妥当吗，妈妈？」

「很妥当，亲爱的。不过是赫里克小姐和我之间的一点小安排。」

「是，妈妈，」梅里先生说，对妻子与校长妹妹的这层关系，话音里带着恭维。「可是，您知道的，妈妈！唉，就真的没有别的办法，非这样不可吗？」

「一点办法没有，亲爱的。除非您能匀我一个孩子，整个上午坐在门厅里应铃？」

「哦，哎，妈妈！我不是说他们坐在门厅里应铃，不比他们要干的那些事有用得多。今早教他们有什么用呢？耐着性子教，可他们衣裳穿一半，脑袋里塞满了种种杂念，独独没有该装的东西。可是您知道的，妈妈」——梅里先生压低了声音——「家长们，您知道的！要是传到他们耳朵里，说孩子被派了用场、耽误了一早晨功课，您知道的！所以我想，妈妈……」

===Original===
“Oh, of course I didn’t really mean it, dear!” said Mrs. Merry, half laughing. “And a boy would not be any good to me, really.”

“No. A boy would not be any good to you, really! A boy would not be of any good to you! Oh, wouldn’t he? I am sure he would not. Now there is a thing to get about, about you all! Here is Mother, kind as she is, having to say that!”

“You might stay with the boys until they have finished their breakfast, dear,” said Mrs. Merry, speaking with her hand on the edge of the table; “and then get them out as soon as possible. We are not going to have prayers this morning, so that the maids can get in to clear the room.”

Mr. Merry waited with his eyes on the door, until it closed behind his wife.

“Not going to have prayers this morning! Oh! So you are to miss that out, are you, besides all the other things you have missed out today? It seems that the only things you are to have today, are meals and prizes. I don’t wonder that the custom of giving prizes is getting to be talked against. I don’t wonder. And I tell you, there is one thing you will have this morning, and that is your work. So get up, and get out, whether you have finished your breakfast or not. ‘Stay with the boys until they have finished their breakfast!’ Am I a nursemaid, or what? Why, Mother is all put about this morning. So get out, and get your walk, and come back prepared to give your mind to your books. Do you hear me?”

===Chinese===
「哦，我当然不是当真的，亲爱的！」梅里太太半笑着说。「再说一个孩子对我其实也帮不上忙。」

「是啊。一个孩子帮不上您的忙，真的！一个孩子对您派不上用场！哦，派得上吗？我敢说派不上。瞧瞧你们大家落的这个名声！妈妈这么心善的人，竟不得不说出这种话！」

「你可以陪孩子们吃完早饭，亲爱的，」梅里太太说，一只手搭在桌沿上；「然后尽快带他们出去。今早我们不做祷告了，好让女仆们进来收拾屋子。」

梅里先生眼睛盯着门口，直到门在妻子身后关上。

「今早不做祷告！哦！今天漏掉的事还不够多，连这个也要漏掉？看来你们今天剩下的只有吃饭和领奖了。难怪如今发奖这个风俗越来越招人议论。难怪。我告诉你们，今早有一样东西你们是跑不掉的，那就是功课。所以都起来，出去，早饭吃完没吃完都一样。『陪孩子们吃完早饭！』我是个保姆吗，还是什么？唉，妈妈今早全乱了方寸。出去吧，散步去，回来就把心思收拢到书本上。听见没有？」

===Original===
“The end of breakfast is another thing that we can miss out today,” said Mr. Burgess, implying personal indifference.

“Oh, well, Mr. Burgess,” said Mr. Merry, his eyes on the departing flock of boys; “oh, well, the boys needn’t hurry you. But ‘stay with the boys until they have finished their breakfast!’ Why, I shouldn’t have dreamt of asking you to do it, Mr. Burgess.”

Mr. Burgess followed his pupils.

“Fanny,” said Mr. Merry, “this about Miss Herrick’s answering the bell? Why, that is all right, of course? There isn’t any way out of it? There isn’t any one of you who could just run upstairs, you know, if you should hear the bell?”

“Well, no, I am afraid not, sir. Mrs. Merry’s orders are, that we are to keep downstairs this morning. There is a lot to be done down there.”

===Chinese===
「早饭的收尾，是我们今天可以漏掉的又一件事，」伯吉斯先生说，暗含着个人的漠然。

「哦，哎，伯吉斯先生，」梅里先生说，眼睛望着那群鱼贯而出的孩子；「哦，哎，孩子们犯不着催您。可是『陪孩子们吃完早饭』！唉，我做梦也不会想到请您来做这个，伯吉斯先生。」

伯吉斯先生跟着他的学生们去了。

「范妮，」梅里先生说，「赫里克小姐应铃这回事？唉，那自然没问题是吧？就没有别的法子？你们中间就没有一个人，听见铃响能跑上楼来一趟，你知道的？」

「这个，怕是没有，先生。梅里太太吩咐了，今早我们一律待在楼下。楼下的活儿多着呢。」

===Original===
“Yes, Fanny, of course. Yes, Fanny. Thank you, Fanny, very much.”

Mr. Merry went up to the hall, and catching sight of Emily, quickened his steps to overtake her.

“Why, Miss Herrick, here is our great day. Our great day for the lads, when we all work together, as you and Mr. Herrick are so kind to us. And, Miss Herrick, you are so very kind to us. I hope there isn’t any little thing you are doing for us today that will be a nuisance to you, you know?”

“Oh, no. Nicholas and I are just coming in, in our most beautiful clothes, when the people come. What could be nicer for us? And I am opening the front door, as we can hear the bell. But as the tradesmen go to the back, and no visitors come in the morning, I don’t know whom I am to open it to. Unless to Mr. Burgess and the boys. That would sound good work, if I just said the number of people.”

“Yes, Miss Herrick,” said Mr. Merry, making way rather hurriedly for Emily. “Yes, Miss Herrick, very good and kind to us all. Oh, Mr. Burgess! So you are not gone, Mr. Burgess. Then make sure that you have your latch key, Mr. Burgess, will you? Because there is a good deal of running about on people today, you know.”

===Chinese===
「是，范妮，当然。是，范妮。谢谢你，范妮，多谢了。」

梅里先生上楼到了门厅，一眼瞥见艾米丽，便加快脚步追上她。

「哎呀，赫里克小姐，今天是我们的盛大日子。孩子们的大日子，我们全体一齐出力，承您和赫里克先生对我们这么好。还有，赫里克小姐，您对我们实在好。您今天为我们做的这些小事，想必不至于成为您的烦累吧，您说呢？」

「哦，不烦。等客人们到了，尼古拉斯和我就穿着最漂亮的衣裳露面。对我们来说还有比这更美的吗？再说由我来开前门，我们听得见铃。不过商贩送货走后门，上午又没有访客，我都不知道这门要开给谁。除非是开给伯吉斯先生和孩子们。这活儿要论人数报上去，倒还真像回事。」

「是，赫里克小姐，」梅里先生说，相当匆忙地给艾米丽让路。「是，赫里克小姐，对我们大家真好真客气。哦，伯吉斯先生！您还没走呢，伯吉斯先生。那您千万确定门锁钥匙带上了，伯吉斯先生，好吗？今天人人都要跑来跑去，您知道的。」

===Original===
“Yes, Mr. Merry, I believe I have it. I always make a point of carrying it. A latch key is a useful thing to have on one, in case of wanting to run in and out.”

“Oh, well, Mr. Burgess. Wanting to run in and out! Oh, well, just today I meant, you know.”

A few hours later Emily opened the door to Bumpus and Masson.

“So you are not ashamed of coming to see us when we are really helping to earn the living. I mean of being seen with us. Did you see Mr. Burgess passing the college this morning, with his paper, and all the boys? How young did you think he looked?”

“About twenty,” said Bumpus. “A nice boy.”

===Chinese===
「带了，梅里先生，我想是带了。我一向特意随身带着。门锁钥匙这东西带在身上有用，万一要进进出出的。」

「哦，哎，伯吉斯先生。进进出出！哦，哎，我只是说今天，您知道的。」

几个小时之后，艾米丽开门迎进了邦珀斯和马森。

「这么说，我们真帮着挣钱糊口的时候，你们不嫌跟我们在一起丢人了。我是说不怕让人看见跟我们为伍。今早你看见伯吉斯先生带着报纸和全体孩子从学院门口过没有？你看他显得多年轻？」

「二十上下，」邦珀斯说。「一个好孩子。」

===Original===
“Oh, you don’t think he looks as young as that? Nicholas, here is Dickie saying that Mr. Burgess looks about twenty, and a nice boy! And he is here to look quite apart from the boys. It is so worrying about the parents noticing how young he is. With Nicholas doing nothing, and Mr. Merry’s having no education, there is only Mr. Burgess to be looked to for all the boys’ advantages. Of course Miss Basden is better than all three of them. But parents don’t count a woman.”

“That is unreasonable of them,” said Bumpus, “as one out of every pair of them is a woman.”

“Perhaps that is how they know,” said Emily. “But Mr. Burgess only missed a year of college because he couldn’t afford to be kept there, and he has been here eighteen months; so he really might be doing something now.”

“Has he no degree, then?” said Bumpus.

“Yes, he worked for it during the first year he was here.”

===Chinese===
「哦，你以为他没显得那么年轻？尼古拉斯，迪基在这儿说伯吉斯先生看上去二十上下，是个好孩子！而他来是要显得跟孩子们截然两样的。家长们的眼睛会看出他多么年轻，这真让人发愁。尼古拉斯什么都不做，梅里先生又没受过教育，孩子们那些长处全指望伯吉斯先生一个人撑着。当然，巴斯登小姐比他们三个加起来都强。可家长们不算女人一份。」

「这可不讲理，」邦珀斯说，「他们自己两两成对，每一对里都有一个女的。」

「也许正因如此他们才有数，」艾米丽说。「不过伯吉斯先生读大学只差一年没读完，是因为供不起，他到这儿已经一年半了；如今真该有点作为了。」

「那他没有学位？」邦珀斯说。

「有，他来这儿的第一年就把它攻下来了。」

===Original===
“I did not know you could do that for Oxford or Cambridge,” said Masson.

“I don’t think it was Oxford or Cambridge,” said Emily. “Don’t be searching and snobbish, William.”

“Well, missing the culminating part of advantages does not show,” said Bumpus.

“Not very much, to parents, I hope. It is only Mr. Merry who really shows how much he has missed; and Mr. Merry can do anything. Isn’t it generous of him to spend his life giving to others what he has not had himself?”

“Why not hand over prayers to him, and retire, Herrick?” said Bumpus. “If he can read.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道牛津剑桥还能这样，」马森说。

「我看不是牛津也不是剑桥，」艾米丽说。「别这么刨根问底、势利眼，威廉。」

「唉，错过了好处的顶头那一截，倒也看不出来，」邦珀斯说。

「希望家长们看不大出来。真正把自己错过的东西显露出来的只有梅里先生；可梅里先生什么事都办得到。他一辈子把自己没有得到过的东西给别人，这不是很慷慨吗？」

「干吗不把祷告交给他，您就告老，赫里克？」邦珀斯说。「前提是他识字。」

===Original===
“It might be bold to make any change,” said Masson.

“And boldness in religion is out of place,” said Emily, “when we have to be humiliated and lowly. Mr. Merry can read. I saw him once, reading. But of course Nicholas has to read prayers. You must see that, Dickie.”

“Yes, yes. I see that, really,” said Bumpus.

“You were obvious, Richard,” said Masson.

“Dear Mr. Merry!” said Emily. “He is not obvious, is he? To support us all, so that people think it is Nicholas! I really don’t think it is obvious.”

===Chinese===
「任何变动都嫌冒失，」马森说。

「何况在宗教里冒失不得，」艾米丽说，「我们只配受辱和谦卑。梅里先生识字。我见过他读书。可祷告当然得尼古拉斯来读。这一点你必须明白，迪基。」

「是，是。这个我明白，真的，」邦珀斯说。

「你这话太露了，理查德，」马森说。

「亲爱的梅里先生！」艾米丽说。「他可不露，是不是？养活我们全家，还让人们以为那是尼古拉斯！我真不觉得这叫露。」

===Original===
“It is in good taste,” said Herrick.

“You do your half, darling,” said Emily. “It is all a matter of the time people take. There are those hundreds of helpless children, coming up from that cellar that we have never seen. I wish Dickens was alive, to expose schools. Mr. Merry has stopped to look back at Mrs. Merry, as if she were a dumb pet that understood.”

“I can understand Merry, too,” said Bumpus.

“I believe you can,” said Emily. “That is a side I do so admire in you. We must go after them, Nicholas, and walk about, with you in your gown. It makes me feel homesick that Mr. Merry shouldn’t work any harder than the rest of us. And it is so humiliating that he hasn’t a gown. I wish I could mend your coat, darling. It is really undignified for you to have your clothes mended by Mrs. Merry, as if you were one of the boys.”

“They know we can’t help me,” said Herrick. “But Burgess can be helped, and he looks himself as if he wished he looked older.”

===Chinese===
「这很有格调，」赫里克说。

「你干你那一半就是了，亲爱的，」艾米丽说。「全看人们肯搭进去多少工夫。瞧那几百个没爹娘操心的孩子，正从我们从未见识过的那间地窖里上来。狄更斯要还活着就好了，好来揭一揭这些学校。梅里先生停下脚步回头看梅里太太，好像她是只通人性的不会说话的爱畜。」

「我也能理解梅里，」邦珀斯说。

「我相信你能，」艾米丽说。「这正是我顶佩服你的一面。我们得跟上他们了，尼古拉斯，去四处走走，你穿着你的袍子。梅里先生竟不必比我们任何人更辛苦，这真让我害起思乡病来。还有，他没有袍子，多丢人。真想替你补补外衣，亲爱的。你的衣裳让梅里太太来补，实在有失体统，倒像你也是孩子们中的一个。」

「他们知道我这个人帮不上，」赫里克说。「可伯吉斯是帮得上的，他自己那副神情也像是恨不得显得老些。」

===Original===
“Well, his heart is in the right place,” said Bumpus.

“And after all, they would know that we shouldn’t have anyone really mature for him,” said Emily. “But he copies Nicholas, so that they know that Nicholas’ influence permeates the school; and that is bad. And then they ask, and find it doesn’t, and that again is bad.”

“Fletcher is to give the prizes,” said Herrick. “It has to be a parson. I don’t know why.”

“Well, God does like reward,” said Bumpus.

“It is a great thing that Burgess at his age isn’t a parson,” said Masson.

===Chinese===
「唉，他的心总归是长对了地方的，」邦珀斯说。

「再说他们心里也明白，我们不可能给配上一个真正成熟的人，」艾米丽说。「可他处处仿尼古拉斯，好让人知道尼古拉斯的影响浸润全校；这是一坏。回头他们一打听，发现并非如此，这又是一坏。」

「发奖由弗莱彻来，」赫里克说。「总得是个牧师。我不知道为什么。」

「唉，上帝确实喜欢奖励，」邦珀斯说。

「伯吉斯这个年纪幸好不是牧师，这是件大事，」马森说。

===Original===
“It is a great success for him. I wonder if he thought of it, himself,” said Bumpus. “Of course he did not have to think of it.”

“I wish Mr. Merry did not look so affectionately at the boys,” said Emily. “It makes Nicholas look so dreadful by comparison. And we don’t realize how bad I look by Mrs. Merry; so unmotherly. And suppose anyone should take Mr. Burgess for a boy, or forget Miss Basden!”

“Does Miss Basden not like being forgotten?” said Masson, as one not disliking this himself.

“She is morbid about it,” said Bumpus. “I have not spoken to her, but I can see that.”

“Don’t be superior to women, and more so to those who earn their living, Dickie,” said Emily. “It is so revealing of you. And not spoken to Miss Basden, when but for her you would have to support most of your nearest friends! You would find it so difficult to do it too. I believe Miss Basden does it.”

===Chinese===
「这对他是一大成功。不知他自己想到过没有，」邦珀斯说。「当然，他不必去想它。」

「但愿梅里先生别那么深情地望着孩子们，」艾米丽说。「一比之下，尼古拉斯显得多可怕。而我们意识不到，站在梅里太太旁边我有多难看；一点没有母亲样。再说，万一有谁把伯吉斯先生当成个孩子，或者忘了巴斯登小姐呢！」

「巴斯登小姐不喜欢被人忘掉吗？」马森说，他自己倒像并不讨厌这个。

「她在这上头病态得很，」邦珀斯说。「我没跟她说过话，可我看得出。」

「别凌驾于女人之上，更别凌驾于自食其力的女人之上，迪基，」艾米丽说。「这太暴露你了。还没跟巴斯登小姐说过话呢——若不是她，你就得养活你最亲近的朋友里的大半！真要你养，你还觉得难上加难呢。我看巴斯登小姐就养着。」

===Original===
“Will there be a great crowd?” said Herrick.

“Well, we ought to want that,” said Masson.

“How generously you both identify yourself with us!” said Emily. “Most of the parents are coming. I hope it doesn’t mean that they want to look into things, or reassure themselves, or anything like that.”

“Will you make the speech on the spur of the moment, Herrick?” said Masson.

“Nicholas can’t spend his genius on speeches for boys,” said Emily. “He has his book to give it to.”

===Chinese===
「人会很多吗？」赫里克说。

「唉，我们本该盼着人多，」马森说。

「你们俩跟我们休戚与共得多慷慨！」艾米丽说。「家长们大多数都要来。但愿这不意味着他们想查查内情，或者安安自己的心，或者诸如此类。」

「您到时候临场发挥致辞吗，赫里克？」马森说。

「尼古拉斯的天才不能耗费在给孩子们的致辞上，」艾米丽说。「他的天才要留给他的书。」

===Original===
“People don’t really make speeches on the spur of the moment,” said Bumpus. “Merry will make it.”

“I thought an outsider always made the speech,” said Masson; “that the schoolmaster’s business was not to praise his own school, and all that.”

“You thought Merry’s business was not that?” said Bumpus. “Then what did you think his business was?”

“You know an outsider is not called in here to do anything,” said Emily. “Mr. Merry does it. And you should not call Nicholas an outsider, when our business today is to prove that he is not one. Now we must go and serve under Mr. Merry.”

“They also serve who only stand and wait,” said Bumpus.

===Chinese===
「世上其实没有谁临场发挥致辞，」邦珀斯说。「梅里会致辞的。」

「我还以为致辞向来请外人来，」马森说；「以为校长不该夸自己的学校，等等。」

「你以为梅里的本分不是这个？」邦珀斯说。「那你以为他的本分是什么？」

「你们知道的，这儿什么事也不请外人来，」艾米丽说。「梅里先生全包了。今天我们的任务就是证明尼古拉斯不是外人，你就别再叫他外人。好了，我们得去梅里先生麾下听差了。」

「站着静候的也是在效力，」邦珀斯说。

===Original===
“Don’t notice it when you see Nicholas not being himself,” said Emily. “His real self really doesn’t do. And go up and shake hands with Miss Basden. And don’t be arch and joking with Mr. Burgess, as if he were young.”

Mrs. Merry and Miss Basden were talking in easy tones, with a nervous unconsciousness of what they said.

“How do you do, Miss Herrick?” said Mrs. Merry.

“Oh, Mrs. Merry, what are you doing?” said Miss Basden.

“Poor Miss Herrick, to be forgotten!” said Mr. Burgess, advancing to Emily with his hands under his gown, and drawing Mr. Merry’s glance.

===Chinese===
「看见尼古拉丁不像他自己时，别在意，」艾米丽说。「他真实的自己做不得。去跟巴斯登小姐握握手。也别跟伯吉斯先生打趣逗笑，仿佛他真年轻似的。」

梅里太太和巴斯登小姐正语调闲适地交谈着，对自己说些什么，怀着一种紧张的浑然不觉。

「您好，赫里克小姐？」梅里太太说。

「哎呀，梅里太太，您这是做什么？」巴斯登小姐说。

「可怜的赫里克小姐，竟被人忘了！」伯吉斯先生说，双手拢在袍下朝艾米丽迎上来，引来了梅里先生的一瞥。

===Original===
“Oh, really, Miss Herrick! Well! What am I doing? It is the idea of shaking hands with so many people.”

“Yes, Mother,” said Mr. Merry, uneasily.

Herrick stepped on to the platform in gown and hood, his expression inviting attention to himself. Mr. Burgess, conscious of his similar garb, took his place near him, and exchanged an easy word. Mr. Merry, who had no right to a gown and hood, stepped up after them, and stood surveying his pupils with an air of fond understanding of boys.

“Now I am not much of a hand at speechmaking. What I do is to teach my boys, and be with my boys, and give all my time to my boys. But what I want to say to you all is, that I am glad to see you all amongst us today; that my wife is glad, and my kind helpers are glad; and Mr. Herrick and Miss Herrick, whom you have really come to see, of course, are glad. In fact we are all glad to be together, to celebrate the good work done by our boys; by your boys, and by my boys; for I have boys, you know, though I have only girls, really, if I may be very Irish for a schoolmaster. And the prizes are not won only by those who have won them, you know, though that sounds Irish again; for we don’t overdo things in the old-fashioned way, you know. So we will see the prize-winners take their prizes, and the other prize earners”﻿—Mr. Merry glanced with tender pride round at the boys﻿—“show how glad they are that they have won them; which seems to be more Irish than ever.”

Mr. Merry stepped down amid clapping from the boys. Mr. Fletcher did his part with covert reference to a sheet of paper. The youngest boy presented flowers to Mrs. Merry.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，真是的，赫里克小姐！唉！我在做什么呢？是一想到要跟这么多人握手。」

「是啊，妈妈，」梅里先生不安地说。

赫里克穿着袍子戴着头巾走上台，那副神情引人注目于他一身。伯吉斯先生意识到自己装束相仿，便在他近旁就位，随意交谈了一句。无权穿袍戴巾的梅里先生跟在他们后面上了台，站在那里，用一副疼爱而知童心的神气检阅他的学生们。

「唉，我这个人不太会致辞。我做的事，就是教我的孩子们，陪着我的孩子们，把我全部时间给我的孩子们。可我想对大家说的一句话是：今天看见大家都在我们中间，我很高兴；我妻子高兴，我的好帮手们高兴；赫里克先生和赫里克小姐——大家真正要来看的，当然——也高兴。其实我们大家欢聚一堂都很高兴，来庆贺孩子们取得的好成绩；你们的孩子们的成绩，和我的孩子们的成绩；因为我也有孩子，你们知道的，虽说真正算起来我只有女儿——做校长的这么说，未免太爱尔兰腔了。还有，奖不只是得奖的人赢来的，你们知道，虽说这话听着又爱尔兰腔了；因为我们不照老一套把事情做过头，你们知道的。那么我们就看获奖的孩子们领奖，其余那些挣了奖的孩子们」——梅里先生带着温柔的骄傲环视孩子们——「显示一下他们为同伴获奖有多高兴；这听着简直爱尔兰腔得无以复加了。」

梅里先生在孩子们的掌声中走下台来。弗莱彻先生对着一张纸条暗中照本宣科，尽了职责。最小的孩子向梅里太太献了花。

===Original===
Mrs. Merry took her stand behind a table furnished by a consistence of fate with cups and an urn.

“Well, Mr. Merry,” said a father, “so you haven’t put my boy among the prize-winners? Of course I don’t mean that. But he doesn’t go in for taking prizes, does he?”

“Ah, your boy,” said Mr. Merry, who knew the ill policy of honesty with parents; “and a nice boy too! No, he doesn’t go in for taking prizes. No, not yet. But I tell you what.” Mr. Merry’s voice became intimate. “If I had a boy, I should like him to be your boy. I will tell you that.”

Mr. Merry passed on, and paused at Mrs. Merry’s table.

“Now, Mother, now, don’t get all into a fuss. The result of all this will be that you are knocked up. I can see that.”

===Chinese===
梅里太太在一张桌后站定，这张桌子承命运的执意，备着茶杯和一把大茶炊。

「哎，梅里先生，」一位父亲说，「这么说您没把我儿子排进获奖的名单？当然，我不是那个意思。可他本来也不争什么奖，是吧？」

「啊，您的儿子，」梅里先生说，他深知对家长诚实是下策；「多好的孩子！是啊，他不争奖。眼下还不争。可我跟您说句掏心的话。」梅里先生的声音亲近起来。「要是我有个儿子，我就愿他是您儿子这样的。这话我告诉您。」

梅里先生继续往前走，在梅里太太的桌旁停了停。

「好了，妈妈，好了，别慌成那样。这一切的结果就是您要累垮。我看得出来。」

===Original===
Mr. Merry had not meant his words for the general ear, but had been more occupied with the feelings which prompted them, than caution in their utterance.

“Now, Mother, it is all right, Mother. You are just a little overdone. That is what it is. We know what it is. We don’t think anything of anything.” Mr. Merry referred in this way to the fact that Mrs. Merry was in tears. “It is just because you look after us all too well. That is what it is.”

“Well, Mr. Merry,” said another father. “And what have you to say for those two boys of mine?”

“Ah, the little fellows! My wife, she has a soft spot in her heart for them.”

“And how do they do at their books? John is a scatterbrain, I am afraid. I suppose these long holidays nowadays are a good thing?”

===Chinese===
梅里先生本无意让这些话入众人之耳，只是一心系着促使他说这话的情感，顾不上出言谨慎。

「好了，妈妈，没事的，妈妈。您只是一时撑过头了。就是这么回事。我们都知道是怎么回事。我们什么都不往心里去。」梅里先生用这话暗指梅里太太正在落泪。「就是因为您把我们照顾得太好了。就是这么回事。」

「哎，梅里先生，」另一位父亲说。「我家那两个小子，您有什么可说的？」

「啊，两个小家伙！我太太心里最疼他们。」

「他们功课怎么样？我怕约翰是个没定性、爱忘事的孩子。如今这些长假想必是件好事吧？」

===Original===
“Ah, little John! Well, some boys haven’t the brains to scatter. And all work and no play, you know!”

“He would not like to be called little John,” said a grown-up sister, who was with the father.

“Wouldn’t he?” said Emily. “Not when we only keep them until they are fourteen! But the young are cruel.”

“Ah, Miss Herrick, you will talk in your way to us,” said Mr. Merry. “You know Miss Basden, do you not, Mr. Bentley?”

“No, I think not,” said Mr. Bentley, simply.

===Chinese===
「啊，小约翰！唉，有的孩子还没有可以撒出去的脑筋呢。再说光用功不玩耍，您知道的！」

「他可不愿意人家叫他小约翰，」随父亲同来的、已长成的姐姐说。

「是吗？」艾米丽说。「我们只把他们留到十四岁呢！不过年轻人是残忍的。」

「啊，赫里克小姐，您总有您的一套说法，」梅里先生说。「您认识巴斯登小姐吧，本特利先生（Mr. Bentley）？」

「不，我想不认识，」本特利先生老老实实地说。

===Original===
“Why, she is always here, Father. Every year,” said the daughter. “How are you, Miss Basden?”

“She is always here. Every year here with us,” said Mr. Merry, lifting his hands on and off Miss Basden’s shoulders. “Always here, so that people don’t notice her any more than they do one of ourselves. Because she is one of ourselves, if she will be, aren’t you, Miss Basden?”

“You see the difference between ordinary people and Mr. Merry,” said Emily to Bumpus. “And you said you did not know Miss Basden. You rank with the ordinary people.”

“I always suspected it,” said Bumpus.

“Mr. Herrick is just bringing out another book, is he not?” said Mr. Bentley. “Is it on any subject that the boys could get any﻿—be interested in now, I mean?”

===Chinese===
「怎么会，她年年都在这儿，爸爸。年年都来，」女儿说。「您好，巴斯登小姐？」

「她年年都在。年年跟我们在一起，」梅里先生说，双手在巴斯登小姐肩上一放一收。「总在这儿，人们便不再留意她，正如不留意我们自己人。因为她本就是我们中间的一个——只要她愿意，是不是，巴斯登小姐？」

「瞧见寻常人和梅里先生的分别了吧，」艾米丽对邦珀斯说。「你方才还说不认识巴斯登小姐。你位列寻常人一等。」

「我早疑心是这样了，」邦珀斯说。

「赫里克先生正要出一本新书，是吧？」本特利先生说。「它写的题目，孩子们能不能捞点——我是说，眼下能感兴趣吗？」

===Original===
“Oh well, well, you know, if Mr. Herrick wrote on a subject, then the book would not be his own. And that would not do for Mr. Herrick. And writing for boys! Well, we could not expect that from him. But there is our atmosphere; our thing that we have to give, that other schools don’t give. And that only Mr. Herrick can do for us. Ah, and he does it for us. I wish I had had it in my young days. Then I might not have been the schoolmaster now. I might have been the other thing. And I hope your laddies will be it. I do hope it. And, bless them, I shouldn’t be surprised.”

Mr. Merry wrung Mr. Bentley’s hand.

“Well, Mr. Burgess, so you are going about, are you, Mr. Burgess? Now what about this theory that we don’t want long holidays? That is not quite on your line, is it?”

“Well, I dare say there is something in it, Mr. Merry. When I first became a schoolmaster, I thought I should never get used to the breaks from work. But I confess I am becoming reconciled. That is the way of schoolmasters, I fear.”

“Oh, well, that is one kind of talk, Mr. Burgess. But what would you have done, now, if you had not become a schoolmaster?”

===Chinese===
「哦，这个，这个，您知道的，要是赫里克先生就一个题目写书，那书就不是他自己的了。那样可不行，对赫里克先生来说。再说为孩子们写作！唉，这个我们不能指望他。可我们有我们的气息；我们有我们要给的东西，别的学校不给。而这一点只有赫里克先生能替我们做到。啊，他也真替我们做到了。我年轻时要是有这个就好了。那我现在也许就不是教书先生了。我也许就是另外那样人了。我盼着您的两个小家伙成为那样的人。真心盼着。愿老天保佑他们，我说不定真能盼到。」

梅里先生紧紧握了握本特利先生的手。

「哎，伯吉斯先生，这么说您在四处走动呢，是吧，伯吉斯先生？对了，有人说我们不需要长假，这理论您怎么看？这不太合您的路子吧？」

「唉，我想它也有几分道理，梅里先生。我刚当上教书先生的时候，以为自己永远习惯不了工作中断。可我承认我正渐渐讲和了。教书先生的通病，恐怕。」

「哦，哎，这是一种说法，伯吉斯先生。可您要是不当教书先生，当年会做什么呢？」

===Original===
“Oh, you are off the point, Mr. Merry.”

“Not at all, not at all. What would you have done, now, if you had not taken up schoolmastering?”

“No, no, off the point, Mr. Merry.”

“Mr. Burgess seems deeply attached to the point,” said Miss Basden.

“It should attract even the humblest educationist, Miss Basden,” said Mr. Burgess, bowing.

===Chinese===
「哦，您跑题了，梅里先生。」

「一点没有，一点没有。您要不是干上了教书这一行，当年会做什么呢？」

「不，不，跑题了，梅里先生。」

「伯吉斯先生对这个题目看来一往情深，」巴斯登小姐说。

「哪怕最谦卑的教育家，也该被这个题目吸引，巴斯登小姐，」伯吉斯先生说着，欠身一礼。

===Original===
“Well, Mr. Merry is an educationist, I suppose you will admit?” said Miss Basden.

“Surely,” said Mr. Burgess.

“Ah, we understand each other, don’t we, Mr. Burgess?” said Mr. Merry. “With your kind help, Miss Basden, and all you do for all of us.”

“Well, it wasn’t quite so degrading as usual, was it?” said Emily, as the four settled into Herrick’s study. “Nobody spoke to me like an employer. Did anybody to you, Nicholas?”

“It went off admirably,” said Herrick. “Admirably. I quite enjoyed it. I should feel quite proud of the school, as I thought a school a thing to be proud of. And Merry surpassed himself.”

===Chinese===
「那么梅里先生是位教育家，您总得承认吧？」巴斯登小姐说。

「那是自然，」伯吉斯先生说。

「啊，我们彼此心照，是不是，伯吉斯先生？」梅里先生说。「多亏您好心帮衬，巴斯登小姐，多亏您为我们大家做的一切。」

「唉，这一回不像往常那么难堪，是不是？」四人坐进赫里克的书房后，艾米丽说。「没人拿东家的口气跟我说话。你呢，尼古拉斯？」

「进行得漂亮极了，」赫里克说。「漂亮极了。我相当尽兴。我简直要为学校骄傲了——倘若我认为学校是件可骄傲的东西的话。梅里超水平发挥。」

===Original===
“I saw you enjoying it,” said Emily. “I saw you being proud of the school, too. That was charming of you. But I followed the workings of Mr. Merry’s mind too sensitively for enjoyment. A great mind on the rack would be a dreadful thing to enjoy. And he did not always surpass himself. He was really indefinite with Mr. Bentley about his boys.”

“Oh, only with that kind of fellow,” said Herrick.

“How can you be so reckless?” said Bumpus. “Or is that the attitude that makes for success?”

“With Merry behind, it will do,” said Herrick.

“We shall have to propitiate Mr. Bentley,” said Emily. “I am afraid it will not do, darling. Mr. Bentley isn’t so fond of your having Mr. Merry behind as you think.”

===Chinese===
「我看见你尽兴了，」艾米丽说。「也看见你为学校骄傲了。真可爱。可梅里先生的心思运转，我追得太贴，顾不上尽兴。一颗伟大的心智在受刑，欣赏起来未免可怕。再说他也不是处处超水平。跟本特利先生谈起他的孩子们，他实在含糊得可以。」

「哦，只对那一类人如此，」赫里克说。

「你怎么能这么不管不顾？」邦珀斯说。「还是说，正是这种态度成就事业？」

「有梅里在后面兜着，不妨事，」赫里克说。

「我们得去安抚本特利先生，」艾米丽说。「恐怕不妨不了事，亲爱的。本特利先生可不像你想的那么乐意你背后有个梅里。」

===Original===
“How will you do that?” said Bumpus. “By having Merry qualify?”

“By asking Mr. Bentley to dinner,” said Emily. “Being for an evening with Nicholas teaches people better than anything. And it is difficult for them to behave like employers when they have been our guests. We ought to have him while the house is disarranged; and we will ask the Fletchers too.”

“Is a disarranged house better for a dinner?” said Masson.

“Yes,” said Emily. “This room has to be regarded as the drawing-room, and a classroom made into the dining-room, and the boys’ basement dining-room suppressed, to have anything at all. And Mrs. Merry can’t be asked to do that often.”

“No, no,” said Herrick.

===Chinese===
「那怎么安抚？」邦珀斯说。「让梅里去考个资格？」

「请本特利先生来吃晚饭，」艾米丽说。「跟尼古拉斯消磨一个晚上，比什么都能教好人。做过我们的客人，再要摆东家的架子就难了。要请就趁屋子还乱着的时候请他；弗莱彻一家我们也一并请上。」

「屋子乱着请客吃饭更好？」马森说。

「是啊，」艾米丽说。「要成个样子，就得把这间屋权当客厅，一间教室改成饭厅，孩子们那间地下饭厅则取消不用。这种事不好常劳烦梅里太太。」

「是，是，」赫里克说。

===Original===
“You will have to ask her about the dinner, darling,” said Emily. “She never can speak to you, so she won’t be able to say words of refusal.”

“Why cannot you ask her as often as you want, if she can’t say words of refusal?” said Bumpus.

“You are so unmanly about Mrs. Merry, Dickie,” said Emily.

“I feel rather unmanly about her,” said Bumpus.

“I feel unmanly in asking her this,” said Herrick.

===Chinese===
「请客的事只好你去问她，亲爱的，」艾米丽说。「她永远跟你说不上话，所以拒绝的话她也说不出口。」

「她要是说不出拒绝的话，那你想问几回不就问几回？」邦珀斯说。

「你在梅里太太的事上太没男人气了，迪基，」艾米丽说。

「我在她跟前确实觉得没男人气，」邦珀斯说。

「我去求她这件事，也觉得没男人气，」赫里克说。

===Original===
“You think it is unwomanly of me to ask you to,” said Emily. “But I have not the true courage that is womanly. It is being so much with men. But how you do realize the domestic problem, Nicholas! You are better than either Dickie or William at that. I think it must mean that you have the mixture of the feminine and masculine, known to be in genius. I feel so hopeful about your book.”

“Ah, Herrick, the time for our exposure gets near, doesn’t it?” said Bumpus.

“Yes, yes, it does,” said Herrick. “It does get near, indeed. And it will be something of an exposure for me. Because my book brings out really a new self in me, a self that I was hardly conscious was there, myself.”

“Yes, books do come out queerly in that way,” said Bumpus. “Now my book shows an old side of me; a young side, I might say, that I thought had been covered up for twenty years. I wonder if any of you will find my old self in it.”

“I suppose that is the difference between an author and an ordinary man,” said Emily. “Because of course there must be some difference. An author does things from a new self or an old self, and an ordinary man just from his ordinary self, as if he were doing an ordinary thing, which of course he is. I am glad that Nicholas’ is the new self, because his early one might not make a book quite suitable for a schoolmaster, for a schoolmaster to write.”

===Chinese===
「你是觉得我支使你去求人，不够女人味，」艾米丽说。「可我天生没有女人该有的那种勇气。跟男人处得太久了。不过你对家务难题体察得真透，尼古拉斯！这一点上你比迪基和威廉都强。我想这一定说明，你身上有那种据说天才必有的雌雄混融。我对你的书更觉得有指望了。」

「啊，赫里克，我们原形毕露的日子近了，是不是？」邦珀斯说。

「是啊，是啊，近了，」赫里克说。「真是近了。而对我来说，这也算一场亮相。因为我这本书捧出的，实在是一个新的我，一个连我自己都几乎不曾意识到其存在的我。」

「是啊，书就是这样古怪地出来的，」邦珀斯说。「我这本书显示的就是旧日的一面；不妨说是年轻的一面，我以为它被埋了二十年了。不知你们中间有谁会在里面认出旧日的我。」

「我想这就是作家与寻常人的分别，」艾米丽说。「因为总得有点分别。作家做事，凭的是一个新的自我或旧的自我；寻常人只凭他那个寻常的自我，仿佛在做一件寻常事——当然他也确实在做寻常事。幸好尼古拉斯捧出的是新的自我，他那早年的自我，写出来的书未必适合一个校长，适合校长执笔。」

===Original===
“Do you remember, how Herrick once said he could live without Emily?” said Masson, as he and Bumpus left the house.

“William,” said Bumpus, “you know there is a thing I will do for you, if you want it done? If you want to marry Emily, it could be easily managed about Herrick. You might have to live with him. That would be easy. Or he could live with you. That would be easy. You are a rich man. I mean, I would bring the thing to Emily, if you would find less surface trouble so. I could bring her mind in turn to you. I would do my best, I would do well, between you.”

“Richard, I will say it to you,” said Masson. “I am grateful enough, to understand you. I know what you think it might mean of change for you. I would accept it, if I needed it. I would take anything from you. But if I had wished to marry Emily, if she had wished to marry me; I will say the first; it would have been enough; I should have asked her many years ago. I should not have thought of Herrick, to be plain. I should ask her now, if the desire came to me. I should ask her myself, like the ordinary man I am.”

===Chinese===
「你还记得吗，赫里克有回说过，没有艾米丽他也活得成？」马森说，这时他和邦珀斯正离开这所宅子。

「威廉，」邦珀斯说，「有件事你要愿意，我就替你去办，你知道么？你要是想娶艾米丽，赫里克那头是很容易安排的。你可能得跟他同住。那不难。或者他跟你住。那也不难。你是个有钱人。我是说，这事由我去向艾米丽提，倘若这样你觉得面子上少些周折。我也能把她的心思引到你身上来。我会尽我的力，会在你们中间办得妥帖。」

「理查德，这话我只对你说，」马森说。「我感激之至，也听得懂你。我知道你以为这对你意味着怎样的改变。我若需要，我会领受。你给我的什么我都肯要。可倘若我曾有意娶艾米丽，倘若她曾有意嫁我——我只说头一件；有它就够了；多年前我就会向她开口了。坦白说，我根本不会想到赫里克。如今只要这心愿再起，我仍会向她开口。我会亲自去求她，像我本来的那个寻常人一样。」

===Original===
“Of course you would,” said Bumpus. “I see now. I never saw before. But I meant well. Oh, but I believe you know how well I meant.”

“I would take anything from you,” said Masson. “But I would have you understand my feeling for Emily. I have all you supposed I might have for her. And I hope she has something for me. I hope and believe it. For it means a great thing in my life for me. But you are the more necessary to me, as her brother is to her. That is not to say that you and he are exactly first to either of us. Do you see?”

“Yes,” said Bumpus. “I think I have always seen.”

===Chinese===
「你当然会，」邦珀斯说。「我现在明白了。从前竟没明白。可我是好意。哦，我相信你知道我的好意有多真。」

「你给我的什么我都肯要，」马森说。「可我要你明白我对艾米丽的感情。你所揣度我或许对她有的，我全都有。我也希望她对我有几分情意。我盼着，也信着。因为它在我生命里是件大事。可你对我更不可少，正如她哥哥之于她。这并不是说你和他在我们两人心里都恰好排第一。你明白么？」

「明白，」邦珀斯说。「我想我一直都明白。」

## V / 五

===Original===
“Are the boys coming?” said the Reverend Henry Bentley.

“They are out of their room, Father,” said his daughter.

“I asked you if they were coming.”

“They are out of their room, Father. We are all a little late this morning.”

“I know we are late. I asked you if they were coming.”

===Chinese===
「孩子们来了没有？」亨利·本特利牧师（the Reverend Henry Bentley）说。

「他们出房间了，爸爸，」他的女儿说。

「我问你的是他们来不来。」

「他们出房间了，爸爸。我们今早都略迟了些。」

「我知道我们迟了。我问你的是他们来不来。」

===Original===
Mr. Bentley rang the bell, and took his seat. He was an upright, white-haired man in the fifties. He had been a parson and a younger son, and had come later into the family estate.

Two boys of twelve and thirteen edged into the room, with the words, “Good morning, Father.”

Mr. Bentley simply turned his eyes on them; and the younger showed the behaviour natural to continual gratitude, by vaguely capering.

The daughter was a tall young woman of thirty, with a pointed chin, and a small, compressed, peaceful mouth. She was the child of Mr. Bentley by an earlier marriage. He was a widower for the second time.

“I am sorry we are late, Father.”

===Chinese===
本特利先生按了铃，落了座。他是个腰板笔直、头发花白的五十多岁男人。他当过牧师，又是幼子，后来才承继了家产。

两个十二岁和十三岁的孩子侧着身子挪进屋来，说了声「早上好，爸爸」。

本特利先生只把眼睛转向他们；小的那个以含糊的雀跃，表露了持续感恩之下自然而然的行为。

女儿迪莉娅（Delia）是个三十岁的高个子年轻女子，下巴尖尖，一张小小的、抿紧的、安静的嘴。她是本特利先生前妻所生。他已两度丧妻。

「对不起，我们迟了，爸爸。」

===Original===
Mr. Bentley did not look at her. He bowed his head over the breakfast with an air of not sharing the general thanklessness. Delia raised her head a little after the rest. There was a suggestion about her of remembering their being late.

“We shall have a windy walk to the college chapel,” said Mr. Bentley.

“What did you say, Father?” said Delia.

Mr. Bentley did not answer.

“We shall have a windy walk to the chapel,” he presently said, in a still lower voice.

===Chinese===
本特利先生不看她。他朝早饭低下头去，一副不与众人同其忘恩的神气。迪莉娅比其余人稍晚一点抬起头。她身上隐隐透着一股记着大家迟到这事的意思。

「去学院礼拜堂这一路要顶风了，」本特利先生说。

「你说什么，爸爸？」迪莉娅说。

本特利先生不答。

「去礼拜堂这一路要顶风了，」过了一会儿他说，声音压得更低。

===Original===
“What did you say, Father?”

Mr. Bentley did not answer.

“Would you like lunch at one or half-past, Father?”

Mr. Bentley was silent.

“Would you like lunch at one or half-past, Father?”

===Chinese===
「你说什么，爸爸？」

本特利先生不答。

「午饭您要一点开还是一点半开，爸爸？」

本特利先生沉默着。

「午饭您要一点开还是一点半开，爸爸？」

===Original===
Mr. Bentley was silent.

“Father, I think you heard me.”

“Since it is the fashion this morning to be deaf, I may as well follow it. I might ask how many people had heard me.”

“How late you were home last night, Father! Was the train delayed?”

“I walked from the station. The train was not delayed. It is not a distance one can walk in a minute.”

===Chinese===
本特利先生沉默着。

「爸爸，我想你是听见了的。」

「既然今早时兴装聋，我不妨也赶个时髦。我倒可以问问，有几个人听见过我说话。」

「你昨晚回来得多晚啊，爸爸！火车误点了吗？」

「我是从车站走回来的。火车没有误点。那段路可不是一分钟能走完的。」

===Original===
“You are so good in those ways, Father. We all ought to copy you.”

“I might as well not go on trying to do my best in any way, for all the attention that is paid to me. I am getting tired of things. I cannot say that I am not. And as for copying me, other people in my house certainly should not do less than I. John, if you cannot control your fidgeting, you must go. I cannot bear it.”

There was a long silence.

“Have you not a word to say this morning, Harry? Nobody would think that I went to the expense of sending you to Mr. Herrick’s, to see you sitting there like a stock, as if you could not open your mouth.”

“No, I have nothing to say, Father.”

===Chinese===
「你在这些事上真好，爸爸。我们都该学你。」

「既然没人把我放在心上，我再怎么事事尽力，也是白搭。我对什么都倦了。我不能说我不倦。至于学我，我家里的人可绝不该做得比我少。约翰，你要是管不住自己扭来扭去，就走吧。我受不了。」

长长一阵沉默。

「你今早就没有一句话可说吗，哈里（Harry）？谁想得到我花钱送你去赫里克先生那儿，却看你像根木头似的坐在那儿，嘴都张不开。」

「没有，我没什么可说的，爸爸。」

===Original===
Mr. Bentley looked round.

“Do not think of letting me keep you, Father,” said Delia.

“I am not able to think of it. I am going to my writing. I have a good deal to do before chapel. That will give you time.”

Mr. Bentley’s writing was held to have bearing on his property, and it gave him the position of the breadwinner.

When they were returning from the chapel, Mr. Bentley spoke.

===Chinese===
本特利先生环顾四周。

「别想着让我留住你，爸爸，」迪莉娅说。

「我想不了那个。我去写我的东西。礼拜堂之前还有不少事要做。这倒给你们腾出工夫来了。」

本特利先生的写作被认为与他的产业有关，这也给了他养家人的地位。

从礼拜堂回来的路上，本特利先生开了口。

===Original===
“An intellectual fellow, the chaplain. He gives you a sermon to think over, a sermon with some stuff in it. I dare say it is natural that the boys should not care to listen to him.”

Delia was silent.

“Really, I shall be glad when their self-absorption gets to such a pitch, that I am justified in closing my doors to them. I am beginning to feel that they have spoilt my home long enough.”

Delia was silent, and Mr. Bentley turned back to the boys.

“Come, come, walk a little more briskly, and as if you were not so utterly given up to self-indulgence. Ah! self-indulgence is a thing I am not enough down upon in my family.”

===Chinese===
「一个有头脑的人，那位牧师。他给你的布道值得回味，是有内容的布道。我想，孩子们不爱听他，也是自然。」

迪莉娅沉默着。

「说真的，等他们的自我沉溺登峰造极、我有充分的理由对他们闭门不纳时，我倒要谢天谢地。我开始觉得，他们把我这个家糟蹋得够久了。」

迪莉娅沉默着，本特利先生回头去看孩子们。

「走走走，脚步轻快些，别一副完全沉溺于自我放纵的样子。啊！自我放纵这东西，我在自家管束得还不够严。」

===Original===
When the lunch was brought, the elder boy did not see. Mr. Bentley came to the table slowly, his expression unsympathetic towards any promptness.

“Harry, pray stop swinging your foot in that worrying manner.”

“Oh, I did not notice,” said the boy. “Did you speak to me?”

“I do not think I did. I believe we have not spoken since we came in. But I wonder what kind of an experience it is, never to be awake, or alive to anything outside oneself. I wonder what the result would have been, if I had spent my life in a state of lethargy, with no thought for anything outside myself. It would have been a nice thing for other people. Really, sometimes I get tired of denying myself, and wishing for nothing for my own life, and meeting simply with the kind of thing that I meet with.”

“Is it I who have brought this on everyone, Father?”

===Chinese===
午饭端上来时，大的那个孩子没有看见。本特利先生缓步走到桌边，那副神情对一切利落举动都无好感。

「哈里，请你别那样烦人地晃脚了。」

「哦，我没留意，」孩子说。「你是跟我说话吗？」

「我想我没有。我们进了这屋怕是还没说过话呢。可我不知道那是一种什么滋味，永远醒不透，对身外的一切都没有知觉。我不知道，倘若我这辈子浑浑噩噩，对身外之事不闻不问，结果会怎样。那对旁人可真是件好事。说真的，有时我克己克累了，一生一无所求，得到的却不过是我日日遇到的这些。」

「是我把这一切带给大家的吗，爸爸？」

===Original===
“Is it you? Oh, yes, you are sure to be the prominent figure in your own view of anything.”

“I cannot alter my nature,” muttered the boy.

“Your nature! Even when you open your mouth, it is the same. You can, of course, adapt yourself to other people, as we all do, as I have done through my life, more than you could ever realize. If I had had visions about my nature, I do not know what would have become of all of you.”

There was again silence.

“What was the text, Harry?” said Mr. Bentley.

===Chinese===
「是你？哦，是啊，什么事在你自己的眼里，你总归是主角。」

「我的性子改不了，」孩子咕哝道。

「你的性子！你一张嘴也是这一套。你当然能迁就别人，我们人人都迁就，我一辈子迁就的，远不是你所能想象的。想当年我要是也惦记着我自己的性子，真不知道你们所有的人会落个什么下场。」

又是一阵沉默。

「经文是什么，哈里？」本特利先生说。

===Original===
Harry answered rightly.

“John, what was the text?”

John gave a start, and lifted his eyes to the ceiling, and his father looked away from him.

The family settled to books.

“What are you reading, John?”

===Chinese===
哈里答对了。

「约翰，经文是什么？」

约翰一惊，抬眼望着天花板，他父亲把目光从他身上移开了。

一家人各自安顿下来读书。

「你在读什么，约翰？」

===Original===
John made a reply.

“I hope you are really reading it?”

“Oh, yes.”

“And what is your book, Harry?”

Harry rose and offered the book to his father.

===Chinese===
约翰答了一句。

「我希望你是真的在读它？」

「哦，真的。」

「你那本是什么书，哈里？」

哈里起身把书递给父亲。

===Original===
Mr. Bentley glanced at it, and went to his desk. His pen caught the paper.

“Another pen,” he said, turning his eyes on his elder son, but somehow held from the use of his name.

“Harry, are you deaf, and too self-indulgent to stir?”

There was a silence.

“Boys,” said Mr. Bentley, his eyes still on his pen, “you seem to make it a custom now to be with us on Sunday afternoons. I have said nothing of it that I can remember. As you are not of much good to anyone, I think we have perhaps had enough of you.”

===Chinese===
本特利先生扫了它一眼，走向书桌。他的笔在纸上勾住了。

「换支笔，」他说，眼睛转向大儿子，却不知怎的没有叫出他的名字。

「哈里，你聋了吗，还是放纵得懒得动了？」

一阵沉默。

「孩子们，」本特利先生说，眼睛仍盯着笔，「如今你们似乎把星期天下午跟我们待在一处当成了惯例。就我所记得的，我对此一字未提。你们既然对谁都没多大用处，我想我们对你们或许也受够了。」

===Original===
The boys disappeared.

“Father, need you write any more today?” said Delia, later. “You will be quite tired before the week begins. Is it necessary?”

“Urgent business letters about the property which we are living on, are necessary. I should not have done any work today, if I had felt I could leave it.”

“Are you going to do anything tonight, Father?”

“No, I think I have done enough for one day, especially as I shall have to be up and about by seven o’clock tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
孩子们不见了。

「爸爸，你今天还必须再写吗？」过后迪莉娅说。「一星期还没开始，你就要累坏了。有这个必要吗？」

「我们赖以为生的产业上有几封要紧的业务信，非写不可。要是我觉得搁得下，今天我本一点工作都不会做。」

「你今夜还做事吗，爸爸？」

「不了，我想一天做这些也够了，何况明天七点前我就得起来奔波。」

===Original===
Mr. Bentley had chosen the morrow for an inspection of his estate, that is, he would rise to his duty while his family lay at ease.

“It is eight o’clock, Delia,” he said, as if the evening meal’s not being ready to the moment was not to be conceived.

Mr. Bentley carved as one not shirking daily burdens, by reason of a particular one about to fall upon him.

“What were you doing all the time you were upstairs, Harry?”

“Oh, different things, Father.”

===Chinese===
本特利先生把第二天定下巡视田产，也就是说，全家安卧之时，正是他起身赴职责之刻。

「八点了，迪莉娅，」他说，那口气仿佛晚饭竟不能分毫不差地备好，是不可思议之事。

本特利先生切肉分食，像个并不因一桩特殊负担将临而推卸日常操劳的人。

「你在楼上那半天都做什么了，哈里？」

「哦，各种各样的事，爸爸。」

===Original===
“What things?”

“Oh, just little, ordinary things, Father.”

“What things? You must know what you have been doing?”

“I have not been doing anything in particular. We were only upstairs a few hours, Father.”

“I do not require you to tell me how long you were upstairs. And I should like to know when you will do something, Harry. Just suppose that I spent my life without ever doing anything. There would be a very different life for you, to going every day to Mr. Herrick’s, and having money spent on you without any trouble for yourself. You would soon get to know the difference, very soon.”

===Chinese===
「什么事？」

「哦，就是些鸡毛蒜皮的平常事，爸爸。」

「什么事？你总知道自己干了些什么吧？」

「我没做什么特别的。我们不过在楼上待了几个钟头，爸爸。」

「我不用你告诉我你在楼上待了多久。我倒想知道，你什么时候能做点事，哈里。你且想想，假如我这辈子什么也不曾做过。那你的日子就完全两样了，哪还能天天去赫里克先生那儿，舒舒服服地让人把钱花在你身上。你很快就会知道差别，很快就知道。」

===Original===
Next morning Mr. Bentley came slowly down his staircase. He was late for his train, his reason for further delay. He walked to his place with an air of uncertainty whether to remain.

“Ring the bell, Harry,” he said, replacing a cover on a dish, and leaning back to read a letter.

“Is it too cold for you, Father?” said Delia. “We kept on and on expecting you, and wondering whether to send it down to be kept hot. But I was afraid it would get too dry.”

“Your wondering whether to send it down to be kept hot, was not of much good, if you had made up your mind it would get too dry,” said Mr. Bentley. He paused once or twice in his speech, and did not lift his eyes.

“I did not think you would be so long, Father. You were so nearly ready when I passed your room.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，本特利先生缓步下了楼梯。他赶火车已经迟了，这又成了他再耽搁的理由。他走到自己的座位前，一副不知是否留下的神情。

「按铃，哈里，」他说，把菜盘的盖子重新盖上，往后一靠看起信来。

「菜凉了不合您意吧，爸爸？」迪莉娅说。「我们一直一直盼着你，盘算着要不要送下去保温。可我又怕它变得太干。」

「你既然认定它会太干，那盘算送下去保温也没有多大用处，」本特利先生说。他说话中间停了一两回，眼睛也不抬。

「我没想到你会这么久，爸爸。我经过你房间时，你都快收拾好了。」

===Original===
Mr. Bentley gave a casual glance at his daughter. He had done what remained with the utmost slowness, and a bitter sense of lookers-on.

“Take this down, and do me some fresh,” he said to the maid.

“Delia, can we go?” said Harry.

“Ask father if he will excuse you,” said Delia.

“Father, can we go? It is past school-time.”

===Chinese===
本特利先生漫不经心地瞥了女儿一眼。他把剩下的事做得慢到了极处，心里怀着旁观者众的苦涩。

「把这个端下去，给我做份新的，」他对女仆说。

「迪莉娅，我们能走了吗？」哈里说。

「问问父亲能不能准你们走，」迪莉娅说。

「爸爸，我们能走吗？已经过上学时间了。」

===Original===
“If it is past school-time, why did you not ask to go at the proper time? Why ask at all, if not at the right time? And using that ridiculous voice, as if there were some benefit to me in your sitting there! You are here to satisfy your own appetites. Go, of course. I do not want you.”

Mr. Bentley observed his sons as they left the room.

“I have never met a man so unfortunate in his children. Self-conscious, conceited, with the manners of clowns! Sitting there, thinking their society such a benefit, and then the first words they utter to do with themselves! Neither of them taking the trouble to say good morning to me, but speaking fast enough as soon as they wanted to get off to their own pursuits! It is unbelievable.”

“I do not think Harry is well. I think he has one of his headaches coming. I have thought so all breakfast time.”

“And why did you not speak of it, if you saw it? Why did you sit there and take no notice of a child you knew to be ill? Really, Delia, I should not have believed it. It is a hard thing, a hard thing, a hard thing. I may not have done much to lead my children as I would have them go, but my example should not have led them to this. It should not.”

===Chinese===
「既然过了上学时间，你为何不在该走的时候要求走？不在合适的时候，又何必开口？还用那种可笑的腔调，仿佛你们坐在那儿对我有什么好处似的！你们坐在这儿是为了满足你们自己的口腹。走吧，当然。我不需要你们。」

儿子们离开房间时，本特利先生目送着他们。

「我从没见过谁在儿女上这么不幸。怕难为情，自高自大，举止像个乡巴佬！坐在那儿，还以为自己做伴是多大恩惠，一张嘴头一句就离不开自己！两人谁都不肯费神跟我说声早上好，可一旦想溜去干自己的事，嘴皮子立刻利索起来！真难以置信。」

「我看哈里不舒服。他的老毛病头痛怕是又要犯了。一顿早饭我都在这么想。」

「你既然看出来了，为什么不说？为什么坐在那儿，对一个明知有病的孩子不闻不问？说真的，迪莉娅，我简直不敢相信。这是狠心，狠心，狠心啊。我或许没怎么领着我的孩子们往我愿他们走的路上去，可我的榜样总不该把他们领到这个地步。不该的。」

===Original===
Delia rose and left the room.

Mr. Bentley was about to follow her, when she reentered, forcing him to pause. He stood with his head and arms so rigid that they shook. Delia edged by him, and stood with her eyes on his face.

“Come, speak, Delia. Do not be mysterious. It is the worst breach of manners. What secret can there be?”

“Can I speak to you, Father?”

“Speak? Of course you can speak. Do get out of this way of making mysteries. Speak, and try to be natural and to the point.”

===Chinese===
迪莉娅起身出了屋。

本特利先生正要跟上她，她却回来了，迫得他停住。他站着，头和胳膊僵直得发颤。迪莉娅侧身挤过他，站在那里，眼睛望着他的脸。

「说吧，说话，迪莉娅。别神神秘秘的。那是最失礼的事。能有什么秘密？」

「我能跟您说话吗，爸爸？」

「说话？你当然能说话。快改掉这装神弄鬼的毛病。说吧，自然些，说正题。」

===Original===
“John and Harry have come home, Father. They want to speak to you, I think.”

“Want to speak to me! Want to speak! Of all the habits my family has ever had! Call them in, and let them speak, make them speak. I will have no more of it. It makes me too ashamed.”

“Let me pass, will you please, Father.”

“Oh, it is nothing that requires anyone to be put about for it. They have come back because they ‘want to speak,’ I believe. My children are fond of that plea.”

“Father, we must see if anything is the matter with them.”

===Chinese===
「约翰和哈里回家了，爸爸。我想他们有话要跟您说。」

「有话跟我说！有话说！我家里多少年多少毛病里数这个！叫他们进来，让他们说，逼着他们说。这毛病我不容了。太让我难堪了。」

「让我过去，劳驾了，爸爸。」

「哦，这算什么事，犯不着谁为它动弹。他们是『有话要说』才回来的，我相信。我的孩子们就爱用这个借口。」

「爸爸，我们总得看看他们是不是出了什么事。」

===Original===
“Harry! John! Come in at once, and tell your sister what you want with her.”

“Well, what is it that you have to say, that you have come home from school when the morning has hardly begun? Let us hear it, so that you can get back again. I do not like this easy breaking off for any reason that comes to hand.”

“I have a headache,” said Harry. “Mrs. Merry sent us home to tell Delia.”

Delia went out of the room with the boy.

“John, go after your sister, and come back at once, and tell me all about your brother, I must know if I am needed. I will put off my journey today.”

===Chinese===
「哈里！约翰！立刻进来，告诉你们姐姐找她做什么。」

「好了，你们有什么话，一清早就从学校跑回来？说来听听，好让你们赶紧回去。我不喜欢这样随手抓个理由就撂下正事。」

「我头痛，」哈里说。「梅里太太让我们回来告诉迪莉娅。」

迪莉娅带着孩子出了屋。

「约翰，跟你姐姐去，马上回来，把弟弟的情形原原本本告诉我，我得知道用不用得着我。今天的出行我推迟了。」

===Original===
“You will not be needed. Harry did not even want you to be told.”

“Oh, you idiotic, self-absorbed children! Have you not reason to grasp, that it is what is good for Harry that has to be thought of? Can you not bring yourselves to some real concern for your brother? Must you go on, thinking of nothing but how things can be settled for you to see the least trouble? Oh, that I could get you to see it! But I have given it up. As I say, I will give up my journey today. I will be ready to hear what is best to do, and ready to do it.”

John gave a caper.

“You see, John, it is not always an easy thing to bring people to see what is right, when one is at the head of a household where people are fond of going their own way, whether it is the right way or not. It cannot be done, my boy, without much of what must seem to people who do not understand﻿—and my family are people who do not understand, I am sorry to say﻿—to be needless, and even trying. But you will look back upon what your father did, when I am no longer with you, and see that it was not done easily.”

John looked at his father with rising tears.

===Chinese===
「用不着您。哈里连让您知道都不愿意。」

「哦，你们这些愚蠢、只顾自己的孩子们！你们就不能明白一点：该考虑的是对哈里好的事？你们就不能真心惦记一下你们的兄弟？你们就非得一门心思，只琢磨怎么把事情安排得让自己少沾一点麻烦？哦，真希望我能让你们开窍！可我已经放弃了。我说了，今天出行我推迟。怎么处置最好，我随时听着，也随时照办。」

约翰雀跃了一下。

「你瞧，约翰，当一家之主，而家里人无论对错都爱自行其是，要让人看清什么是对，并不总是容易的。要做到这一步，我的孩子，就免不了许多在不懂的人看来——我很遗憾，我家里人正是不懂的人——纯属多余、甚至惹人厌的事。可等我不在了，你回头想想你父亲做的这些，就会明白这一切来得并不轻松。」

约翰望着父亲，眼泪涌了上来。

===Original===
Mr. Bentley just laid a hand on his head, and went upstairs and stood by himself, repeating his speech with additions which had not occurred to him.

When he went down later, he saw that his daughter had dropped a spoon she had brought from the sick room. He watched her look with a resolve not to help the search, and sat down and opened the paper.

“Well, I have to go on under this fresh burden of anxiety. It is not an easy thing to do, for anyone who cannot sink at once into his own affairs. Ah, it is a sad thing in many ways to be wrought upon by things outside oneself.”

“I am sure we all feel about Harry. But he is not really ill. And it is hardly right to make a great trouble of a little one.”

“You are a self-satisfied young woman.”

===Chinese===
本特利先生只把一只手放在他头上，便上楼独自站着，把那番话重讲一遍，还添上了方才不曾想到的种种。

稍后他下楼，看见女儿把从病室带来的一把小勺失手掉了。他打定主意不帮她找，只看着她找，随后坐下，翻开了报纸。

「唉，我又得在这份新的忧虑重担下撑下去。对任何一个不能立刻沉进自己事务里的人来说，这都不容易。啊，为身外之事所牵动，在许多方面都是伤心的事。」

「我相信我们都惦记着哈里。可他并不是真病了。小事闹成大祸，总不大应该。」

「你是个自满的年轻女人。」

===Original===
“Self-satisfied? Oh, you do not know me, I think, Father. And I am sure I have no right to be self-satisfied. I am many things I should not be. I am quite conscious of that.”

“You are a wonderful young woman, to be conscious that you are not perfect.”

“I think we are all rather out of sorts, and strung up, so to speak, about it.”

“It is a great thing if Harry gets over this,” said Mr. Bentley. “It is hardly to be expected, I suppose, in such a case of neglect, though of course it is right to do everything as if we did expect it.”

There was silence.

===Chinese===
「自满？哦，我想你并不了解我，爸爸。我敢说自己也绝没有自满的资格。我有许多不该有的毛病。这一点我很清楚。」

「能意识到自己不完美，你可真是个了不起的年轻女人。」

「我想我们都有点心绪不宁，可以说，都为这事绷得太紧了。」

「哈里要是熬过了这一关，可是件大事，」本特利先生说。「我想，疏忽到这个地步，多半不能指望了，当然，我们做事仍要抱着指望的样子，这才是对的。」

一阵沉默。

===Original===
“Well, upon my word, you all make life very hard for a man. I do not know what I have done, that I should be subjected to this. Here I have done my best for all these years, gone on doing all I could do; never lost patience; never dwelt upon what I might have had. And now, because I try to keep a wise and firm hand over people for their own good, and to prevent them from sinking down, down, down, for their own sakes﻿—whose, if not for theirs, I should like to know?﻿—to be given as much to bear as if I were a tyrant and a monument of selfishness, instead of﻿ ﻿… ! Oh, it will not bear discussing. Why should I discuss it? Some people have much from others, and some have nothing and give all. It is just that, I suppose.”

“There is John come in again from school,” said Delia. “I will go out and tell him not to come in.”

Delia was capable of revenge.

“Oh, what does it matter whether he comes in or not?” said Mr. Bentley, his eyes dilating. “That is not the sort of thing to give our thought to now. It is not a time for thinking about nothing.”

Delia turned to the door.

===Chinese===
「唉，说实在的，你们大家一起把一个男人的日子弄得真苦。我不知道我做过什么，要受这样的对待。这些年我尽了全力，一味地做我能做的一切；从未失过耐心；从未念叨过我本可以过的日子。如今，只因为我为人们的切身好处着想，对他们握着一只明智而坚定的手，不让他们为了自己往下沉、沉、沉——不是为他们，还能为谁，我倒要问问？——担子却压得我喘不过气，倒好像我是个暴君、一尊自私的纪念碑，而不是……！哦，这话经不起讨论。我干吗要讨论它？有的人从别人那里得的多，有的人一无所得还倾其所有。大概就是这么回事。」

「约翰又从学校回来了，」迪莉娅说。「我出去叫他别进来。」

迪莉娅是会报复的。

「哦，他进不进来有什么要紧？」本特利先生说，眼睛睁大了。「现在不是操心这种事的时候。眼下可没工夫想这些无关紧要的。」

迪莉娅转向门口。

===Original===
“John,” called Mr. Bentley, “come into the room, and hear what there is to be heard about Harry. It is not right that you should not hear of your own brother. I will have no concealments and associations on false pretences in my family. Ah! That is what makes family breaches. That is what leads to them.”

“Did you call me, Father? I had gone upstairs. I was not quite sure.”

“You heard me call, I suppose?”

“Yes, Father. That is why I came down.”

“Then I imagine you were sure. Really, John, you talk as if you were not in your right mind. I suppose, after all, you are too stupid, and childish for your age to be told anything, even what it is not right that you should not know. Go away, then. Go away to your own concerns. I will not tell you, then.”

===Chinese===
「约翰，」本特利先生喊道，「进屋来，听听哈里的消息。连自己弟弟的事都听不着，这不像话。我家里不容隐瞒，不容假借名目的私下勾连。啊！家庭裂痕就是这么来的。就是这么来的。」

「您叫我了吗，爸爸？我刚上了楼。我不太确定。」

「我想你是听见我叫了吧？」

「是的，爸爸。所以我才下来。」

「那你我看你是确定的。说真的，约翰，你说起话来倒像神志不清。我算是明白了，你终究太蠢，又幼稚得超出年纪，什么事都不配告诉你，连那些不告诉你都不对的事也不配。那就走吧。找你自己的事去吧。那我不告诉你了。」

===Original===
At this point the maid brought in a note of concern from Emily.

“Do you see much of Mr. Herrick and Miss Herrick?” said Mr. Bentley, when he had read it.

“No, Father.”

“How much do you see of them? You know what I mean.”

“Not﻿—not much, not at all, Father.”

===Chinese===
这时女仆送进来一张艾米丽写的慰问短简。

「你常见到赫里克先生和赫里克小姐吗？」本特利先生读罢短简，问道。

「不常见，爸爸。」

「见到多少次？你懂我的意思。」

「不——不多，压根儿不，爸爸。」

===Original===
“Do you see anything of anyone, may I ask? Does anyone have anything to do with you? Teach you, for instance?”

“Mr. Merry, and Mr. Burgess, and Miss Basden; and Mrs. Merry, sometimes.”

“I think Miss Herrick is very charming,” said Delia.

“Oh, don’t be so obvious, Delia. Don’t be too obvious. We all know that. That is what made me ask about it. That is what it is, of course. Well, I shall have a very fair idea of what to say when we go to that prize-giving.”

As he returned from the prize-giving Mr. Bentley spoke.

===Chinese===
「恕我冒昧，你跟谁有来往吗？有谁搭理你吗？比如，教过你什么吗？」

「梅里先生，伯吉斯先生，还有巴斯登小姐；有时还有梅里太太。」

「我觉得赫里克小姐很有魅力，」迪莉娅说。

「哦，别这么露骨，迪莉娅。别太露骨。这个我们都知道。所以我才问起。当然，就是这么回事。好了，去参加那个发奖典礼时该说什么，我心里就相当有数了。」

从发奖典礼回来时，本特利先生开了口。

===Original===
“This wind makes exceedingly trying walking. We never seem to be without it now.”

“We do have it very often. I hope the boys will think to put on their coats to come home.”

“Oh, from that school? I expect that Merry will wrap them up, and watch them out of sight, to see they do not step in the puddles. That is the sort of thing they are accustomed to there, I think.”

Delia was silent.

“I wish I could find a school for them more like the one I was sent to as a boy.”

===Chinese===
「这风走起路来真够呛。如今我们好像一刻也躲不开它。」

「确实常刮。但愿孩子们回家时记得穿上外衣。」

「哦，从那个学校？我料定梅里会把他们裹得严严实实，再目送他们到看不见，盯着他们别踩进水坑。他们那儿的做派，我想就是这一套。」

迪莉娅沉默着。

「真想给他们找一所学校，像我小时候被送去的那么一所。」

===Original===
“I do not think Mr. Merry is as easygoing with them as he seems. I think that is just his little way.”

“I was not speaking of his being easygoing, or about his little way. I was thinking of his being such an unmitigated nincompoop. Such talk as his no sane man would credit, if he did not hear it with his ears. It is enough to ruin a boy to listen to it.”

“Mrs. Merry is a nice woman,” said Delia.

“What difference does it make, whether she is a nice woman or not? That does not teach the boys, or help them to earn their living. And I do not know if Miss Herrick is a nice woman. I should think not. But I do know that she does not do either.”

They entered the house in silence.

===Chinese===
「我看梅里先生并不像表面那么纵容他们。那只是他的小做派。」

「我说的不是他纵容不纵容，也不是他的小做派。我想的是他这么个不折不扣的蠢货。他那套话，一个神志健全的人若非亲耳听见，绝不会相信。孩子听多了，足够毁了。」

「梅里太太是位好女人，」迪莉娅说。

「她好不好又有什么分别？那又教不了孩子们，也帮不了他们谋生。再说赫里克小姐是不是好女人，我不知道。我看不是。可我确实知道，这两样她一样也不占。」

他们默默进了家门。

===Original===
“Whatever on earth are those boys doing? Palavering about, handing cups to people they have never seen! They are fonder of waiting on strangers than on their father.”

“They have to do it, Father. They do not enjoy it.”

“Do not enjoy it! It is the only sort of thing that John does enjoy. I wish I had sons more like myself.”

The boys came in.

“Oh, we have had the prize-giving! Harry has got his prize. We had to hand round the tea.”

===Chinese===
「那两个孩子到底在干什么？跟些素不相识的人东拉西扯，还递茶杯！伺候生人倒比伺候他们的父亲起劲。」

「他们不得不做，爸爸。他们并不乐意。」

「并不乐意！那是约翰唯一乐意做的事。真希望我的儿子们多像我一些。」

孩子们进来了。

「哦，我们参加了发奖典礼！哈里得了奖。我们还不得不四处递茶。」

===Original===
“You spent a very long time in handing round tea. I spoke to Mr. Merry about you both. He had not much to say for you.”

“And you seem to have a great many friends at that school, as far as I can judge. Giggling, and talking, even while the prizes were being given away! It does not look as if you had no friends to ask to your home, as always seems to be the case, when I ask you why you do not bring them here, and let us have an idea what kind of people you are with, when you are away from us all day at such an expense. There are very few of them who have been brought up as you have; I am sure of that. Anyone would think you would be proud to let them see your father and your sister and your surroundings. I can’t think what makes you so affected and self-conscious about it. Now, once for all, what is it?”

“Well, do not speak, then, do not speak. Go off to your own employment, and settle down by yourselves; and do not say a word to your father, who makes sacrifices all day, that you may have every advantage; and was trying to arrange something else, that you might have further pleasure. Go away then, and do not speak. Behave as my children always do. Go away, without a word, to your own concerns.”

===Chinese===
「你们递茶递了老半天。我跟梅里先生说了你们俩。他没替你们说上几句话。」

「照我看，你们在那个学校里的朋友可真不少。发奖的时候还咯咯笑、说个不停！可见你们并不像每次我问你们为什么不带朋友来家里时显得那样没朋友——我一问，你们总这样——好像我无从知道，你们整天离家、花那么多钱，到底是跟些什么人混在一起。他们中间教养像你们这样的，没有几个；这一点我敢肯定。谁都会以为，你们巴不得让他们看看你们的父亲、你们的姐姐和你们的家境。我真不明白你们在这上头这样扭捏、这样怕难为情是为了什么。好了，就这一回，说，到底为什么？」

「好，那就别说，别说吧。去干你们自己的事，自己安顿去；对你们的父亲一个字也不必说——他整天做出牺牲，好让你们样样占先；他还正想再安排点什么，好让你们更开心。那就走吧，什么也别说。照我的孩子们一贯的样子做。走吧，一声不吭地，找你们自己的事去。」

## VI / 六

===Original===
“I hope, Uncle Peter,” said Francis, “that Mr. Herrick and Mr. Bumpus will excuse me for not being present at their literary inauguration. I have very little time to myself, and I am obliged to deny myself all recreative reading, and being read to must be counted under that head, I think. So, if you will make my apologies to the authors, I will leave you before they come in. Though I admit it hardly seems fair to deny myself as audience, as I am safe in that way myself. We clergymen have an unfair advantage.”

“Yes, the poor laity!” said Miss Lydia.

“The arrangement was just for Peter and me to hear the books,” said Theresa.

“Yes, yes,” said Francis, in a kindly voice. “Then I may betake myself with a clear conscience to the solid pursuits which must be my portion, I fear.”

“I too must go to my solid pursuits,” said Miss Lydia. “But the dear men things! How interested they have been in it all!”

===Chinese===
「我希望，彼得伯伯，」弗朗西斯说，「赫里克先生和邦珀斯先生会原谅我不出席他们的文学揭幕礼。我自己支配的时间极少，不得不戒绝一切消遣性阅读，而听人朗读，我想也得归入那一类。所以，请您代我向两位作者致歉，我就在他们进来之前告辞了。不过我得承认，把自己排除在听众之外，实在有欠公平，因为我自己在这方面是稳当的。我们牧师占着一份不公平的便宜。」

「是啊，可怜的平信徒们！」莉迪亚小姐说。

「原先的安排，就只让彼得和我来听这两部书，」特蕾莎说。

「是啊，是啊，」弗朗西斯用和善的声音说。「那我就可以问心无愧地去做那些实实在在的功课了，那想必才是我的本分。」

「我也得去做我实实在在的功课，」莉迪亚小姐说。「可是亲爱的人们！他们对这一切多么上心啊！」

===Original===
“Well, listeners never hear good of themselves,” said Bumpus, coming in.

“But they don’t often hear anything as bad as that,” said Emily. “What are we to say? People don’t speak about their own kindness, and of course it is kindness in Dickie and Nicholas to be going to read their books to us.”

“Ah, yes, yes, it is all for us,” said Miss Lydia. “And we must not underrate the pleasure things.”

“Well, we shall not overrate them,” said Herrick.

“Could we overrate things of that kind?” said Emily. “Well, are you going boldly to begin, or am I to work at leading up to it? I couldn’t expect anyone else to do that. Peter admires you too much, and Lydia not nearly enough, and William and Theresa are above leading up to things.”

===Chinese===
「唉，听的人从来听不见自己的好话，」邦珀斯说着走了进来。

「可这么难听的话也不常听到，」艾米丽说。「我们该说什么呢？人们不谈自己的好意，而迪基和尼古拉斯肯把书读给我们听，这当然是好意。」

「啊，是啊，是啊，全是为了我们，」莉迪亚小姐说。「我们可不能低估了这些乐事。」

「唉，我们也不会高估它们，」赫里克说。

「这类事我们高估得了吗？」艾米丽说。「那么，是你们自己大大方方地开始，还是由我来做铺垫？这个我不能指望别人。彼得对你们佩服太过，莉迪亚又远远不够，威廉和特蕾莎则不屑于做铺垫。」

===Original===
“Well, well, we might begin,” said Herrick.

“Yes, yes, begin, Herrick,” said Bumpus.

“Yes, it is your business, dear,” said Emily. “Dickie is a relative, and has to be put last in everything. It would be presuming upon your intimacy with the house to hold back.”

“It is so nice to help in any way,” said Miss Lydia.

“I said that Lydia did not admire you nearly enough,” said Emily.

===Chinese===
「好吧，好吧，我们可以开始了，」赫里克说。

「是啊，是啊，开始吧，赫里克，」邦珀斯说。

「是啊，这是你的事，亲爱的，」艾米丽说。「迪基算亲戚，凡事都得排在末位。你再踌躇，就是仗着跟这家人亲近而失礼了。」

「能帮上点忙总是好的，」莉迪亚小姐说。

「我刚说了，莉迪亚对你们佩服得远远不够，」艾米丽说。

===Original===
“She really admires us terribly little,” said Bumpus.

“Well, she has killed any desire in me, but to do my simple best in anything I may undertake,” said Herrick, opening his papers.

“Yes, we must keep Lydia here. She will put the right spirit into it,” said Bumpus.

“Oh, no, no, I can’t be here. But it is so nice to be wanted, thank you, thank you. So nice to go off to what calls me most, feeling that I should be welcome at what calls me less, calls me too, though it does not need me. For it does call me. It does call me. But I must go to the need.”

“I am so proud of you for writing the book, Nicholas,” said Emily, “and especially for going to be known to have written it. And so remorseful for thinking you might not be great, when you always hinted. But the great always forgive.”

===Chinese===
「她佩服我们的分量确实少得可怕，」邦珀斯说。

「唉，她把我心里的欲望全杀光了，只除了在任何着手的事上尽我朴素的本分，」赫里克说着，打开了文稿。

「是啊，我们得把莉迪亚留下。她会给这事注入正当的精神，」邦珀斯说。

「哦，不，不，我不能待在这儿。可被人需要真好，谢谢你们，谢谢。去奔赴那最召唤我的，同时又知道那不那么召唤我的地方也欢迎我——它也召唤我，虽说它并不需要我——这真好。因为它是召唤我的。它召唤我。可我必须去那需要我的。」

「你写了这本书，我真骄傲，尼古拉斯，」艾米丽说，「尤其是就要让人知道它是你写的了。我还一直疑心你也许算不上伟大，你却时时点破，我真是悔极了。不过伟人总是宽恕的。」

===Original===
“Well, well,” said Herrick. “It came to me, you know, that night when we sat up in turns with old Crabbe. The whole thing came upon me, just through that little service to somebody else.”

“It was really too much reward,” said Emily. “But of course you deserved it.”

“Why, Bumpus,” said Herrick, looking at Bumpus’ papers over his shoulder; “your beginning sentence is the same as mine!”

“The same as yours?” said Bumpus. “Why, it can’t be. It can’t be, surely. Why, there wouldn’t be anything in common in our books.”

“Dickie, don’t say out what is in your mind openly to Nicholas,” said Emily.

===Chinese===
「唉，唉，」赫里克说。「它是降临到我身上的，你们知道，就在我们轮班守着老克拉布的那一夜。整件事就那样落在我身上，只因为替旁人尽了那么一点小劳。」

「这报偿实在过分了，」艾米丽说。「不过你当然受之无愧。」

「哎呀，邦珀斯，」赫里克说着，从他肩头看他的稿纸；「你的开篇句子跟我的一样！」

「跟你的一样？」邦珀斯说。「哎呀，不可能。肯定不可能。咱们两本书里不该有任何共通的东西呀。」

「迪基，别把心里想的当着尼古拉斯明说出来，」艾米丽说。

===Original===
“Why, what is there in an opening sentence?” said Herrick. “All opening sentences are much on a line, aren’t they? All books have got to begin.”

“Nicholas, you have had faith in yourself,” said Emily. “Do let us get it over. Anyhow, you have one sentence as good as Dickie. So let us have the beginning. That may be the only part we can bear.”

“Yes, get on, Herrick,” said Bumpus. “Your book is ready to the word, and mine isn’t. You are a greater than I. You have your own mind in hand.”

“I have sympathy with boasting,” said Emily. “I hope Nicholas has.”

“Yes, it is ready, Bumpus, it is ready,” said Herrick. “It may be the last and only thing, but something it is at last. You may all like to remember the day, when you heard it from my own lips.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，一个开篇句子算得了什么？」赫里克说。「所有开篇句子都大同小异，不是吗？书总都得开头。」

「尼古拉斯，你对自己是有信心的，」艾米丽说。「快让我们把它了结了吧。不管怎样，你有一个句子跟迪基的一样好。那就把开头念出来吧。那也许是我们唯一听得下去的部分。」

「是啊，念吧，赫里克，」邦珀斯说。「你的书一字不差全就绪了，我的还没有。你比我伟大。你把自己的头脑攥在手里。」

「我对自夸抱有同情，」艾米丽说。「我希望尼古拉斯也有。」

「是啊，是就绪了，邦珀斯，就绪了，」赫里克说。「它也许是我最后也是唯一的东西，但它终究是一件东西。你们大家不妨记住这一天，你们亲耳从我口中听见了它。」

===Original===
“But don’t spoil it for me by reminding me that I may be without a breadwinner,” said Emily; “especially as you don’t win any bread. Dickie is not doing things like that. I think I have proposed to you, Dickie. But we haven’t time to go on about it now.”

“Mine is not such a one as this one,” said Bumpus. “I have had some delay with it. I left it in old Crabbe’s room that night he died; and it was got rid of with the other things. Crabbe’s illness brought on your novel, Herrick, but it held back mine. But it is the better for the rewriting of it.”

“Old Crabbe’s room? That night he died?” said Herrick. “Did you leave your book in the room, did you say, Bumpus? Ah, yes, it was a sad night, that, for us. Good old Crabbe! I always wondered that he never wrote. I have often said it. Did you leave your book in the room, did you say, Bumpus?”

“He never wrote. Not a line,” said Bumpus. “He was of the different kind. Yes, I left my only copy of it in the room, the typed copy; I always tear up manuscript; and it was cleared away with the other things. And rewriting it has meant reworking at it. You must make the best of it. I find I don’t take up writing again so easily. My best went into that early thing. Well, I have told my tale. That is the better thing, there where it is. Not that that matters now.”

“Couldn’t you write the early one again?” said Emily, keeping her face turned from her brother. “You could make it come back to you. A book when it is written could hardly go. William and I are the only people Nicholas has no influence over. It makes me admire Nicholas, but not you.”

===Chinese===
「可别拿我可能没了养家人这件事败我的兴，」艾米丽说；「何况你根本挣不来面包。迪基就不来这一套。我想我求过婚了吧，迪基。不过眼下没工夫细说这个。」

「我那本可不像这一本，」邦珀斯说。「它被耽搁了一阵。克拉布去世的那晚，我把它落在了他屋里；它就跟其余东西一并清掉了。克拉布的病催生了你的小说，赫里克，却把我的耽搁了。不过经过重写，它倒更好了。」

「老克拉布的屋子？他去世的那晚？」赫里克说。「你把书落在屋里了，你是这么说的吗，邦珀斯？啊，是啊，那一夜对我们真是伤感。好心的老克拉布！我一直纳闷他怎么从不写作。这话我常说。你把书落在屋里了，你是这么说的吗，邦珀斯？」

「他从来没写过。一行也没有，」邦珀斯说。「他是另一类人。是啊，我把它唯一的一份稿子落在了那屋里，打字的那份；手稿我一向都撕掉；它就跟其余东西一起清掉了。重写它，也就等于重新下功夫。你们只能往好里想了。我发现自己重新拾起写作不那么容易了。我的最好都进了早年的那部东西。好了，我把我的事说完了。那东西待在它待的地方，这样更好。虽说如今这已无所谓了。」

「你就不能把早年那部再写出来？」艾米丽说，脸一直背着兄长。「你能让它回到你笔下。一本书既已写成，是不会溜掉的。尼古拉斯影响不了的人，只有威廉和我两个。这让我佩服尼古拉斯，可不是佩服你。」

===Original===
“Well, does one do that thing?” said Bumpus. “Having done the other. Besides, that sort of thing comes and goes, and then exists of itself, if it has got on paper. But I confess I should like to be able to bring it out, and have the credit of it.”

“Exists of itself when it has got on paper!” said Emily. “It was as good as that, was it? Then I wish you could have the credit of it.”

“Perhaps somebody could really begin to read it,” said Mr. Fletcher.

“Perhaps it could read itself,” said Theresa.

“Emily, Emily!” said Herrick. “Please forgive this sister of mine, Bumpus. She quite puts me off reading my own book today. She does indeed. We will just have yours. Anyhow, we will have yours first.
